# SHARDFALL — research dossier

Seven parallel research passes, each asked for numbers and primary sources rather than
advice. Where a lens marks something as its own proposal rather than a citation, that is
preserved below — the distinction matters when these become balance tables.

Read `PLAN.md` for what we actually decided to do with this. This file is the evidence.

## Art direction and visual identity

### Summary

Researched art direction for a canvas-2D, no-asset-files game and produced a validated visual spec for Shardfall. Primary sources found: Thomas Vasseur's own Dead Cells art deep-dive (30fps animations, ~50px in-game character height, 3DS Max → custom no-antialias low-res renderer → PNG sequences + normal maps + toon shader, keyframes-first with interpolation only outside keys, "spared me hundred of hours"), Motion Twin's 80.lv interview (crits = 1-frame freeze + slowdown, techniques lifted from Street Fighter IV / BlazBlue / Mark of the Wolves), Slynyrd's palette methodology (8 ramps x 9 swatches, 20 degrees hue shift per swatch, 45 degrees between ramps, saturation peaks mid-ramp), full hex lists for DB16 / AAP-64 / EDG32, Lospec's outline rules, Downwell's 4-colour scheme, and WCAG SC 1.4.11's 3:1 non-text contrast threshold as an objective readability gate. I then built a 63-colour Shardfall palette in code and measured every actor-vs-terrain pair with the real WCAG luminance formula, retuning twice: the first attempt failed badly (rust enemy at 1.14:1 against lit terrain), the shipped version clears 3.92:1 minimum against terrain base and 2.61:1 against the brightest terrain highlight pixel. Frame budget is costed at 257 cached bitmaps / ~0.44 MB RGBA for all 19 enemies plus the player. Script that generates and verifies the palette: /tmp/claude-1000/-workspaces-Sweet-Spot/00ff769b-1474-43f1-895c-6b76d1865b29/scratchpad/pal.js

### Findings (17)

#### Dead Cells pipeline — what it actually was

**Numbers.** Character in-game height 50 px. Animations 30 fps. Team 1 artist for ~1 year, then 2. Export .fbx. Confidence: HIGH — this is the artist's own byline article.

Thomas Vasseur (sole artist for the first year, later joined by Gwenael Massé) built: (1) a flat 2D pixel-art model sheet as reference, (2) a rigged 3D character in 3DS Max, (3) export to Filmbox/.fbx, (4) a homebrew renderer that renders the 3D animation at very small size WITHOUT antialiasing — which is what produces the pixel look, (5) output as PNG frame sequences plus matching normal maps, (6) a basic toon shader lights the normal maps at runtime so sprites relight per level without redrawing. Backgrounds/decor were hand-painted in Photoshop and then a normal map was drawn for them too. The stated reason for going low-res-3D rather than hand-drawing: 'the ingame height of the character will only be 50 pixels', so high-detail 3D modelling was pointless. Vasseur explicitly says reusing skeleton/model parts across monsters 'spar[ed] me hundred of hours of work'.

**Apply to Shardfall.** You cannot ship a 3D pipeline, but you can ship the *idea that made it cheap*: one rigged skeleton, many skins. Define ONE parametric humanoid draw function (torso/head/2 arms/2 legs as a chunky quad chain) plus ONE parametric quadruped and ONE parametric blob/floater. All 13 grunts + 5 minibosses become (rig, proportions, palette ramp, top-shape appendage, scale) tuples — that is Vasseur's 'reuse the skeleton' trick expressed as code. Also steal the normal-map idea cheaply: store a per-shape face normal (up / left / right / down) at draw time and pick ramp index from dot(normal, lightDir) instead of hardcoding colours — this gives you free relighting per biome from one draw function.

*Source: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/production/art-design-deep-dive-using-a-3d-pipeline-for-2d-animation-in-i-dead-cells-i- and https://80.lv/articles/interview-with-the-developers-of-dead-cells*

#### Dead Cells animation philosophy — keyframes first, interpolation only outside keys

**Numbers.** 30 fps animation clock. Confidence HIGH for 30fps and pose-to-pose. NOTE: I could NOT find a primary source for per-animation frame counts in Dead Cells — one SEO blog claims '8-12 frame attack animations' but it is AI-generated content and I would not cite it. Treat any specific Dead Cells frame count as unsourced.

Dead Cells animations are pose-to-pose, matching traditional 2D animation principles: nail the read with as few keyframes as possible, and only then add interpolated frames 'before or after the key frames. Never in-between'. Vasseur also states they compensated for animation limitations with VFX rather than more frames — 'Exporting the whole as a sequence of frames also allows us to slip in a blend mode or two for an added wow effect', and impact effects/movement flourishes carry the combat feel. Retakes were near-free: adjusting an animation took 'less time than uploading these GIFs'.

**Apply to Shardfall.** This is the single most important cost-saving finding for you: spend frames on keyposes and spend everything else on VFX. A 2-keypose attack with a good arc streak, a 3-frame hitstop and a white flash reads better than an 8-frame attack with no VFX. Budget accordingly — my proposed frame table below is deliberately keypose-heavy and thin everywhere else.

*Source: https://www.gamedeveloper.com/production/art-design-deep-dive-using-a-3d-pipeline-for-2d-animation-in-i-dead-cells-i-*

#### Dead Cells impact feel — sourced numbers

**Numbers.** Crit freeze = 1 frame (at 60fps ≈ 16.7 ms). Confidence MEDIUM-HIGH — stated in a developer interview, not a technical doc. The widely-repeated '40-80 ms hitstop' / '3-5 frame freeze' figures circulate on game-feel blogs with no primary source; treat as folk wisdom, not data.

Motion Twin state they borrowed impact technique directly from fighting games — Street Fighter IV, BlazBlue and Garou: Mark of the Wolves are named. Critical hits specifically use a 1-frame freeze, plus slowdown, particle effects and an impact sound. This is a four-channel impact stack (time, motion, particles, audio) fired from one event.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Wire one impactEvent(x, y, dmg, isCrit, elem) that fires all four channels. Proposed (my numbers, not sourced): normal hit = 2 frames freeze (33 ms) + 3-frame white flash + 4 particles + shake 3; crit = 5 frames freeze (83 ms) + 5-frame flash + 10 particles + shake 8. Your existing `shake` variable already exists (seen at index.html line 1759, `shake=Math.max(shake,16)` for the slam pattern) so the channel is already there — 16 is a good boss-slam value, so scale normal hits well below it.

*Source: https://80.lv/articles/interview-with-the-developers-of-dead-cells*

#### Silhouette-first design — the actual tests used

**Numbers.** Level Design Book states enemy type must be identifiable at ~10+ metres. No source gives pixel-level thresholds; the black-fill test is qualitative. Confidence MEDIUM (widely-agreed craft practice, not a controlled study).

The industry-standard procedure is the squint test / black-fill test: fill the character 100% black and squint (or scale down); you should still identify who it is and what weapon or ability it has. Enemy-design guidance states each enemy must have a unique silhouette so the player can discern enemy TYPE at mid-range. Supporting rules that recur across sources: build from primitive geometry (circle/square/triangle) before any detail; exaggerate one feature rather than many; use negative space deliberately to prevent shapes colliding; silhouette must survive motion, not just the idle pose. Overwatch's lead artist Arnold Tsang notes body size and running pose were themselves treated as identification cues.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Convert the qualitative test into a mechanical rule you can actually check in code. Proposal (mine): every entity family gets a mandatory unique TOP-SHAPE occupying the top 2 rows of its sprite box, and a unique GROUND-CONTACT count. Top shapes: Player = single 2px tuft offset toward facing; Beast = two 2px horns with a 2px gap; Construct = flat top overhanging the body by 1px each side; Swarm = domed, no top feature; Wraith = single 1px tapered spike. Ground contacts: Player 2 legs, Beast 4, Construct 2 wide blocks, Swarm 4 thin, Wraith 0 (floats, with a gap between sprite and floor). Then build a debug key that renders every entity as a pure black silhouette on white at 1x and 0.5x in a grid — if you cannot name them all in one glance, the design fails. That debug view is ~20 lines and is the highest-value art tool you can build.

*Source: https://book.leveldesignbook.com/process/combat/enemy and https://bigredillustration.com/articles/importance-of-silhouette-in-character-design/*

#### Shape language — the mapping, and how to use it as a system

**Numbers.** No numbers; this is categorical. Confidence HIGH as consensus craft practice.

Consistent across all sources: circles/round forms = friendly, soft, safe, approachable; squares = stable, strong, reliable, grounded, physically powerful; triangles/angular = dangerous, aggressive, fast, unpredictable, villainous. The operative rule is that shape language is a FOUNDATION layer — the character must communicate its role through primary forms before any texture or detail is added, because in gameplay players have no time to study detail.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Bind shape to BEHAVIOUR, not to flavour, so the silhouette becomes a mechanical tell. Proposal (mine): triangle-dominant = fast/charging (closes distance, telegraphs then rushes); square-dominant = tanky/slow (high armor, blocks, must be flanked or out-ranged); circle-dominant = ranged/floating/erratic (arcs, orbits, projectiles). With 19 enemies you have room for exactly 3 body-plans x 3 shape classes x 2 scale tiers = 18, plus 1 unique. Once a player learns 'triangle = it will charge me' they read a new enemy correctly on first sight, which is what makes a roguelite's enemy roster feel designed rather than random.

*Source: https://ejaw.net/shape-language-in-character-design/ and https://rocketbrush.com/blog/shape-language-in-game-character-design-how-to-make-characters-readable-and-consistent*

#### Downwell — proof that 4 colours is enough

**Numbers.** 4 colours. Confidence HIGH (Wikipedia, well-documented game). Note: I could not find a Fumoto interview stating the rationale explicitly — the rationale above is my reading of the documented scheme.

Downwell (Ojiro Fumoto) runs a four-colour palette: black background, white outlines, red as the highlight for gems, enemies and other important items, blue for water/environment features. Palettes are unlockable and swap the exact hues but not the structure. The structure is the design: black = nothing, white = geometry you stand on, red = anything that matters (both threat and reward), blue = environmental modifier.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall is currently flat fillRect quads, which means you are ALREADY at Downwell's starting point and just lack the discipline layer. Adopt Downwell's semantic assignment on top of your richer palette: reserve maximum-value colours (luminance > 0.5) exclusively for things the player must react to — actors, projectiles, pickups, shard glow. Terrain must never exceed luminance 0.08 in ANY pixel including its highlight edge. That single rule does more for readability than any amount of sprite detail, and it is one clamp in your palette table.

*Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downwell_(video_game)*

#### Palette construction method — the only source with real numbers

**Numbers.** 8 ramps x 9 swatches = 128. +20 deg hue shift/swatch. 45 deg between ramps. Ramp length 3-5 typical. 10-15 deg minimum perceptible shift. Confidence HIGH for Slynyrd (author's documented method), MEDIUM for the 3-5 / 10-15 figures (aggregated tutorials).

Slynyrd's Pixelblog-1 documents an actual construction method with figures: the 'Mondo' general-purpose palette is 128 colours = 8 ramps x 9 swatches. Hue shifts +20 degrees per swatch as you move up a ramp; ramps are spaced 45 degrees apart to cover the 360-degree wheel. Saturation is non-uniform — it PEAKS in the middle swatch and darker colours generally carry more saturation than lighter ones; saturation takes larger steps at the extremes and smaller steps where it is highest. Brightness rises left to right with smaller increments as it approaches 100%, and typically does not start at 0. Explicit warning: avoid simultaneously high saturation AND high brightness. Corroborating tutorials give the practical ramp length: 3 colours (shadow/base/highlight) is the standard minimum and is sufficient for 16x16 and 32x32 sprites; 4-5 steps for larger sprites; most pixel art uses 3-5 per ramp. A subtle 10-15 degree hue shift per step is enough to read as three-dimensional without adding colours.

**Apply to Shardfall.** I used exactly this method to generate the Shardfall palette below: 20 deg cooling per step downward, 12 deg warming per step upward, saturation peaking mid-ramp, brightness eased so highlights bunch. 5 steps for terrain (you have 16px tiles that need shadow/base/lit/edge + a black), 4 steps for actors (your sprites are 12-34px wide — 4 is the documented sweet spot and each extra step costs you a fillStyle switch per entity per frame).

*Source: https://www.slynyrd.com/blog/2018/1/10/pixelblog-1-color-palettes*

#### Reference palettes — full hex and what makes each work

**Numbers.** DB16 = 16 colours, >10k Lospec downloads. AAP-64 = 64 colours, ~80,776 downloads. EDG32 = 32 colours. Confidence HIGH on hex values (read directly from Lospec). NOTE: neither Lospec page publishes the author's ramp grouping — the ramp decomposition above is my reading of the ordered hex list, not a cited claim.

DB16 (DawnBringer, 16 colours, Aseprite default preset): #140c1c #442434 #30346d #4e4a4e #854c30 #346524 #d04648 #757161 #597dce #d27d2c #8595a1 #6daa2c #d2aa99 #6dc2ca #dad45e #deeed6. It has the same count as EGA but reads far better because the colours are hand-picked for even VALUE spacing rather than even hue spacing — the ramp #140c1c > #4e4a4e > #757161 > #8595a1 > #deeed6 is a usable 5-step neutral ramp on its own, and every hue has a dark partner. AAP-64 (Adigun A. Polack, 2017): 64 colours built so ramps transition as smoothly as possible especially in fade-ins/fade-outs; it is structured as ~11 identifiable ramps of 4-6 steps (e.g. the warm ramp #221c1a #322b28 #71413b #bb7547 #dba463 #f4d29c, and the cool neutral #333941 #4a5462 #6d758d #8b93af #b3b9d1 #dae0ea). EDG32 (ENDESGA, made for NYKRA): #be4a2f #d77643 #ead4aa #e4a672 #b86f50 #733e39 #3e2731 #a22633 #e43b44 #f77622 #feae34 #fee761 #63c74d #3e8948 #265c42 #193c3e #124e89 #0099db #2ce8f5 #ffffff #c0cbdc #8b9bb4 #5a6988 #3a4466 #262b44 #181425 #ff0044 #68386c #b55088 #f6757a #e8b796 #c28569 — it is explicitly ordered as ramps with hue shifting baked in, which is why it is the standard recommendation for beginners: the value and hue relationships are pre-solved.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Do NOT adopt any of these wholesale. All three are general-purpose and bright-biased; Shardfall is a dark layered underground game and needs its terrain crushed into the bottom 8% of the luminance range, which no general palette does. Use EDG32's *structure* (ordered ramps, hue-shifted) and DB16's *discipline* (every hue has a dark partner) but generate your own values against your own contrast constraint — which is what the script does.

*Source: https://lospec.com/palette-list/dawnbringer-16 , https://lospec.com/palette-list/aap-64 , https://lospec.com/palette-list/endesga-32*

#### An objective readability threshold you can actually assert in a test

**Numbers.** 3:1, Level AA. Luminance coefficients 0.2126 / 0.7152 / 0.0722, linearisation threshold 0.03928, gamma ((v+0.055)/1.055)^2.4. Confidence HIGH — W3C normative.

WCAG 2.1 Success Criterion 1.4.11 'Non-text Contrast' (Level AA) requires graphical objects required to understand content to have a contrast ratio of at least 3:1 against adjacent colours. Contrast ratio = (L1 + 0.05) / (L2 + 0.05) where L is relative luminance = 0.2126*R + 0.7152*G + 0.0722*B on gamma-linearised sRGB. The standard explicitly says do not round when comparing (2.999:1 fails).

**Apply to Shardfall.** This is the finding that turns 'make enemies readable' into a unit test. Add a test to your existing test/ suite that loops every actor body colour against every terrain base colour and asserts CR >= 3.0. It is ~15 lines, it runs headless, and it makes an entire class of art regression impossible. Note the green channel dominates luminance (0.7152) — this is why your bile-green enemies are automatically the most readable and your rust/violet enemies are the hardest, and why colour choice cannot be made by eye.

*Source: https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG21/Understanding/non-text-contrast.html*

#### PROPOSED SHARDFALL PALETTE — 63 colours, generated and contrast-verified

**Numbers.** 63 colours total (30 terrain + 20 actor + 3 ground + 10 FX). MEASURED plane luminances: BG_FAR 0.0022, BG_NEAR 0.0046, terrain median 0.0288, actor median 0.3536, FX white 1.0. Plane separation ratios: terrain/bg-near = 6.30x, actors/terrain = 12.27x. MEASURED actor-vs-terrain contrast, worst case per actor against all 6 terrain BASE colours: player 7.40, bile 6.03, tin 4.79, rust 3.99, orchid 3.92 — ALL pass 3:1. Against the brightest terrain EDGE pixel: player 4.62, bile 4.01, tin 3.20, rust 2.66, orchid 2.61. Outline #05050a vs actor bodies: 6.39-11.29. Outline vs terrain: 1.33-1.63 (deliberately low — the outline is for actors, not tiles). My own proposal, verified numerically, not from a source. IMPORTANT: my first attempt at this palette FAILED — rust hit 1.14:1 against lit terrain — which is exactly what happens if you pick these by eye.

TERRAIN, 5 steps each dark->light (shadow, base, base-lit, lit, edge). T_SURFACE_LOAM #1a1312 #2e211b #3d2c1d #4a3d2a #574d39 | T_STONE_HOLLOW #131617 #21262b #282e39 #363b46 #464852 | T_FUNGAL_DEEP #111712 #1c2b21 #20392d #2d463c #3c524b | T_EMBER_VEIN #1a1112 #2d1919 #3c2019 #483126 #544236 | T_FROST_SHELF #151a19 #222c2d #27363c #364248 #474e54 | T_VOID_CORE #111014 #211b27 #2d2035 #3b2d41 #493c4d. ACTORS, 4 steps (shadow, body-shadow, body, rim): A_PLAYER_BONE #5c4c40 #9e8a68 #cdc195 #f7f5df | A_BEAST_RUST #572321 #994a32 #c88154 #f2c9a0 | A_CONSTRUCT_TIN #3d5357 #5d8397 #83a4c4 #c7d6ed | A_SWARM_BILE #525727 #799733 #8dc44c #b7ed8e | A_WRAITH_ORCHID #5a335c #9c4f90 #c974af #f2b8da. GROUND: BG_FAR #07070c, BG_NEAR #0d0e16, OUTLINE #05050a. FX (8 hues, hard cap): white #ffffff, flash #fff2cf, crit #ffd541, fire #ff6a1e, cold #5fd8ff, shock #c9a0ff, poison #a6e22e, bleed #ff2e4d, shard #3fe0d0, gold #d8a53f. Generated with 20 deg cool-shift down / 12 deg warm-shift up, saturation peaking mid-ramp, eased brightness — i.e. the Slynyrd method.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Drop this in as a PALETTE const next to CONFIG. Your TILES table currently stores a single flat colour `T.c` per tile (index.html ~line 249, used at line 1039) — change it to store a ramp index and derive the 5 steps, so biome retinting is one number. Your existing chunk renderer already darkens the bottom 2px of each tile with #00000030 (line 1042) and applies a hash-based #00000018 to 30% of tiles (line 1041) — that is already an implicit top-light plus value noise, so formalising the ramp costs you almost no new code, it just replaces alpha-black overlays with real ramp steps (which is strictly better: alpha-black desaturates, ramp steps hue-shift).

*Source: generated + verified by /tmp/claude-1000/-workspaces-Sweet-Spot/00ff769b-1474-43f1-895c-6b76d1865b29/scratchpad/pal.js*

#### Outline rules — the one decision that most determines whether it looks intentional

**Numbers.** 1px outline width is the pixel-art convention at these sprite scales. Confidence HIGH for the rules, MEDIUM for '1px' (convention, not a cited figure).

Lospec's outline tutorials give the operative rules: an outline should ALWAYS be darker than both the object and the background behind it. Two valid strategies: (a) full dark outline all the way around — maximum readability, slightly heavy, comic/UI flavour, explicitly recommended for 'quick and reactive video games' where sprites must pop off the background; (b) selective outlining ('sel-out') — wrap the whole silhouette in a hard dark line to lock the shape, then on the LIT side (conventionally top/left) replace the outline with a lighter body colour, keeping the dark line where the sprite meets the background on the unlit side (bottom/right). Sel-out is described as the professional default.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Use full dark outline (#05050a, 1px) on ALL actors, projectiles and pickups, and NO outline on terrain. Reason: Shardfall is a destructible-terrain game with a Noita-like busy foreground, and rule (a) is explicitly the one for reactive games. Terrain going un-outlined is what creates the plane separation — outlined things are things that can kill you or help you, un-outlined things are scenery. This is a semantic outline rule, and it is stronger than a stylistic one because the player learns it without being told. Then add sel-out as a second pass ONLY on the player and the 5 minibosses: replace the top and left outline pixels with ramp step 3 (the rim colour). That gives your hero and your bosses a visual tier above the grunts for free.

*Source: https://lospec.com/articles/pixel-art-outlines/ and https://lospec.com/articles/pixel-art-outlines-part-2-using-color/*

#### Cheap canvas-2D readability techniques — implementations and what each buys

**Numbers.** Bayer 4x4 values as listed. Flash 3 frames = 50 ms at 60fps. Shadow alpha 0.35 max, fades to 0 at 48px air height. Squash/stretch 0.78-1.22 range. These are my proposals; only the canvas tinting caveats are sourced.

(1) OUTLINE — bake it into the cached sprite: draw the silhouette in #05050a at offsets (0,-1)(0,1)(-1,0)(1,0) then the sprite on top. 4 extra drawImage at BAKE time, zero at runtime. Buys: guaranteed figure/ground separation regardless of what tile is behind. (2) RIM LIGHT — 1px of ramp[3] along the top and left edges of every shape, baked. Zero runtime cost. Buys: implies a light source and a 3D form from a flat quad; this is the single biggest 'not programmer art' upgrade per byte. (3) DROP SHADOW — one ellipse/rect under each entity, alpha = 0.35 * clamp(1 - airHeight/48), width = hitboxW * (1 - airHeight/96). One draw call per entity. Buys: grounds the character and communicates jump height, which a side-view flight game desperately needs. (4) FLASH ON HIT — pre-bake a second white-silhouette bitmap per pose; on hit, swap for 3 frames. 2x cache memory, zero runtime cost, and it sidesteps the known canvas tinting problem (the offscreen + globalCompositeOperation='source-atop' trick fails for pure white, and non-source-over composite ops are documented as significantly slower in some browsers). (5) SQUASH & STRETCH — ctx transform anchored at feet, volume-preserving so sx*sy ~= 1: takeoff sy 1.18 / sx 0.86 held 4 frames; landing sy 0.78 / sx 1.22 easing back over 8 frames. One setTransform per entity. Buys: weight, and it makes a rectangle feel alive. (6) COLOUR TEMPERATURE SEPARATION — terrain hues confined to 150-290 deg (cool) except the ember biome; actors carry warm-shifted ramps; enemies never share a hue family with the biome they spawn in. Zero cost, it is a palette constraint. (7) DITHERING FOR DEPTH — 4x4 Bayer matrix (0 8 2 10 / 12 4 14 6 / 3 11 1 9 / 15 7 13 5, divided by 16) baked once into 3 offscreen 4x4 canvases at 25/50/75 density, used via createPattern and a single fillRect per depth band. Buys: a gradient between BG_FAR and BG_NEAR without a gradient, which keeps the retro read and costs one fill.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Ordering by value-per-effort for your codebase: rim light and baked outline first (they change everything and cost bake time only), then drop shadow (you have flight — shadows are how the player judges altitude), then flash-on-hit, then squash/stretch, then dither, then temperature. Do NOT do runtime globalCompositeOperation tinting for the hit flash given the documented Firefox slowness and the white-tint failure mode — bake the white silhouette.

*Source: https://github.com/mattdesl/bento/wiki/Sprite-Tinting , https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=762973 , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Canvas_API/Tutorial/Optimizing_canvas*

#### What 'iconic' means mechanically

**Numbers.** No numbers available. Confidence MEDIUM — the Hollow Knight movement/readability quotes are from developer interviews; the Mignola influence for Hades is documented; my extraction of the common rule is my own synthesis.

Across the three games named: Hollow Knight's Knight is almost entirely BLACK with shades and hues used only to pick out features — the character is a silhouette by construction, and Team Cherry paired it with deliberately straight terrain and Mega-Man-style zero-acceleration movement specifically because 'the terrain helped make that quick movement super readable' and it aids 'being able to judge distances, and being able to judge behaviors'. Hades chose a pen-and-ink line style (carried over from Pyre) explicitly modelled on Mike Mignola — clear hard line, chiaroscuro, strong colour contrast, angular characters — and Supergiant describe themselves as 'first and foremost a game design-led team' with art in service of that. Dead Cells' recognisability comes from the flaming-green-head-on-a-dark-body read, which survives at 50px. The common mechanical rule: ONE high-contrast focal element on an otherwise low-contrast body, plus a unique outer contour. Not detail — contrast placement.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Give every entity exactly ONE focal element, and make it the thing the player must watch. Proposal (mine): the focal is always the entity's 'shard' — a 2x2 or 3x3 block in an FX-tier colour placed at the attack origin, at luminance > 0.6 while the body sits at 0.15-0.45. Player's shard is FX_SHARD #3fe0d0; each enemy family's shard is its element colour; an elite's shard is 4x4 and pulses. Because the focal marks the attack origin, learning to look at the bright dot IS learning to read the attack — the icon and the tell are the same pixel. That is what Hollow Knight's white mask does and it costs you one fillRect.

*Source: https://sourcegaming.info/2025/04/09/straight-from-the-source-team-cherry/ and https://www.pointnthink.fr/en/the-art-of-hades-en/ and https://mcvuk.com/business-news/behind-the-art-of-hades-we-value-artistic-integrity-and-excellence-in-artistic-craft-at-supergiant-however-were-first-and-foremost-a-game-design-lead-team/*

#### Games that shipped a code-drawn / primitive-shape look successfully

**Numbers.** Thomas Was Alone: entire cast = rectangles; BAFTA for the narration performance. Downwell: 4 colours. Confidence HIGH on the facts, MEDIUM on my extracted pattern.

Thomas Was Alone (Mike Bithell) is the strongest precedent for Shardfall's exact starting point: the entire cast is coloured rectangles. Bithell: 'The way the mechanics were designed, it kind of felt like the characters needed to be rectangular, and I decided to go all out for this minimalist look. I was afraid of that feeling sterile, which is where the voiceover comes in.' Identity was carried by (a) distinct proportions per character, (b) distinct colour, (c) distinct ABILITY — each personality was built on top of its mechanical role — and (d) narration. It won a BAFTA. Downwell shipped on 4 colours. Noita ships a distinct look from a per-pixel simulation plus strong dynamic lighting — 'each area feels dark, dangerous and alive' — i.e. the light and the motion are the art. The pattern: minimal forms succeed when a non-art channel (motion, light, audio, narration, mechanics) carries the identity that detail normally would.

**Apply to Shardfall.** You already have the two channels these games used: motion (flight, destructible terrain, gem-modified attack shapes) and light (you are underground). Bithell's lesson is the direct one — a rectangle is fine IF proportion + colour + ability are locked together. That means your 19 enemies must differ in proportion FIRST, colour second, and each proportion must map to its behaviour. Your current spread of 10-34px widths is a proportion axis you are not yet using semantically.

*Source: https://www.pocketgamer.biz/a-tale-of-friendship-the-making-of-thomas-was-alone/ and https://whatculture.com/gaming/thomas-was-alone-developer-interview-exclusive and https://www.gamedeveloper.com/game-platforms/road-to-the-igf-nolla-games-i-noita-i-*

#### Layered depth / parallax — the one hard number available

**Numbers.** Integer-divisor scroll rates. 10-20 / 20-40 / 40-60 percent for sky/far/mid. Confidence HIGH for the divisor rule (author's stated method), LOW for the percentages (aggregated blog content, no primary source).

Slynyrd's parallax article gives an integer-rate rule: start the furthest layer at 1 pixel per frame and step each nearer layer to the next faster PERFECT divisor of the canvas width, so the loop is seamless. For a 96px canvas the legal rates are 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48 — the gaps between increments follow a rising curve, which produces natural speed separation between layers for free. Broader guidance (weaker sourcing): sky layer at 10-20% of camera speed, far layer 20-40% with low saturation and low detail, mid layer 40-60%. Atmospheric perspective is universally recommended (distant = desaturated, lower contrast) but NO source gives numeric desaturation/darkening per layer.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Your camera shows ~17 tiles = 272 logical px wide. Divisors of 272 are 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 17, 34, 68, 136 — so use scroll rates 1/8, 1/4, 1/2 of camera for three background layers and they will tile seamlessly. Since no source gives per-layer desaturation numbers, here is my proposal derived from the measured plane luminances above: BG_FAR layer at luminance ~0.002 (#07070c), BG_NEAR at ~0.005 (#0d0e16), terrain at ~0.029. That is a 2.1x step to the near background and a 6.3x step to terrain — a deliberately non-linear ramp, so the two background layers read as 'the same darkness at different depths' while terrain snaps forward. Cave-interior backgrounds should be the SAME hue as the terrain ramp but two steps darker, never a different hue, or the eye reads them as objects.

*Source: https://www.slynyrd.com/blog/2019/11/12/pixelblog-23-parallax-scrolling*

#### PROPOSED VISUAL SPEC — grid, sizes, light, animation budget

**Numbers.** MEASURED CACHE COST: 1 player x 34 frames at 26x36 = 126 KB; 13 grunts x 11 frames at 14x20 = 143 bitmaps, 153 KB; 5 minibosses x 16 frames at 20x28 = 80 bitmaps, 175 KB. TOTAL 257 cached bitmaps ~= 0.44 MB RGBA. Doubling for the white flash-silhouette variant = ~0.88 MB. Bake cost at ~40 fillRect per frame = ~10,300 fillRect calls once at load, i.e. a few ms. Every number in this spec is my proposal except the 30 fps (Dead Cells, sourced) and the 3:1 contrast gate (WCAG, sourced).

PIXEL GRID: render at 1 logical px, but snap every drawn form to a 2px module (outlines and the focal shard are the only 1px-legal elements). A 2px module on your 16px tile gives 8 chunk-cells per tile and turns a 20px-tall player into a 10-cell-tall figure — the NES character-sprite range that is empirically readable. Canvas must use imageSmoothingEnabled=false and an integer devicePixelRatio scale. LIGHT: single fixed key from top-left, unit vector (-0.707, -0.707). Every quad: top row and left column = ramp[+1], bottom row and right column = ramp[-1], plus a 2px ambient-occlusion band beneath any solid surface. The shard glow is an ADDITIVE secondary light only — it may add, never subtract, so it can never break the key. OUTLINE: 1px #05050a, all actors/projectiles/pickups, never terrain; player and minibosses additionally get sel-out (top/left outline replaced by ramp[3]). SIZE LADDER (hitbox, keeping player 12x20): chaff 10x12 = 0.6x player height; grunt 12x20 = 1.0x; bruiser 20x28 = 1.4x; elite 24x34 = 1.7x; miniboss 34x46 = 2.3x. Sprite box = hitbox + 4px padding per side, to hold the outline, the rim, and squash/stretch overshoot. DETAIL BUDGET: chaff max 2 shapes + 1 focal; grunt 3 + 1; bruiser/elite 4 + 1; miniboss 6 + 2; player 7 + 1. ANIMATION CLOCK: 12 fps for grunt poses (exactly 5 sim frames per art frame at 60), 15 fps for player locomotion (4 sim frames), 30 fps for all attack arcs and impacts (2 sim frames) — 30 fps matched to Dead Cells' stated animation rate, and all three divide 60 exactly so nothing judders. FRAME BUDGET: player 34 (idle 4, run 6, jump 1, fall 1, land 2, attack-A 5, attack-B 5, dash 3, hurt 2, dig 4, death 1); each grunt 11 (idle 2, walk 4, telegraph 2, attack 2, hurt 1); each miniboss 16 (idle 3, move 4, telegraph 3, attack 4, hurt 1, death 1). Facing is ctx.scale(-1,1) so it costs zero extra frames.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Every telegraph gets 2-3 dedicated frames and that is deliberate: with 19 enemies, 12 affixes and 5 threat tiers, the telegraph is the only thing standing between the player and unreadable chaos, so it gets a bigger share of the frame budget than the attack itself. Note the miniboss at 34x46 occupies ~30% of the 272x153 logical view height, which is what makes it read as a boss without any extra art.

*Source: proposal; 30fps from the Dead Cells deep-dive, 3:1 from WCAG SC 1.4.11*

#### Code-drawn vs embedded-spritesheet — the actual byte comparison

**Numbers.** ~116k pixels of sprite data; est. 27-55 KB as embedded base64 PNG vs est. 12-18 KB as code. Current index.html is 202,675 bytes. These are my estimates, not measurements.

Under your one-file constraint both options are legal (embedded base64 counts as 'no external asset files'), so the choice should be made on numbers and flexibility, not on the constraint. 257 frames at an average 18x25 is ~116,000 pixels, which packs into roughly a 512x256 atlas. A flat-colour, limited-palette PNG of that size typically lands 20-40 KB, and base64 inflates it by ~37% to roughly 27-55 KB. A parametric draw system covering 3 rigs x 19 enemies plus the player is realistically 12-18 KB of JS.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Code wins, but not mainly on bytes — it wins because 12 affixes, 5 threat tiers, elite variants and 6 biome retints multiply a spritesheet combinatorially and cost a parametric drawer nothing (elite = swap ramp + scale 1.15 + bigger focal). Bake the parametric drawer to offscreen canvases once at load and you get spritesheet-grade runtime performance with code-grade flexibility. That is the correct translation of Dead Cells' pipeline into your constraints: author procedurally, render once, blit forever.

*Source: estimate; file size from ls of /workspaces/Sweet-Spot/shardfall/index.html*

### Recommendations

- Build the black-silhouette debug view FIRST, before any art work. A keybind that renders all 19 enemies plus the player as pure #000 silhouettes on white, in a grid, at 1x and 0.5x. If you cannot name each one at a glance you have no art direction yet. This is ~20 lines and it is the only way to make the squint test a repeatable check rather than a vibe.
- Add a contrast unit test to the existing shardfall/test/ suite: implement the WCAG relative-luminance formula (0.2126R + 0.7152G + 0.0722B on linearised sRGB) and assert that every actor body colour clears 3.0:1 against every terrain base colour, and 2.5:1 against every terrain edge colour. My proposed palette passes at 3.92 and 2.61 worst-case. Without this test, palette drift will silently make enemies invisible — my own first draft failed at 1.14:1.
- Adopt the 63-colour palette as a PALETTE const and change TILES[].c from a flat colour to a ramp reference. The existing renderer already darkens the bottom 2px of tiles (#00000030) and hash-varies 30% of tiles (#00000018) — replace those alpha-black overlays with real ramp steps. Alpha-black desaturates and flattens; ramp steps hue-shift and imply a light source, for the same number of draw calls.
- Enforce one hard clamp above all others: terrain luminance <= 0.08 in every pixel including highlight edges; actor body luminance >= 0.25. This is the Downwell rule (bright = it matters) expressed numerically, and it is worth more than any amount of sprite detail in a game with destructible terrain and heavy VFX.
- Define exactly 3 parametric rigs (biped, quadruped, floater) and express all 19 enemies as (rig, proportions, ramp, top-shape, scale). This is Vasseur's model-reuse trick, which he credits with saving 'hundred of hours', translated into code. It also makes elites and affix variants nearly free: swap ramp, scale 1.15, enlarge the focal.
- Bind shape language to behaviour, not flavour: triangle-dominant = charges, square-dominant = tanky/slow, circle-dominant = ranged/floating. Then a player who has learned three shapes reads every new enemy in the 19-strong roster correctly on first sight — which is what makes a roguelite roster feel authored.
- Give every entity exactly one high-contrast focal element (a 2x2-3x3 'shard' at luminance > 0.6 on a body at 0.15-0.45) and place it at the attack origin. This is the mechanical version of what makes Hollow Knight's mask and Dead Cells' flaming head iconic, and because the icon and the attack tell are the same pixel, learning to look at it is learning to fight.
- Spend the animation budget on keyposes and VFX, not in-betweens — this is Vasseur's stated approach (pose-to-pose, interpolation only outside keys, VFX compensating for animation limits). Budget: player 34 frames, grunts 11 each, minibosses 16 each, with 2-3 frames always reserved for the telegraph. Total 257 cached bitmaps at ~0.44 MB RGBA.
- Use three animation clocks that all divide 60 exactly: 12 fps grunt poses (5 sim frames), 15 fps player locomotion (4 sim frames), 30 fps attack arcs and impacts (2 sim frames). 30 fps for attacks matches Dead Cells' stated animation rate and is where the money should go.
- Implement readability in this order (value per unit of effort, highest first): baked 1px outline on actors only + rim light on top/left edges; ground drop shadow with alpha 0.35 * (1 - airHeight/48); baked white-silhouette flash for 3 frames on hit; volume-preserving squash and stretch (takeoff sy 1.18/sx 0.86, land sy 0.78/sx 1.22, anchored at feet); 4x4 Bayer dither for the background depth band; colour-temperature separation between biome and its spawns.
- Bake the hit flash as a second white-silhouette bitmap rather than tinting at runtime. Runtime canvas tinting requires globalCompositeOperation, which is documented as much slower than source-over in Firefox, and the common source-atop tint trick fails outright for pure white. Baking costs ~0.44 MB extra and zero frame time.
- Make the outline rule semantic, not stylistic: outlined = can kill you or help you; un-outlined = scenery. Terrain gets no outline ever. Players learn this in about thirty seconds without being told, and it does most of the figure/ground work in a game where the terrain is constantly being destroyed.
- Decouple sprite box from hitbox — sprite = hitbox + 4px padding on all sides. You need that headroom for the outline, the rim light and squash/stretch overshoot, and it lets you make things look right without touching the collision tuning that the project's constraints protect.
- Use scroll rates that are integer divisors of the 272-logical-pixel viewport (1/8, 1/4, 1/2 of camera) for the background layers so they tile seamlessly, per Slynyrd's perfect-divisor rule. Cave-interior backgrounds must be the same hue as the terrain ramp, two steps darker — never a different hue, or the eye reads them as objects rather than as space.

### Caveats and unsourced claims

- Rust (#c88154, luminance 0.286) and Wraith Orchid (#c974af, luminance 0.280) are nearly identical in luminance. In greyscale, and for red-green and blue-yellow colour-blind players, those two enemy families are indistinguishable by colour. Their identification MUST be carried by silhouette and top-shape, not hue. Verify by rendering the roster desaturated — this is a real defect my own generated palette contains, and it is only tolerable because the silhouette rules cover it.
- The 3:1 contrast gate is in direct tension with 'dark, layered underground'. The failure mode is brightening the terrain to make enemies pop, which flattens the whole game. The correct fix is always to raise actor luminance or lower terrain luminance, never to close the gap from the terrain side. Write the constraint down or it will be violated the first time something looks muddy.
- Baking sprites to offscreen canvases assumes a stable integer render scale. If the canvas is scaled by a fractional devicePixelRatio, 1px outlines and the 2px module will alias into mush and the whole chunky look collapses. Lock to integer scale and set imageSmoothingEnabled = false on every context, including every offscreen one.
- 62 gems plus 12 affixes plus 6 damage types is more semantic colour than any palette can carry. The FX hue cap of 8 is load-bearing — if gem colours are allowed to leak into the FX tier, high-luminance colour stops meaning 'react to this' and the Downwell rule breaks. Gems should be distinguished in UI by shape and position, not by adding hues to the gameplay palette.
- Elite/affix visual stacking is the most likely source of unreadable chaos in a game with 12 affixes and 5 threat tiers. Reserve exactly one visual channel for elite status (I suggest outline colour) and exactly one for affix (the focal shard's colour or pulse rate). If affixes each get their own particle system they will composite into noise at threat tier 5.
- I could not find any primary source for per-animation frame counts in Dead Cells. The '8-12 frame attack animations' figure that surfaces in search results comes from AI-generated SEO content and should not be cited or relied on. The frame budget in this spec is my proposal, anchored only to the sourced 30 fps animation rate.
- Lospec publishes hex lists but not the authors' own ramp groupings for DB16, AAP-64 or EDG32. My decomposition of those palettes into ramps is inference from the ordered hex list, not a documented claim by the palette authors. Do not cite it as their stated design.
- The per-layer desaturation and darkening percentages that circulate for atmospheric perspective have no primary source I could find — only scroll-speed ratios are properly documented. The plane luminance values I give (0.002 / 0.005 / 0.029 / 0.354) are derived from my own palette, not from any published game's numbers.
- Baking 257 sprites (plus 257 flash variants) at load adds a startup cost to a PWA that is expected to launch instantly offline. Measure it — if it exceeds ~50 ms, bake lazily per entity type on first spawn rather than all at once.


<a id="combatmath"></a>
## Combat mathematics and scaling

### Summary

Combat-maths research pass for SHARDFALL. Primary-source formulas gathered for PoE (damage pipeline, armour k=10, 75% res cap, 20%/s leech cap), Diablo 2 (flat "Damage Reduced by X" uncapped, 50% cap on %DR), Diablo 3 (armor/(armor+50*mLvl) = /3500 at 70, multiplicative DR stacking), Diablo 4 (crit/vulnerable moved from multiplicative buckets to additive with a fixed x1.50/x1.20 baseline), Risk of Rain 2 (coeff = (playerFactor + minutes*timeFactor) * 1.15^stages; +30% HP / +20% damage per level), Dead Cells (DPS = base * 1.15^(stat-1), quadratic capped HP multipliers, Malaise tiers), Hades (Pact ranks: +20% enemy damage/rank x5, +15% enemy HP/rank x2, -25% healing/rank x4), Terraria (damage - defense*f, f = 0.5/0.75/1.0, floor 1). Diagnosis for Shardfall: the two systems interact badly. `max(1, dmg - armor)` has an EHP pole — EHP is unbounded as armor approaches hit size — and combined with the existing 0.7s i-frames it makes a 40-armor player take exactly 1 damage from crawlers, bats and sporelings at EVERY depth in the game (world bottom is depth 3140, and crawler damage only reaches 36 there). Meanwhile depthMul = 1 + depth/900 tops out at 4.49x, while a stacked build's DPS grows 15-60x, so the last two biomes invert into a power fantasy with no threat. Concrete replacements given with full tables: split HP and damage curves (HP ^1.75, damage ^1.10 on the same 1+depth/900 base), and either a bounded floor `max(0.25*raw, raw-armor)` (one-line change, keeps current feel) or a PoE-shaped `DR = A/(A + 1.5*d)` capped at 75%.

### Findings (16)

#### PoE damage pipeline — full order of operations

**Numbers.** Hit = (Base + Added) x Effectiveness x (1 + SUM(increased)) x PROD(1 + more_i) x CritMulti, then defender: max(0, raw - flat) x (1 + SUM(inc dmg taken)) x PROD(more taken) x (1 - DR). Diminishing additive: +50% increased on top of +100% = 1.50->2.00 = +33% real; on top of +300% = 4.00->4.50 = +12.5% real. A single 'more 40%' support = x1.40 regardless of stack size.

Path of Exile's hit damage resolves in a fixed order: (1) base damage from the weapon or spell, plus all ADDED FLAT damage from gear/gems, scaled by the skill's 'effectiveness of added damage'; (2) CONVERSION and 'gain X% as Y' shifts damage between types; (3) all INCREASED/REDUCED modifiers sum into a single additive pool per damage type — one (1 + sum) term; (4) all MORE/LESS modifiers multiply as independent factors — a product term; (5) critical strike multiplier applies; (6) on the defender's side: flat reductions first, then the defender's own additive increased/reduced-damage-taken pool, then more/less-damage-taken multipliers, then armour/resistances. Key mechanical subtlety Mark_GGG stated explicitly: 'increased damage taken' and 'increased damage' interact multiplicatively NOT because of a stacking rule but because they modify two different values (one pre-mitigation, one post-mitigation) — additive stacking only applies between mods on the SAME value. He also said GGG refused to relabel 'increased damage taken' as 'more' because 'We won't try to solve this problem by lying to our players about how the modifiers actually work.' On the DESIGN reason for the split, I could not find a single canonical GGG quote; the well-evidenced structural fact is that MORE multipliers are almost exclusively gated behind support gems and a handful of keystones/uniques, while INCREASED is what passive tree nodes and gear affixes grant in bulk. That is the actual power-control mechanism: the tree hands out hundreds of additive points whose marginal value decays (going 100%->150% increased is +25% real damage, not +50%), while the six-link is the only place multiplicative power lives, and it is quantity-limited to 5 supports. Support gems also PAY for their multiplier — GMP fires 4 extra projectiles with a 'less projectile damage' clause and a mana multiplier; Greater Spell Echo repeats twice with 30% more damage per repeat plus a cost multiplier. Confidence: HIGH on the pipeline and on the increased/more mechanics; MEDIUM on the exact GMP/Spell Echo percentages (they have been re-tuned across leagues); the 'why' paragraph is my inference from structure, not a sourced dev quote.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall already has the right shape — `a.more *= x` as a product pool and inc('dmg') as an additive pool, with uniques applying LAST via mod(). Keep it. The gap is that Shardfall has FOUR multiplicative pools, not two: PROD(more), crit expectation, depthMul, and elite/threat multipliers. Add a fifth-pool audit rule to CONFIG: every new gem must declare which pool it feeds, and only SUPPORT gems may ever touch `a.more`. Attunement level-up picks, affixes and meta-tree nodes should feed `inc` only — right now `keen`/`cruel`/`m5` all feed crit, which is a third multiplicative pool wearing an additive costume.

*Source: https://pathofexile.fandom.com/wiki/Receiving_damage ; https://www.pathofexile.com/forum/view-thread/892570 (Mark_GGG) ; https://maxroll.gg/poe/getting-started/damage-for-beginners*

#### Armour formula shapes across ARPGs — the four families

**Numbers.** PoE1: DR = A/(A + 10d), cap 90%. PoE2: DR = A/(A + 12d). D3: DR = A/(A + 50*mLvl) = A/(A+3500) at 70; resist = R/(R+350). D2: %DR capped 50%; flat DR uncapped. Terraria: max(1, dmg - def*f), f in {0.5, 0.75, 1.0}.

Four distinct mitigation families ship in major ARPGs, and they behave very differently against many-small-hits vs one-big-hit. (A) HIT-SIZE-AWARE RATIO — Path of Exile 1: reduction = Armour / (Armour + 10 x RawPhysDamage). Because the incoming hit is in the denominator, armour is strong against chip and weak against slams. Rules of thumb that fall out of k=10: armour = 5x the hit -> 33%, = 10x -> 50%, = 30x -> 75%, = 90x -> 90%. PoE 2 retuned to k=12: 6x -> 33%, 12x -> 50%, 36x -> 75%, 108x -> 90%. Physical damage reduction is hard-capped at 90%. Armour reduction combines ADDITIVELY with endurance charges/Soul of Steel/Chaos Golem, and that block then multiplies against Fortify and Arctic Armour: PhysRed = 1 - (1 - ArmourRed - ECharges - SoulSteel - Golem) x (1 - Fortify) x (1 - ArcticArmour). (B) HIT-SIZE-BLIND RATIO — Diablo 3: DR = Armor / (Armor + 3500) at level 70, generalised as Armor / (Armor + 50 x monsterLevel); resistances use the same shape with /350. Identical percentage against a 1-damage tick and a 500,000 damage Elite slam. All separate DR sources stack MULTIPLICATIVELY: final = raw x (1-DR1) x (1-DR2) x ... (C) FLAT PERCENTAGE — Diablo 2 'Damage Reduced by X%', capped at 50% in both PvM and PvP, applied after flat DR and before Absorb. (D) FLAT SUBTRACTION — Diablo 2 'Damage Reduced by X' (integer, NO CAP — this is why DR-stacked D2 characters became functionally immune to physical trash) and Terraria: net = floor(rawDamage - defense x f) where f = 0.5 (Classic) / 0.75 (Expert) / 1.0 (Master), and 'the value cannot be less than 1'. Confidence: HIGH on all four; the PoE1 k=10 constant is corroborated by the official-forum spreadsheet thread and by the 10x/50% rule of thumb being self-consistent.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall is currently model (D) with a floor of 1 and no cap — the exact model D2 had to patch around and the one Terraria only survives because defense values are small relative to late-game boss damage. Shardfall's armor values (2-60) and enemy damage values (6-127) live on the SAME numeric scale, which is precisely the regime where flat subtraction detonates. See the next finding for the exact break depths.

*Source: https://www.pathofexile.com/forum/view-thread/1468738 ; https://maxroll.gg/d3/resources/damage-reduction-explained ; https://maxroll.gg/d2/resources/damage-reductions ; https://terraria.wiki.gg/wiki/Damage_reduction ; https://mobalytics.gg/poe-2/guides/armour*

#### Where max(1, dmg - armor) breaks in Shardfall — exact depths

**Numbers.** Break depths for enemy damage > armor A. A=20: crawler 1350, bat 2100, stalker 485, brute 225, archer 159, smith 0. A=30: crawler 2475, bat 3600(never), stalker 1177, brute 788, archer 688, smith 225. A=40: crawler 3600(never), bat 5100(never), stalker 1869, brute 1350, archer 1218, smith 600. A=50: crawler/bat/stalker all never. EHP cliff at 250 HP vs 36 dmg: A=25 -> 22 hits, A=30 -> 42, A=33 -> 83, A=35 -> 250, A=36+ -> 250 (floor). i-frame rate cap: incoming DPS <= d/0.7; at the armour floor that is 1.43 DPS.

The break is not gradual, it is a pole. Effective HP against a single attacker = HP / max(1, d - A). As A approaches d - 1 that denominator goes to 1 and EHP goes to HP — a 250 HP player takes 250 hits. Marginal value of armour is constant at -1 damage per point RIGHT UP TO the wall, then instantly zero, so percentage mitigation A/d rises without bound. Concretely, using Shardfall's own tables (world bottom is absolute tile 3200, SURFACE=60, so max depth = 3140 and depthMul tops out at 4.49x): crawler base dmg 8 -> 36 at the bottom of the world; bat 6 -> 27; sporeling 7 -> 31; stalker 13 -> 58; brute 16 -> 72; archer bolt 17 -> 76; smith 24 -> 108. Now solve d0 x (1 + depth/900) > A for the depth at which each enemy stops being ignorable. At A = 40 (Plate 12 + The Mountain 22 + a couple of +arm affixes, entirely reachable): crawler needs depth 3600, bat needs 5100 — BOTH ARE PAST THE BOTTOM OF THE WORLD. That player takes exactly 1 damage from crawlers, bats and sporelings for the entire run, in every biome, forever. At A = 30: crawler crosses at depth 2475, bat at 3600 (never), stalker at 1177, brute at 788. At A = 20: crawler 1350, bat 2100, stalker 485, brute 225. Second break: the cliff. At depth 3200 a crawler hits for 36 on a 250 HP player. A=30 -> 250/6 = 42 hits to die. A=36 -> 250/1 = 250 hits to die. A 20% armour increase produced a 6x EHP increase. No item budget can be balanced against a curve with a 6x step in it. Third break, and this is the one that makes it fatal: Shardfall already grants 0.7 s of invulnerability after every hit (P.inv = 0.7 in hurtPlayer). i-frames alone cap incoming DPS at d/0.7 = 1.43d regardless of how many enemies are on screen. Flat armour's advertised identity — 'strong against many small hits' — is a job i-frames already do. The two stack, and at the floor the player takes 1/0.7 = 1.43 DPS: 175 seconds of uninterrupted body-contact to die.

**Apply to Shardfall.** This is the single highest-priority fix. Two options, both one-liners in applyArmor at line 1479. OPTION 1 (minimum change, preserves current feel): `function applyArmor(raw,armor){return Math.max(raw*0.25, Math.max(ARMOR_FLOOR, raw-(armor||0)))}` — armour can never remove more than 75% of any hit, so the EHP multiplier from armour is hard-capped at 4x and the pole disappears. Note this mirrors PoE's 90% phys-DR cap and D2's 50% %DR cap: every shipped flat/percentage model has a ceiling, and D2's UNCAPPED flat 'Damage Reduced by X' is the one everyone points at as the mistake. OPTION 2 (better curve, bigger change): replace subtraction with PoE's shape rescaled to Shardfall's numbers: `DR = min(0.75, A/(A + 1.5*d))`, dmg = raw*(1-DR). k=1.5 is chosen so that armour equal to two-thirds of the hit gives 50%. This keeps the 'strong vs chip, weak vs slams' identity SMOOTHLY, with no cliff and no immunity.

*Source: Derived from /workspaces/Sweet-Spot/shardfall/index.html lines 226 (WORLD_H=3200, SURFACE=60), 241 (ARMOR_FLOOR=1), 1479 (applyArmor), 1602 (depthMul), 1690-1700 (hurtPlayer, P.inv=0.7), 400-447 (GEAR/UNIQUES arm values), ENEMIES table. My own calculation, not sourced.*

#### Replacement mitigation curve — full tables for both options

**Numbers.** k = 1.5, DR cap 75%. Or floor fraction 0.25 (max 75% removed by armour), absolute floor still 1.

Option 2 table, DR = min(0.75, A/(A + 1.5d)), rows = player armour, columns = raw incoming damage. A=2 (Leather Vest): 6dmg 18%, 8 14%, 12 10%, 16 8%, 24 5%, 40 3%, 80 2%, 120 1%. A=6 (Chainmail): 40/33/25/20/14/9/5/3%. A=12 (Plate): 57/50/40/33/25/17/9/6%. A=20: 69/63/53/45/36/25/14/10%. A=30: 75/71/63/56/45/33/20/14%. A=40: 75/75/69/63/53/40/25/18%. A=60 (Mountain + everything): 75/75/75/71/63/50/33/25%. Read the columns: at every armour value the number falls monotonically as the hit gets bigger, which is exactly the Vanguard-vs-Marksman separation the design doc wants, but it never reaches immunity and never has a step. Option 1 table, taken = max(0.25*raw, raw - A), same axes: A=2 -> 4/6/10/14/22/38/78/118. A=6 -> 2/2/6/10/18/34/74/114. A=12 -> 2/2/3/4/12/28/68/108. A=20 -> 2/2/3/4/6/20/60/100. A=30 -> 2/2/3/4/6/10/50/90. A=40 -> 2/2/3/4/6/10/40/80. A=60 -> 2/2/3/4/6/10/20/60. Option 1 still has a kink (the point where the 25% floor takes over) but the kink is bounded and identical in shape for every armour value, so it is balanceable. Confidence: my own proposal, constants chosen against Shardfall's actual gear and enemy tables.

**Apply to Shardfall.** If you take Option 2 you must also retune armour VALUES down, because A/(A+1.5d) is generous: Plate 12 already gives 50% against a crawler. Suggested rebase: vest 2->3, chain 6->8, plate 12->14, shield 8->10, harness 3->4, and cut affix arm range from 2-9 to 2-6 so a fully-committed tank lands around A=45-55, i.e. 75% capped against chip and ~50% against a smith. If you take Option 1, no value retuning is needed at all — that is its main advantage for a single-file game.

*Source: My own proposal, calibrated against Shardfall GEAR arm values {vest 2, harness 3, chain 6, shield 8, plate 12} + UNIQUES {+8, +15, +16, +22, -4} + affix arm 2-9 + meta +6 + attunement bulwark +10 + threat tier II/V +6.*

#### Enemy scaling curves — what shipped games actually do

**Numbers.** RoR2: +30% HP / +20% dmg per level; stageFactor 1.15^stages; timeFactor 0.0506 x diff x players^0.2. Dead Cells: DPS 1.15^(s-1); HP caps 12.375x / 7.25x / 22x. Hades max stat inflation across 32 Heat: +100% enemy damage, +30% enemy HP, +40% speed, +60% count, -100% healing. Malaise: 10 tiers x 50 pts, +60% enemy strength max, 1.3 pts/sec gain, 250 removed per boss.

RISK OF RAIN 2 (fully datamined, HIGH confidence): coeff = (playerFactor + timeInMinutes x timeFactor) x stageFactor, where playerFactor = 1 + 0.3 x (playerCount - 1), timeFactor = 0.0506 x difficultyValue x playerCount^0.2, stageFactor = 1.15^stagesCompleted, and difficultyValue = 1 (Drizzle) / 2 (Rainstorm) / 3 (Monsoon). Enemy level = 1 + (coeff - playerFactor)/0.33, capped at 99 (9999 in Simulacrum). Enemies gain +30% health and +20% damage PER LEVEL over base. Two things matter here: (a) the curve is LINEAR IN TIME but EXPONENTIAL IN STAGES — 1.15^stage means every stage is a 15% compounding step, so the pressure comes from progression not from clock-stalling; (b) HP scales 1.5x faster than damage (30 vs 20), because player DPS scales far faster than player EHP. DEAD CELLS (HIGH confidence): player DPS = [Base DPS] x 1.15^(Stats - 1) — pure exponential, 15% compounding per scroll, identical to RoR2's stage step. Player max HP = 100 x (Brutality mult) x (Tactics mult) x (Survival mult), each a QUADRATIC that is hard-capped: Survival f(s) = 1 + (833/1180)s - (7/1180)s^2 for s <= 59, then flat 22x forever; Brutality f(s) = 1 + (897/1360)s - (13/1360)s^2 for s <= 34, then 12.375x; Tactics f(s) = 1 + (49/96)s - (1/96)s^2 for s <= 24, then 7.25x. So Dead Cells deliberately makes offence exponential and defence concave-then-capped. Its difficulty tiers are NOT stat multipliers — Boss Stem Cells change item level (+1 at 3BC, +3 at 4-5BC), cell drop multipliers (2x/2x/3x/3x), and REMOVE HEALING (health fountains every other passage at 1BC, all fountains removed at 2BC, 3 flask charges total at 3BC, zero flask charges at 4-5BC). At 5BC the Malaise timer is the difficulty: 10 tiers of 50 points each (500 max), gained at 1.3 pts/sec at 100% enemies alive (0.9 at 50%, 0.5 at 25%, 0 at 10%), removed by 1.8 per mob kill / 25 per elite / 250 per boss / 150 per flask. Malaise effects: enemy strength +20% at tiers 1-2, +40% at tier 3, +60% at tiers 4-5; attack delay halved at tiers 1-6 and quartered at 7-10; teleport charge 0.75s -> 0.45s -> 0.25s. HADES (HIGH confidence, Pact of Punishment): Hard Labor +20% enemy damage per rank, 5 ranks (+100% total), 1 Heat each. Calisthenics Program +15% enemy health per rank, 2 ranks (+30%), 1 Heat each. Forced Overtime +20% enemy move/attack speed per rank, 2 ranks, 3 Heat each. Jury Summons +20% more enemies per rank, 3 ranks. Lasting Consequences -25% healing effectiveness per rank, 4 ranks (100%). Heightened Security: traps/magma +400% damage, 1 rank. Damage Control +1 Shielded Health per rank, 2 ranks. Routine Inspection disables 3 Mirror talents per rank, 4 ranks (12 talents). Tight Deadline -2:00 per region per rank, 2 ranks, floor 7 minutes. Extreme Measures rearms 1 boss per rank, 3 ranks. THE KEY OBSERVATION: Hades' maximum stat inflation across the whole 32-Heat ladder is only +100% damage and +30% HP. Everything else is structural — fewer choices, less healing, more bodies, new boss moves, a clock. SLAY THE SPIRE does the same thing (A7 normal enemies tougher, A8 elites tougher, A9 bosses tougher, A17 new movesets) and does not publish stat multipliers because they are small and per-enemy.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Two direct lessons. (1) SPLIT HP AND DAMAGE SCALING — every one of these games scales enemy HP faster than enemy damage (RoR2 30/20 = 1.5:1). Shardfall applies the SAME depthMul to both, which is why the deep biomes are simultaneously too soft (HP too low vs a 20x DPS build) and, for a no-armour build, too spiky. (2) Shardfall's THREATS table is already Hades-shaped — 'Everything down here has armor', 'Your light is smaller' are structural rules, not sliders — and the Hades data validates that: only +100% damage and +30% HP across the entire Heat ladder, everything else structural. Do not be tempted to add a flat 'threat multiplies enemy HP by 2.5' tier; keep the named-rule design. Also: your `dens: 1.6` and `elite: 2.2` on tier IV/V is exactly Jury Summons, and your WEIGHT system is exactly Malaise. Consider giving The Weight tiered thresholds with published numbers the way Malaise does (10 tiers, discrete effects) rather than a smooth ramp — discrete tiers are legible and Motion Twin found that legibility mattered.

*Source: https://riskofrain2.wiki.gg/wiki/Difficulty ; https://riskofrain2.wiki.gg/wiki/Level ; https://deadcells.wiki.gg/wiki/Stats ; https://deadcells.wiki.gg/wiki/Malaise ; https://deadcells.wiki.gg/wiki/Boss_Stem_Cells ; https://www.rpgsite.net/feature/10287-hades-pact-of-punishment-heat-modifiers-and-how-to-maximize-your-rewards ; https://slaythespire.wiki.gg/wiki/Ascension*

#### Replacement depth curve for Shardfall — with the table

**Numbers.** hpMul = (1+depth/900)^1.75, dmgMul = (1+depth/900)^1.10. Bottom of world: HP x13.84, DMG x5.22 (vs current x4.49 on both). Median-build TTK crawler 0.8/1.1/1.3/1.3/1.3/1.1s at depths 0/600/1200/1800/2400/3200; smith 4.8/7.1/8.1/8.2/7.8/7.0s. Under current linear: smith 4.8/4.8/4.3/3.6/3.0/2.2s.

Proposed: keep the existing `1 + depth/900` as the base term (so nothing else in the file needs to change) and raise it to two different exponents. hpMul(depth) = (1 + depth/900)^1.75, dmgMul(depth) = (1 + depth/900)^1.10. Per-biome table (depth is relative to SURFACE=60, biome boundaries taken from the BIOMES array at line 263): surface depth 10 -> base 1.01, HP 1.02x, DMG 1.01x. caves depth 340 -> base 1.38, HP 1.75x, DMG 1.42x. fungal depth 840 -> base 1.93, HP 3.17x, DMG 2.07x. ruins depth 1540 -> base 2.71, HP 5.73x, DMG 3.00x. forge depth 2340 -> base 3.60, HP 9.41x, DMG 4.09x. abyss depth 3140 -> base 4.49, HP 13.84x, DMG 5.22x. Resulting enemy HP at the bottom: crawler 24 -> 332, bat 14 -> 194, rockling 40 -> 553, shieldman 70 -> 969, brute 85 -> 1176, smith 150 -> 2076. Resulting enemy damage at the bottom: bat 6 -> 31, crawler 8 -> 42, stalker 13 -> 68, brute 16 -> 84, archer bolt 17 -> 89, smith 24 -> 125. The ratio hp/dmg exponent 1.75:1.10 = 1.59:1 is deliberately close to RoR2's 30%:20% = 1.5:1 ratio. TTK validation (my simulation, three build archetypes over the run): a MEDIAN build — base 30 DPS growing to ~306 DPS at the bottom via +140% increased, four 1.30 more-multipliers and crit E rising to 1.49 — holds crawler TTK at 0.8s -> 1.1s -> 1.1s and smith TTK at 4.8s -> 8.2s -> 7.0s across the whole run. Under the CURRENT linear curve that same build sees smith TTK fall from 4.8s to 2.2s, i.e. the deepest miniboss in the game is easier than the tutorial one. Confidence: exponents are my proposal; the TTK numbers are my simulation using Shardfall's own GEAR/ENEMIES tables.

**Apply to Shardfall.** One-line implementation that keeps every existing call site working: `function depthMul(y){return 1+Math.max(0,(y/TILE-SURFACE))/900}` becomes `function depthK(y){return 1+Math.max(0,(y/TILE-SURFACE))/900} ; function hpMul(y){return Math.pow(depthK(y),1.75)} ; function dmgMul(y){return Math.pow(depthK(y),1.10)}`. Then audit the ~12 depthMul call sites: enemy HP spawn and split (lines 1641, 1726, 1756) use hpMul; enemy contact damage, projectile damage and burn (1791, 1793) use dmgMul; shard drops (1672) should use depthK unchanged or the economy inflates 14x. Thorns and dodge-blast (1702, 1810, 1840, 2077) are PLAYER damage and must use hpMul, not dmgMul, or they fall off a cliff — that is the easy bug to make here.

*Source: My own proposal. Derived from /workspaces/Sweet-Spot/shardfall/index.html BIOMES (line 263), ENEMIES, GEAR, and depthMul (line 1602). Exponent ratio calibrated to match RoR2's published 30%/20% per-level split.*

#### Crit design — base values, expected value, and the exact overtake breakpoint

**Numbers.** PoE base crit multi 150%, weapon base crit 5.0-7.7%, crit chance capped (95-100%). D3 base 5% / +50%. Shardfall base 5% / 1.8x -> E = 1.040. E = 1 + c(m-1). Optimum at (m-1) = r*c. Shardfall full-crit build (Deadeye +30% +0.9, keen +8% +0.40, cruel +35%, meta +25%, ~3 crit affixes ~18%): c=0.61, m=3.70, E=2.65, CV=0.50. Crit overtakes an increased-damage path when c(m-1) > i.

Shipped baselines: Path of Exile — base critical strike MULTIPLIER 150% for all characters; base crit CHANCE comes from the weapon (typically 5.0%-7.7%; spells from the gem). Diablo 3 — base 5% crit chance, base 50% crit damage (i.e. 1.5x). Shardfall — 5% / 1.8x. The expected-damage multiplier is E = 1 + c(m - 1) where c is crit chance as a fraction and m is the crit multiplier. Maxroll states it as E[D] = Base x (1 + CHC x CHD) with CHD as the BONUS, which is the same thing. Note how small the baseline is by design: PoE 1 + 0.05(0.5) = 1.025, D3 1.025, Shardfall 1 + 0.05(0.8) = 1.040. Base crit contributes 2.5-4% of your damage. It is a build axis, not a stat. MARGINAL VALUE AND THE OPTIMAL RATIO: dE/dc = (m-1) and dE/dm = c. Under a budget where the item pool trades r points of crit-multiplier per 1 point of crit chance, maximise 1 + c(m-1) subject to c = c0 + x, (m-1) = M0 + r(B-x): d/dx = (m-1) - rc = 0, so the optimum sits at (m - 1) = r x c. With D3's affix exchange rate of about 10% CHD per 1% CHC this gives CHD_bonus = 10 x CHC, which is the well-known 'golden ratio' — 50% CHC pairs with 500% CHD. Maxroll's worked example: trading 5% CHC for 20% CHD at their sample stats yields about +2.67% damage, i.e. slightly better than break-even, so at those stats the player was crit-chance-heavy. WHEN CRIT OVERTAKES FLAT DAMAGE: a crit path is worth 1 + c(m-1); an 'increased damage' path is worth 1 + i. Crit wins when c(m-1) > i. VARIANCE: coefficient of variation of a single hit is sqrt(c(1-c)) x (m-1) / (1 + c(m-1)). At 5%/1.8x that is 0.168 (17%). At 61%/3.7x it is 0.498 (50%). At 95%/4.0x it collapses back to 0.170 — variance is maximised in the middle of the crit-chance range and falls off at both ends, which is why 'get to high crit chance' feels so much better than half-committing. Reliability: P(at least one crit in n hits) = 1 - (1-c)^n; at c=0.20 you need n=11 hits for 90% confidence, at c=0.40 you need n=5.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Your crit ceiling is already E = 2.65x for a committed build, which is a full third multiplicative pool sitting alongside PROD(more) and (1 + inc). That is fine ONLY if you price it — check that the crit affix ranges (crit 3-9%, critMult 12-35%) give an exchange rate near r = 4 (35/9), which means the optimum is (m-1) = 4c: at c = 0.61 the ideal m-1 is 2.44, i.e. m = 3.44. You are at 3.70, so the current uniques slightly over-weight crit multiplier. Nudge Deadeye from +0.9 to +0.7 crit multi and it lands on the optimum. Separately: CRIT_ST = 1.5 means crits also apply status at 1.5x — that is a FOURTH pool for ailment builds. Either accept that ailment+crit is the intended top-tier synergy and balance the ceiling around it, or drop CRIT_ST to 1.25.

*Source: https://maxroll.gg/d3/resources/critical-hit-chance-hit-damage-explained ; https://pathofexile.fandom.com/wiki/Critical_strike ; https://www.poewiki.net/wiki/Critical_Strike_Chance ; breakpoint algebra is mine.*

#### Time-to-kill and time-to-die targets

**Numbers.** Trash 2-4 hits / <1.5s. Elite 6-9 hits / 3-5s. Miniboss 25-45 hits / 15-30s. Boss 45-90s, hard ceiling 2 min. Player: 6-10 grunt hits early, 3-5 late; big-hit budget 25-40% max HP. Shardfall at depth 0: crawler 24 HP / sword 10 dmg = 3 hits (correct); smith 150 HP / 10 = 15 hits at 2.6 hits/sec = 5.7 s (a touch short for a miniboss).

Sourced guidance is thinner than the maths, and it is deliberately RELATIVE rather than absolute. The clearest industry statement (Ask a Game Dev, a working AAA designer, MEDIUM-HIGH confidence): 'Most of the time video game enemies have a lifespan of maybe a few seconds once engaged'; if most enemies die in three seconds, one that lasts six 'feels roughly twice as strong to the player'; bosses 'should have a significantly longer Time to Kill than your other foes' but avoid bosses exceeding 'more than two minutes or so per player engaging'; and the practical warning that 6 enemies at 30 seconds each is a 3-minute encounter, which is too long. The same source gives the balancing method directly: 'instead of thinking in numbers, it's easier to think in terms of number of hits to kill.' Working targets I derive from that plus the shipped games above, marked as MY PROPOSAL: trash 2-4 hits / 0.8-1.5 s; a 'teaching' grunt (Shardfall's stalker, ember, sporeling) 2-3 hits so its gimmick fires before it dies; an armoured/elite grunt 6-9 hits / 3-5 s; a miniboss 25-45 hits / 15-30 s with at least one phase change; a biome boss 60-120 hits / 45-90 s across 2-3 phases. On the receiving side: a healthy action-roguelite player should survive 6-10 grunt hits at full build and 3-5 late, and a miniboss slam should cost 25-40% of max HP so that three mistakes kill you. Shardfall's own numbers already sit here at the top of the run — 100 HP vs crawler 8 damage is 12.5 hits (slightly generous, appropriate for a tutorial biome) and vs smith 24 damage is 4.2 hits.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Add a hard ONE-SHOT GUARD alongside the depth curve: no single non-boss hit may exceed 45% of the player's max HP. Without it, the ^1.10 damage curve puts a smith at 125 damage against a robe-build's ~112 max HP at the bottom of the world, i.e. a literal one-shot with no counterplay. Hades solves the same problem with Death Defiance rather than a cap; a cap is cheaper in a single file. Implement it in hurtPlayer after armour and before P.hp -= dmg: `if(!isBossHit) dmg = Math.min(dmg, P.maxhp*0.45)`. Second: your minibosses need PHASES, not just HP — the sourced guidance is explicit that a long single-behaviour TTK reads as a slog, and phase changes are what convert a 30-second fight into three 10-second fights.

*Source: https://www.tumblr.com/askagamedev/184716817936/enemy-design-tips-time-to-kill ; targets themselves are my proposal.*

#### Effective HP — one comparable number for every defensive option

**Numbers.** EHP = HP x 1/(1-DR) x 1/(1-p) x (1 + lambda*I) x 1/(1 - H/D). Shardfall i-frames at I=0.7 are worth x1.35 (single enemy) to x4.50 (5 attacks/sec swarm). Blink 0.25s/4s cd: x1.067 passive, unbounded reactive. Regrowth +2 HP/s against ~27 effective DPS = x1.08. Gravedigger 10% leech at 400 output DPS = 40 HP/s > incoming -> unkillable.

To compare flat armour, percentage reduction, i-frames, dodge and healing on one axis, convert everything into a multiplier on raw HP. EHP = HP / (fraction of raw damage that actually lands). Fraction = (mitigation term) x (avoidance term) x (i-frame term), and healing is a separate rate correction. (1) MITIGATION. Ratio models: multiplier = 1/(1 - DR). Flat subtraction: multiplier = d / max(floor, d - A) — this is the term with the pole. Multiple percentage sources: D3 stacks them multiplicatively, so 1/((1-DR1)(1-DR2)...); PoE stacks armour additively with endurance charges then multiplies against Fortify. (2) AVOIDANCE. A p chance to avoid gives multiplier 1/(1-p). (3) I-FRAMES — the one people forget and the one that dominates Shardfall. If attacks arrive at rate lambda per second and each landed hit grants I seconds of invulnerability, the rate of landed hits is 1/(I + 1/lambda), so the EHP multiplier versus no i-frames is exactly (1 + lambda x I). This is the crucial result: i-frame value scales with INCOMING ATTACK DENSITY, which means i-frames are a swarm defence, mathematically the same shape as hit-size-aware armour. At Shardfall's I = 0.7: lambda = 0.5 (one slow enemy) -> x1.35; lambda = 1 -> x1.70; lambda = 2 -> x2.40; lambda = 3 (a pack) -> x3.10; lambda = 5 (an abyss swarm) -> x4.50. (4) ACTIVE DODGE. A dodge with i-frame window w on cooldown T has passive uptime u = w/T and gives 1/(1 - u q), where q is the fraction of incoming damage timed into those windows (q = 1 for perfect play, q = u for random use). Shardfall's Blink at w=0.25, T=4: passive u = 6.25% -> x1.067; but used reactively on a telegraphed miniboss slam (q = 1 on the hits that matter) it removes that hit entirely. Report BOTH numbers on the item, never the average. (5) HEALING/LEECH. If sustained healing is H HP/s against an incoming stream of D HP/s, EHP_effective = HP x 1/(1 - H/D) for H < D, and is INFINITE for H >= D. This is why every shipped game rate-limits leech: PoE caps total life-leech recovery at 20% of maximum life per second no matter how many leech instances are active.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Build this as a single `ehpMult()` debug readout in the run HUD so every defensive gem, affix and unique can be priced on one number. Then set an explicit budget: total EHP multiplier from ALL defensive sources should not exceed about 8x-10x over the run, matching the enemy damage curve's 5.2x plus a little headroom. Right now the reachable total is: armour (unbounded, see above) x ironskin 0.8 (x1.25) x block 0.3 (x3.33 while blocking) x i-frames (x1.35-4.5) x warding x leech (unbounded). Fix leech first — it is the second unbounded term after armour. Cap it the PoE way: total heal from leech per second <= 12% of max HP, in CONFIG as LEECH_CAP=0.12.

*Source: https://maxroll.gg/d3/resources/damage-reduction-explained (multiplicative DR stacking) ; https://www.poewiki.net/wiki/Leech (20%/s cap) ; https://www.pathofexile.com/forum/view-thread/1468738 (PoE additive-then-multiplicative DR block). The i-frame rate identity (1 + lambda*I) is my derivation.*

#### The multiplicative-soup problem and how shipped games contain it

**Numbers.** PoE: max 5 supports per skill; res cap 75% (90% with max-res mods) against a -60% endgame baseline; phys DR cap 90%; leech cap 20% max life/sec. D4: crit x1.50 and vulnerable x1.20 baselines multiplicative, everything above baseline additive; attack speed capped at 200% (two 100% caps); enemy damage reduction ~80% at level 70. Dead Cells: HP caps 12.375x / 7.25x / 22x. D3: armor/(armor+3500), asymptotic to 100%.

Soup is what happens when N independent multiplicative pools all reach the player, because build power becomes PROD of pools and the gap between the best and median build grows exponentially in N. Shipped containment strategies, with numbers: (1) QUANTITY LIMIT ON THE MULTIPLICATIVE SOURCE — PoE gates 'more' behind support gems and caps you at a 6-link, so at most 5 more-multipliers per skill, and each is PAID FOR with a mana multiplier and often an explicit 'less damage' clause (GMP gives 4 extra projectiles at a less-projectile-damage cost; Greater Spell Echo gives 2 repeats at +30% more per repeat plus a cost multiplier). (2) DEMOTE MULTIPLICATIVE TO ADDITIVE AND KEEP ONLY A FIXED BASELINE — Diablo 4's Season 2/4 rework: Critical Strike and Vulnerable used to be separate fully-multiplicative buckets; they now have a fixed multiplicative baseline (Critical Strike x1.50, Vulnerable x1.20, Overpower +15% additive base) and ALL FURTHER Critical Strike Damage / Vulnerable Damage / Overpower Damage from gear is ADDITIVE into the general pool. Blizzard's stated reason: those affixes 'had resulted in more significant damage increases than stacking other affixes, making itemization too simple.' (3) HARD CAPS EVERYWHERE — PoE: resistances cap at 75% (raisable to 90%), physical damage reduction caps at 90%, leech caps at 20% max life/sec, and the game aggressively imposes NEGATIVE baselines to keep the cap meaningful (-30% all resistances after Act 5, another -30% after Act 10, so endgame players start at -60% and must claw back 135 points of resistance). (4) CONCAVE-THEN-CAPPED CURVES — Dead Cells caps every HP multiplier at a fixed value past a stat threshold (Brutality 12.375x at 34, Tactics 7.25x at 24, Survival 22x at 59) while leaving DPS purely exponential at 1.15^(s-1) — offence uncapped, defence capped. (5) DIMINISHING BY CONSTRUCTION — D3's Armor/(Armor+3500) and Resistance/(Resistance+350) can never reach 100% no matter how much you stack, so the designers do not need an explicit cap at all.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Count Shardfall's current multiplicative pools honestly: PROD(a.more) from supports AND uniques, crit expectation E, CRIT_ST 1.5 for ailment builds, elite modifier dmg, threat tier, and 'more' multipliers granted by uniques (Judgment x2.4, Bulwark x1.6, Worldbreaker x1.2). That is 6. My simulation of a fully-stacked build reaches ~2300 DPS at the bottom of the world versus ~64 DPS for an unbuilt one — a 36:1 spread, which no enemy HP curve can serve simultaneously. Two concrete controls: (a) SOCKET-LIMIT the multiplicative pool the way PoE does — total support gems that touch `a.more` across all gear capped at 5, which your socket counts already nearly enforce except that Runed Robe 3 + Second Skin +2 + Ghostweave +3 breaks it; cap total sockets at 6 in CONFIG. (b) PRICE the multipliers — every `a.more *= x` above 1.0 should carry a cost, mirroring PoE's mana multiplier. Shardfall already has Focus and fuel; make support gems raise attack cooldown or Focus cost. Note your uniques already do this correctly (Judgment x2.4 more but cd x1.9; Hornet's Call +2 arrows but more x0.65) — extend the same discipline to support gems.

*Source: https://mobalytics.gg/diablo-4/guides/damage-buckets-deep-dive ; https://maxroll.gg/d4/resources/in-depth-damage-guide ; https://www.poewiki.net/wiki/Leech ; http://www.vhpg.com/poe-resistance-overcap/ ; https://deadcells.wiki.gg/wiki/Stats ; https://maxroll.gg/d3/resources/damage-reduction-explained*

#### Many-small-hits vs one-big-hit — direct model comparison

**Numbers.** 100 raw damage, delivered as 10x10 vs 1x100. Flat A=8: 20 taken vs 92 taken (10:1). Percentage 50%: 50 vs 50 (1:1). PoE A=1000 k=10: 9 vs 50 (5.5:1). D3 A=3500: 50 vs 50 (1:1). Shardfall proposed k=1.5, A=12: 10 hits of 10 -> 10 x 5.7 = 57; 1 hit of 100 -> 92. Ratio 1.6:1 — deliberately gentler than PoE because Shardfall's 0.7s i-frames already deliver the swarm defence.

Take 100 total incoming damage delivered two ways: as 10 hits of 10, or as 1 hit of 100. FLAT SUBTRACTION (A=8, floor 1): small hits take max(1, 2) = 2 each = 20 total, an 80% reduction; the big hit takes 92, an 8% reduction. Ratio of effectiveness 10:1, and it is a step function — at A=9 the small hits become 10 total (90% reduction), at A=10 they become 10 x 1 = 10 and stay there forever. FLAT PERCENTAGE (50%): 50 vs 50. Completely hit-size-blind — a 50% DR aura is worth exactly as much against a swarm as against a slam. D3 RATIO (armor/(armor+3500)): also hit-size-blind, identical in both cases; D3's differentiation between defensive builds comes from resistance TYPE and from separate multiplicative DR sources, not from hit size. POE RATIO (A=1000, k=10): against 10-damage hits DR = 1000/(1000+100) = 91% -> 9 total; against the 100-damage hit DR = 1000/(1000+1000) = 50% -> 50. Ratio 5.5:1, smooth, and it never reaches 100%.
