So PoE's shape gives you the SAME design identity as flat subtraction with about half the extremity and none of the discontinuity. This is the whole argument for switching. Worth noting what the flat model did historically: Diablo 2's 'Damage Reduced by X' integer affix has NO cap, and stacking it to 30+ made characters functionally immune to low-damage physical attacks — the exact failure mode Shardfall is walking into, in a game where enemy base damage starts at 6.

**Apply to Shardfall.** This is the finding that should settle the k value. Because Shardfall's i-frames ALREADY give x1.35-x4.50 EHP scaling with attack density, armour should NOT also be steeply hit-size-aware or the two compound into immunity. That argues for a shallower k than PoE's. If you take Option 2, k = 1.5 gives a 1.6:1 swarm-vs-slam ratio, which reads as 'armour helps more against chip' without doubling up on the i-frame effect. If you want armour to be strongly anti-swarm, the correct move is the opposite one: SHORTEN i-frames (0.7 -> 0.4, which drops the swarm multiplier from x4.50 to x3.00 at lambda=5) and raise k, so the two systems are not both solving the same problem.

*Source: Comparison is my calculation; the model constants are from the sources cited in the armour-formula finding; the D2 flat-DR precedent from https://maxroll.gg/d2/resources/damage-reductions*

#### Elite modifiers and threat tiers — where they multiply in

**Numbers.** Armored elite smith, abyss, threat V. Current: 2021 HP, 129 dmg, 31 armour. With hpMul^1.75 unchanged elite mults: 6228 HP (20s TTK at 306 DPS). With elite hp mult rebased 3.0 -> 1.8: 3737 HP (12s). With 3.0 -> 1.5: 3114 HP (10s).

Shardfall's elite modifiers multiply on top of depthMul (the 'armored' elite is hp x3.0, dmg x1.2, spd x0.8, arm +9), and threat tiers add further flat armour (+6 at II and V) and speed (x1.25 at III and V). Under the current linear curve an armored elite smith in the abyss at threat V has HP 150 x 4.49 x 3.0 = 2021 and armour 16 + 9 + 6 = 31 with damage 24 x 4.49 x 1.2 = 129. Under the proposed split curve the same enemy has HP 150 x 13.84 x 3.0 = 6228 and damage 24 x 5.22 x 1.2 = 150. That HP number is too large — 6228 HP against a median 306 DPS build is 20 seconds for a single elite grunt, which busts the sourced TTK guidance (elite target 3-5s, and 'six enemies at 30 seconds each is a 3-minute encounter'). Elite HP multipliers must therefore be reduced when the base curve steepens: they were sized against a 4.5x cap and cannot survive a 13.8x one unchanged.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Rebase every elite HP multiplier down by roughly the square root of the curve steepening: the abyss HP mult goes from 4.49 to 13.84, a factor of 3.08, so divide elite HP mults by about 1.75 (sqrt 3.08). armored 3.0 -> 1.7, and check the others the same way. Elite DAMAGE multipliers can stay as they are, since dmgMul only rose from 4.49 to 5.22. Alternatively — and this is the Hades lesson — convert elite HP multipliers into extra ARMOUR and extra MECHANICS instead of raw HP, because 6000 HP is a slog while 3000 HP plus a shield phase is a fight.

*Source: Derived from /workspaces/Sweet-Spot/shardfall/index.html ELITE_MODIFIERS (line 539-542) and THREATS (line 828-840). My calculation.*

#### Threat tier II ('Everything down here has armor') under the current model

**Numbers.** Shieldman arm 12 + threat II arm 6 = 18. Per-hit output after armour: bow 8 -> 1 (87.5% loss), sword 10 -> 1, wand 7 -> 1, crossbow 20 -> 2, greataxe 30 -> 12 (60% loss). Hornet's Call: 3 arrows x 5.2 -> 3 x 1 = 3 total vs base bow 1 x 8 -> 1. With the proposed ratio model at k=1.5: bow 8 vs A=18 -> DR = 18/(18+12) = 60%, damage 3.2; greataxe 30 vs A=18 -> DR = 18/(18+45) = 29%, damage 21.4. Still favours big hits, by 2:1 rather than 12:1.

Threat tier II grants every enemy +6 armour, and tier V grants the same. Under Shardfall's flat model this is applied to ENEMY armour, which sits in the same max(1, dmg - armor) function. The failure is symmetric to the player's: a low-per-hit, high-rate build gets deleted by flat enemy armour while a slow heavy build barely notices. Bow base damage 8 versus a shieldman's 12 armour plus 6 threat armour = 18: every arrow does 1. Sword base 10 versus the same 18: 1. Greataxe 30: 12. Crossbow 20: 2. And Hornet's Call (+2 arrows, more x0.65) makes it strictly worse — 8 x 0.65 = 5.2 per arrow, three arrows, all reduced to 1 each. A unique designed to be a damage upgrade becomes a 62% damage DOWNGRADE the moment threat II is on. This is not a scaling problem; it is a build-invalidation problem, and it happens at the very first threat tier the player can unlock.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Whichever mitigation model you pick for the player, apply the SAME model to enemies — the asymmetry is what makes this invisible in playtesting until someone brings a multishot build to threat II. With the k=1.5 ratio model, threat II's +6 armour becomes a meaningful but survivable 20-40% damage cut for fast builds instead of a 90% one. If you keep flat subtraction, threat II must instead grant PERCENTAGE reduction (say 25%) rather than +6 flat, or it will keep invalidating exactly the archetypes you built multishot, Widow's Kiss and Hornet's Call to support.

*Source: Derived from /workspaces/Sweet-Spot/shardfall/index.html THREATS (line 833), ENEMIES shieldman/smith arm values, GEAR damage values, UNIQUES bow. My calculation.*

#### Ailments and damage-over-time interacting with armour

**Numbers.** Widow's Kiss bleed at stR 0.5 on a 10-damage sword = 5/tick. vs armour 18: flat model -> 1 (80% loss, and 100% loss vs 12-armour brute-tier enemies too). Ratio model k=1.5 -> 1.47. Armour-exempt -> 5. Ashfall burn 0.6 on a 7-damage wand = 4.2/tick; same three outcomes: 1 / 1.3 / 4.2.

Shardfall applies burn/bleed as separate damage instances (STACK_MAX=3, stR ratios like Widow's Kiss bleed 0.5, Ashfall burn 0.6). A per-tick DoT is by definition many-small-hits, so under flat subtraction ANY enemy armour makes DoT worthless — a bleed tick of 0.5 x 10 = 5 damage against 18 armour is 1. Under the ratio model it is 5 x (1 - 18/(18+7.5)) = 1.47, still weak but proportionate. Shipped games handle this by exempting DoT from mitigation entirely (PoE's damage-over-time is not affected by armour at all — armour only applies to HITS) or by pooling ticks. This is the single cleanest fix available and it costs one boolean.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Make burn/bleed bypass armour entirely (pass noArmor=true, the flag hurtPlayer already has, and add the mirror on hurtEnemy). This immediately fixes ailment builds against threat II, gives armour a clean identity ('armour stops hits, status resist stops ailments' — and you already have an `sres` stat for the other half), and makes CRIT_ST 1.5 meaningful. It also gives the Warding aura (+50% status resist, -10% damage) a real job.

*Source: PoE precedent: armour applies to hits only, not damage over time — https://pathofexile.fandom.com/wiki/Receiving_damage . Shardfall values from index.html UNIQUES and STACK_MAX (line 242). Calculation mine.*

#### Shard economy and reward scaling under a steeper curve

**Numbers.** Current max shard multiplier: 4.49 (depth) x 2.90 (threat V) x 1.3 (scav) = 16.9x. Under proposed split: enemy HP 13.84x but shards still 4.49x -> shards per unit of effort falls to 32% of the surface rate. To hold it flat, shard multiplier should track hpMul: use Math.pow(depthK(y), 1.75) for the shard term too, or a compromise ^1.4 (7.6x) if you want a mild diving premium without runaway economy.

Shard drops currently use depthMul directly (line 1672: total = n x greed x depthMul(y) x threat.shard), so shards scale 4.49x at the bottom and threat tiers add 1.00-2.90x on top, for a maximum of about 13x the surface rate. RoR2 does the same thing deliberately — reward = coeff x monsterValue x rewardMultiplier, using the SAME coefficient that scales the monsters — which keeps the risk/reward slope flat. If you raise enemy HP to a 13.84x curve while leaving rewards on the 4.49x curve, the shards-per-second rate collapses at depth and the meta-progression loop inverts: farming the caves becomes strictly better than diving. Keep the reward curve tied to the same base term you use for enemy HP, or the run-length target of 8-12 minutes will not hold because players will stop diving.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Use ^1.4 on the shard term rather than ^1.75. That gives 7.6x at the bottom against 13.84x HP, so shards-per-second at depth is 55% of the surface rate per unit of HP destroyed — but because deep enemies are worth 6-9 base shards versus the crawler's 2, the actual per-kill yield still rises steeply. Verify by simulating shards-per-minute at each biome and targeting a flat-to-slightly-rising curve.

*Source: https://riskofrain2.wiki.gg/wiki/Difficulty (reward = coeff x monsterValue x rewardMultiplier). Shardfall line 1672. Calculation mine.*

#### Fall damage and non-combat damage sources under the new curve

**Numbers.** FALL_DMG 0.11/px/s over 520. At 1000 px/s: 53 damage = 53% of 100 HP, 15% of 350 HP. Hades comparison: traps +400% at 1 Heat. Thorns 12 x depthMul = 54 at the bottom under the current curve, and would be 166 under hpMul^1.75 or 63 under dmgMul^1.10 — these are PLAYER damage sources and must use the HP curve, not the damage curve.

FALL_DMG = 0.11 HP per px/s over FALL_SAFE = 520, and it does NOT scale with depth. As max HP grows over the run (base 100 + plate 80 + Mountain 140 + hp affixes), fall damage becomes proportionally trivial: a 1000 px/s impact is 0.11 x 480 = 53 damage, which is 53% of a 100 HP starting character and 15% of a 350 HP late character. Meanwhile Hades' Heightened Security condition takes the opposite approach — traps and magma deal +400% damage — precisely because environmental hazards that do not scale stop teaching anything. Same issue applies to Thorns (flat 12 x depthMul), the dodge-blast (18 x depthMul) and any other hard-coded constant that currently rides depthMul.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Make fall damage proportional rather than flat: `dmg = P.maxhp * 0.14 * (speed - FALL_SAFE)/480` so a hard landing always costs about 14% of max HP regardless of build. And when you split depthMul, grep every one of the 12 call sites and classify each as player-damage (use hpMul) or enemy-damage (use dmgMul) — thorns at line 1702/1810/1840 and dodgeBlast at 2077 are player damage and will silently become useless if they inherit the damage curve.

*Source: https://www.rpgsite.net/feature/10287-hades-pact-of-punishment-heat-modifiers-and-how-to-maximize-your-rewards (Heightened Security +400%). Shardfall lines 230-231, 1702, 1810, 1840, 2077. Calculation mine.*

### Recommendations

- FIX ARMOUR FIRST — it is the only unbounded term in the whole game. Minimum change: `function applyArmor(raw,armor){return Math.max(raw*0.25, Math.max(ARMOR_FLOOR, raw-(armor||0)))}` at index.html:1479. This caps the EHP multiplier from armour at 4x, removes the pole, requires zero retuning of item values, and matches the fact that every shipped game with subtractive or percentage mitigation has a ceiling (PoE 90% phys DR, D2 50% %DR; D2's UNCAPPED flat 'Damage Reduced by X' is the known counterexample). Add `const ARMOR_MAX_FRAC=0.75` to CONFIG next to ARMOR_FLOOR.
- If you want the better curve rather than the cheap fix, replace subtraction with `DR = Math.min(0.75, A/(A + 1.5*d))` — PoE's shape at k=1.5 instead of k=10, rescaled because Shardfall's armour and damage numbers live on the same scale. Full table is in the findings. This requires rebasing armour values down (vest 2->3, chain 6->8, plate 12->14, affix arm range 2-9 -> 2-6) so a committed tank lands at A=45-55.
- SPLIT THE DEPTH CURVE INTO TWO. Keep `1 + depth/900` as the base and raise it to different exponents: enemy HP ^1.75, enemy damage ^1.10. At the bottom of the world (depth 3140) that is HP x13.84 and damage x5.22, versus x4.49 on both today. The 1.75:1.10 ratio is 1.59:1, deliberately close to Risk of Rain 2's published +30% HP / +20% damage per level. Per-biome multipliers: surface 1.02/1.01, caves 1.75/1.42, fungal 3.17/2.07, ruins 5.73/3.00, forge 9.41/4.09, abyss 13.84/5.22.
- Implement as three functions so the 12 existing call sites can be classified: `depthK(y)` = the raw 1+depth/900 term, `hpMul(y)` = depthK^1.75, `dmgMul(y)` = depthK^1.10. Enemy spawn HP (lines 1641, 1726, 1756) uses hpMul; enemy contact/projectile/burn damage (1791, 1793) uses dmgMul; PLAYER damage sources — thorns at 1702/1810/1840 and dodgeBlast at 2077 — must use hpMul, not dmgMul, or they silently fall off. Shard drops (1672) use depthK^1.4.
- ADD A ONE-SHOT GUARD: no single non-boss hit may exceed 45% of max HP. Without it the ^1.10 damage curve puts a smith at 125 damage in the abyss against a robe build's ~112 max HP. One line in hurtPlayer before `P.hp -= dmg`. Hades solves the same problem with Death Defiance; a cap is cheaper in a single file.
- REBASE ELITE HP MULTIPLIERS by about sqrt(3.08) = 1.75x downward, because they were sized against a 4.49x cap and the new one is 13.84x. armored 3.0 -> 1.7 and so on. An armored elite smith at threat V in the abyss would otherwise be 6228 HP = 20 seconds of TTK for a median build, which busts the 3-5s elite target. Elite DAMAGE multipliers can stay unchanged.
- MAKE BURN AND BLEED BYPASS ARMOUR ENTIRELY (PoE precedent: armour applies to hits only, never to damage over time). One boolean. This fixes ailment builds against threat II, gives armour and status-resist clean non-overlapping identities, and makes CRIT_ST=1.5 and the Warding aura meaningful. Right now a Widow's Kiss bleed tick of 5 does exactly 1 against any armoured enemy.
- CAP LEECH AS A RATE, not a percentage of hit. `const LEECH_CAP=0.12` — total healing from leech per second cannot exceed 12% of max HP. PoE caps total life-leech recovery at 20% of max life per second no matter how many instances are active. Gravedigger's 10% leech on a 400 DPS build is 40 HP/s against ~27 effective incoming DPS, i.e. currently unkillable, and it gets worse as the offensive curve steepens.
- CAP THE MULTIPLICATIVE POOL. Total sockets that can hold a `more`-touching support gem should be capped at 5-6 across all gear (Runed Robe 3 + Second Skin +2 + Ghostweave +3 currently breaks this), mirroring PoE's 6-link. And PRICE every `a.more` above 1.0 with a cooldown or Focus cost, the way PoE charges a mana multiplier and the way your own uniques already do (Judgment x2.4 more but cd x1.9, Hornet's Call +2 arrows but more x0.65).
- ENFORCE A POOL DISCIPLINE RULE IN CONFIG: only SUPPORT gems may write to `a.more`. Attunement level-up picks, affixes and meta-tree nodes feed `inc` only. Today `keen`, `cruel` and meta node `m5` all feed crit, which is a third multiplicative pool wearing an additive costume. Diablo 4's Season 2/4 rework did exactly this: crit and vulnerable kept a fixed multiplicative baseline (x1.50 and x1.20) and everything above baseline became additive, because those affixes 'had resulted in more significant damage increases than stacking other affixes, making itemization too simple.'
- RETUNE CRIT TO ITS OWN OPTIMUM. Your affix exchange rate is roughly r = 35/9 = 3.9 crit-multi points per crit-chance point, so the optimal build sits at (m-1) = 3.9c. A committed build reaches c=0.61, giving an ideal m of 3.44; the current unique/meta stack reaches 3.70, so Deadeye is slightly over-weighted. Drop it from +0.9 crit multi to +0.7 and the crit axis lands exactly on its own optimum, with E = 2.49 instead of 2.65.
- BUILD AN `ehpMult()` DEBUG READOUT that folds armour, ironskin, block, i-frames and leech into a single number using EHP = HP x 1/(1-DR) x 1/(1-p) x (1 + lambda*I) x 1/(1 - H/D). Set an explicit budget of 8x-10x total across the run, matching the 5.22x enemy damage curve plus headroom. Without one number you cannot price a defensive gem against an offensive one, and the i-frame term in particular (worth x1.35 against one enemy and x4.50 against a 5-attacks/sec swarm) is currently invisible in every balance conversation.
- CONSIDER SHORTENING I-FRAMES FROM 0.7s TO 0.5s if you keep armour anti-swarm. The (1 + lambda*I) identity means 0.7s i-frames already deliver x3.10 EHP against a 3-attack/sec pack and x4.50 against a 5-attack/sec swarm — the exact job flat armour claims to do. Two systems solving the same problem is why the current model reaches immunity. Either shorten i-frames or flatten armour's hit-size sensitivity; do not leave both steep.
- MAKE FALL DAMAGE PROPORTIONAL: `dmg = P.maxhp * 0.14 * (speed - FALL_SAFE)/480` instead of the flat 0.11/px/s. A 1000 px/s landing currently costs 53% of a starting character's HP and 15% of a late one's, so the mechanic stops teaching. Hades goes the other way with Heightened Security (+400% trap and magma damage at 1 Heat) for the same reason.
- KEEP THE THREATS TABLE STRUCTURAL, NOT NUMERIC. Hades' entire 32-Heat ladder only inflates enemy stats by +100% damage and +30% HP — everything else is fewer boon choices, less healing, more bodies, new boss moves, a clock. Your named-rule tiers are already the right design; resist adding a 'threat V: enemies have 2.5x HP' tier. Do consider giving The Weight discrete published tiers the way Dead Cells' Malaise does (10 tiers x 50 points, +20%/+40%/+60% enemy strength at tiers 1-2/3/4-5, gained at 1.3 pts/sec, removed 250 per boss kill) rather than a smooth ramp — legible thresholds are why Malaise reads as a mechanic instead of as the game cheating.
- VALIDATE WITH A TTK HARNESS BEFORE SHIPPING THE CURVE. My simulation of a median build (base 30 DPS growing to ~306 via +140% increased, four x1.30 more-multipliers and crit E rising to 1.49) holds crawler TTK at 0.8/1.1/1.3/1.3/1.3/1.1 s and smith TTK at 4.8/7.1/8.1/8.2/7.8/7.0 s across depths 0/600/1200/1800/2400/3200 under the proposed curve. Under the current linear curve that same build sees smith TTK FALL from 4.8s to 2.2s. Targets to hold against: trash 2-4 hits under 1.5s, elite 6-9 hits and 3-5s, miniboss 25-45 hits and 15-30s with at least one phase change, boss 45-90s and never past 2 minutes.

### Caveats and unsourced claims

- The 36:1 DPS spread between a fully-stacked build and an unbuilt one is a bigger problem than the depth curve, and no single enemy HP curve can serve both. My simulation puts a stacked build at ~2300 DPS and a weak one at ~64 DPS at the bottom of the world. Steepening enemy HP to ^1.75 makes the run correct for a median build and unplayable for a weak one (smith TTK 33 seconds). The exponent fix and the multiplicative-pool cap MUST ship together or you will trade one broken end of the distribution for the other.
- Switching from flat subtraction to a ratio model changes the FEEL of every armour item in the game and invalidates the DESIGN-PLAN §1.4 comment at index.html:1687 that explicitly justifies flat subtraction as a Vanguard/Marksman separator. The Option 1 bounded-floor fix preserves that comment's intent; Option 2 does not, and would need the design doc updated. Pick deliberately.
- Raising enemy HP 13.84x while leaving the shard formula on 4.49x inverts the meta-progression loop — farming the caves becomes strictly better than diving, and the 8-12 minute run target collapses because players stop going down. The shard term must move with the HP term (I suggest ^1.4).
- I could not find a primary GGG source stating the DESIGN reasoning behind the increased/more split, only Mark_GGG's mechanical explanation. The 'more multipliers are gated behind support gems to control power creep' argument is my inference from the game's structure, not a sourced dev quote — treat it as a strong hypothesis, not a citation.
- Exact 'less damage' percentages on PoE support gems (GMP, Spell Echo) have been retuned repeatedly across leagues; I have cited them qualitatively rather than numerically. If you want to copy specific costs, pull current values from poedb rather than trusting a number from memory.
- Slay the Spire's per-ascension HP multipliers are not published anywhere I could find — the wikis describe them qualitatively only ('normal enemies have more HP' at A7, elites at A8, bosses at A9). Do not cite specific percentages for it. Hades and Risk of Rain 2 are the well-documented difficulty-ladder references.
- The proposed one-shot guard (45% of max HP) interacts badly with intentional boss slams. Tag boss/miniboss telegraphed attacks as exempt or the guard will neuter exactly the attacks minibosses need to threaten with, and you will have removed the reason to learn the telegraph.
- Making DoT bypass armour buffs every ailment build in the game simultaneously — Widow's Kiss, Ashfall, ignite, bloodscent, rupture, CRIT_ST 1.5. Expect ailment builds to jump a tier and plan a compensating pass on ailment ratios (Widow's Kiss bleed 0.5, Ashfall burn 0.6) rather than shipping the bypass alone.


<a id="enemies"></a>
## Enemy and boss design

### Summary

Audit of Shardfall's enemy/boss tables against researched telegraph, taxonomy, budget and affix numbers. Four structural problems: windups too fast (Threat III's wind x0.8 pushes 11 of 14 grunts below human reaction time); no recovery state, so tell-punish-reset is only two-thirds built; lunge makes telegraphed range a lie by 1.9x-3.6x; boss phases do nothing because e.pat is written at index.html:1756 and read nowhere. Anchors: simple visual RT 230-250ms, choice RT +150-200ms, designers budget 0.25-0.30s, Tekken's practical limit 20 frames (333ms) with 23f+ (383ms) truly reactable, Hollow Knight's Hornet at 30fps uses 15f (0.50s) and 20f (0.667s) anticipations with 0.33-1.0s recoveries. Shardfall's stalker at 0.22s (0.176s at Threat III) is below all of them. Findings include a full retuned windup/active/recovery table, a 9-role taxonomy, an RoR2-derived credit budget, and a 6-biome roster plan with introduction order.

### Findings (20)

#### Reaction-time floor

**Numbers.** Simple RT mean 230-250ms (band 200-300ms), elite floor 100-120ms. Choice RT = simple +150-200ms (380-450ms). Designer budgets 0.25s (GDKeys, 15f at 60fps) to 0.3s (note.com).

A pack turns a simple-RT problem into a choice-RT problem, so a windup fair in a duel is unfair in a group. GDKeys formula: Anticipation = reaction time + ability trigger time + difficulty buffer.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Floor 0.36s single-target (250ms RT + 60ms to identify which animation started + 50ms input lead-in), 0.45s for groups of 3+. Eight of 14 grunts are below 0.36 today: stalker .22, voidling .24, ember .26, bat .28, rockling .30, sporeling .30, wraith .30, voidspawn .32. Three more (crawler/spitter .34, archer melee .36) sit on the RT mean, which is a coin flip.

*Source: Reaction-time literature aggregates; GDKeys 'Anatomy of an Attack'; note.com/darkangels_417. High confidence.*

#### Fighting-game reactability threshold

**Numbers.** Tekken: 16f (267ms) unreactable/must predict; 20f (333ms) 'pretty much the limit'; 22-23f borderline; 23f+ (383ms) truly reactable.

Frames alone do not determine reactability: a 20-frame move is unreactable if its animation flows seamlessly from idle, reactable if the silhouette changes distinctly. Reactability = frames x animation distinctiveness.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Every enemy shares one windup treatment ('swell + strobe', index.html:3028), so the player sees THAT something winds up but not WHAT. Per-role silhouettes buy back ~60ms of identification cost, exactly the gap between 0.36 and 0.42.

*Source: Tekken 8 Steam 'REACTABLE'; GameFAQs Tekken 7 thread. High as consensus, medium as transferable.*

#### Hollow Knight Hornet frame data (closest genre match)

**Numbers.** Hornet at 30fps: dash 15f/10f/10f = 0.50s anticipation, 0.33s attack, 0.33s recovery. Throw 20f (0.667s)/20f+15f/10f. Lasso 20f/30f (1.0s)/~1.0s. Heal 45f = 1.5s. Mantis Lords: 3 attacks, 210 HP then 160+160 (530 total), mechanic-gated.

Nothing in Hornet's kit anticipates faster than 0.50s; fastest recovery 0.33s. Mantis Lords gates on a MECHANIC: killing lord 1 spawns two more, so the player causes the transition.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall's fastest grunt windup (0.22s) is 44% of Hollow Knight's fastest BOSS anticipation. Retarget to 0.36-0.85s, mode 0.45-0.55. Active windows (0.12-0.24s) are fine inside the 0.30s dodge i-frames.

*Source: GDKeys (measured frame counts); Fextralife Mantis Lords. High.*

#### No recovery state: the punish window does not exist

**Numbers.** Guidance 0.25-1.0s post-attack hesitation; Hornet 0.33-1.0s; Shardfall 0s. Crawler's nominal gap is cd 1.5 - wind 0.34 - act 0.16 = 1.0s, 100% of it spent chasing.

When e.act expires the enemy falls straight through to normal movement; e.acd (set at windup start) is the only gate. The gap between attacks is a CHASE state, not a vulnerable one. Committing to an attack costs the enemy nothing.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Add a `rec` field and e.rec state between act expiring and cooldown resuming: velocity damped to ~0.1, e.dir LOCKED, incoming damage x1.30 in a distinct colour. Values 0.20 / 0.35 / 0.60-0.80 / 0.9-1.2s (skirmisher / standard / heavy / boss heavy). rec must exceed dodge duration 0.22s plus one swing so a punish always fits.

*Source: chaoticstupid.com 'Telegraphs 2: Post-attack vulnerability'; GDKeys; direct read of the upEnemies melee branch. Certain on the code.*

#### Lunge makes the telegraphed range a lie

**Numbers.** reach = range + lunge x act: crawler 54px (2.3x stated), bat 68 (2.6x), brute 64 (1.9x), rockling 93 (3.6x), stalker 79 (3.3x), ember 64 (2.9x), wraith 82 (2.9x), voidspawn 81 (3.1x), voidmaw 108 (2.7x). Screen ~272 world px, so rockling's threat radius is 34% of the visible screen while its tell sits at 1.6 tiles.

index.html:466 promises 'nothing damages you without a tell', but the tell shows POSITION while the strike lands at position + lunge x act.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Declare `reach` and DRAW it: a 2px ground streak (air streak for flyers) of exactly that length, fading in over the first 35% of the windup. One fillRect. Fallback: cap lunge x act at 0.5 x range.

*Source: Computed from ENEMIES (468-530) and upEnemies, where e.vx is set to the lunge value and not damped while lunging. Arithmetic certain.*

#### Threat III shortening windups is the least fair mechanic in the game

**Numbers.** wind x0.8 gives stalker 0.176, voidling 0.192, ember 0.208, bat 0.224, rockling/sporeling/wraith 0.24, voidspawn 0.256, crawler/spitter 0.272 versus an RT band of 200-300ms. Fair alternatives from Hades: Hard Labor +20% damage/rank (5 ranks), Jury Summons +20% enemies/rank (3), Calisthenics +15% HP/rank (2), Forced Overtime +20% speed/rank (2 ranks at 3 Heat each, priciest in the pact).

0.176s is below the lower bound of the normal RT band entirely. D3's auto-skip affixes and PoE's removed T17 mods share one property: they remove a player verb rather than adding a threat. Shortening a telegraph removes 'react'. Supergiant prices enemy SPEED at 3 Heat/rank versus 1 for damage.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Delete `wind` from THREATS. Threat III becomes cooldown x0.75, damage x1.20, +1 elite slot. If any scaling survives, clamp: wind = Math.max(0.36, base * (T.wind||1)). Threat III also carries spd 1.25 and `swift` is spd 1.7 — stacked 2.125x approach speed, and approach time is a hidden telegraph. Cap swift at 1.35; make speed and windup mods mutually exclusive.

*Source: index.html:830-838; Maxroll D3 affixes (Waller 'typically the worst', Juggernaut 'to the point of auto-skip', Shielding 'one of the worst'); PoE 3.35 T17 removals; Elden Ring input-reading criticism; RPGSite Hades Pact table. High.*

#### Enemy role taxonomy and what each teaches

**Numbers.** Level Design Book: 6 roles (Grunt, Squad, Leader, Tank, Swarm, Sniper) plus a Speed x Health x Range x DPS matrix. Skylanders: 4 (Near, Far, Swarmer, Heavy). L4D1 7 threats, L4D2 8. Doom Eternal '7+ types at a time'. DmC: 4 tiers 'kept evenly spread, higher groups smaller'.

L4D is the best functional taxonomy because each special is defined by what it does to your positioning: Smoker separates (built to punish teams that stay in tight groups), Boomer blinds and calls the horde, Hunter pins, Charger displaces, Spitter denies area. Roster properties: diversity, hierarchy, emergence.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Nine roles, each teaching one verb: RUSHER (the dodge), SKIRMISHER — fast, fragile, disengages (spacing), BRUISER — slow, huge tell, long recovery (the punish loop), BLOCKER (flanking), SNIPER (cover), AREA-DENIAL (read the arena), EXPLODER (kill order and kill distance), POPULATION (priority targeting), ANTI-AIR (flight costs something). Shardfall covers 6; skirmisher, area-denial and anti-air are absent and population is boss-only. Anti-air is the biggest miss because flight is a core verb with no counterweight.

*Source: book.leveldesignbook.com/process/combat/enemy; gamedesignskills.com; L4D wiki; Noclip 'Designing Demons'. High.*

#### How many roles before encounters feel varied

**Numbers.** L4D1 7, L4D2 8, Doom Eternal 7+ simultaneous. C(9,2)=36 role pairs, C(9,3)=84 triples. Halo: a 3-second loop inside a 30-second loop inside a 3-minute loop.

Griesemer's framing is recombination, not count: 'No 30 second stretch of Halo is ever repeated.' AI owns the 30-second decisions; designers own the 3-minute ones (when to send reinforcements, when to retreat). Past ~10 roles players stop being able to name what they see.

**Apply to Shardfall.** An 8-12 minute run is ~20 thirty-second loops, so you need ~20 distinguishable encounters. Nine roles supplies that; 14 near-identical stat blocks do not. Also add the 3-minute layer — spawnFromChunks has no pacing authority, so there is no rest-tension-rest rhythm.

*Source: Engadget 'Half-Minute Halo: An Interview with Jaime Griesemer' (2011). High on quotes, medium on '~9' as my synthesised target.*

#### Encounter budget: Risk of Rain 2's Director

**Numbers.** creditsPerSecond = creditMultiplier x (1 + 0.4 x coeff) x (players+1)/2, multiplier 0.3-2.0. Card costs 8 (Beetle) to 600+ (champion). Max 4-5 per wave. Intra-wave interval 0.1-1s, inter-wave 2.3-30s. Elite cost x6 (T1), x3.5 (Honor), x36 (T2); the Director picks the highest tier it can afford. T1 elite power: 4x HP, 2x damage. At 40+ monsters on map the spawn FAILS. Anti-trickle: a monster is 'too cheap' if the Director holds more than 6x the elite value AND the spawn is not instantaneous AND it is not the most expensive available.

The best-documented numeric spawn budget in the genre. The 6x elite cost is priced slightly above the 4x/2x power gain, keeping elites a treat rather than a tax.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Costs: crawler 8, bat 10, voidling 10, sporeling 12, stalker 16, rockling 18, spitter 18, ember 20, vent[new] 22, drone[new] 24, archer 26, voidspawn 30, shieldman 34, wraith 36, brute 40, herald[new] 60, smith 70. Budget B = 24 + depth_m x 0.055 x threat.dens (46 at 400m, 112 at 1600m, 200 at 3200m). Elite cost x2.5 (yours are only 1.2-3.0x HP / 1.1-1.4x damage, so 6x over-prices them), second tier x8. Replace EN.length>120 with 24 alive AND 6 on-screen. Steal the anti-trickle rule.

*Source: riskofrain2.wiki.gg Director/Directors and Monsters pages (datamined). High.*

#### The attack-token rule, from Shardfall's own dodge numbers

**Numbers.** dodge() at index.html:2084: dodgeT 0.22s, dodgeCd 0.55s, i-frames 0.30s (0.345 with the Cunning node) = 1.82 answered strikes/sec. Post-hit invuln 0.7s (line 1704) caps absorption at 1.43/sec. Mean enemy cooldown ~1.75s. Break-even = 1.82 x 1.75 = 3.2 attackers. At six in range: 3.43 strikes/sec incoming vs 1.82 answerable.

Above ~3 simultaneous attackers the game silently stops being a dodging game and becomes an i-frame-tanking game. This is also where simple RT becomes choice RT (+150-200ms), so the effective floor rises to ~0.45s exactly when enemies attack fastest.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Attack-token pool of 2 (3 in boss arenas, one reserved for the boss). Claim on entering windup, release at end of recovery; token-less enemies circle and block escape lanes, giving free crowd behaviour. Role caps on top: max 1 ranged per 4 on-screen (hard max 2), max 1 blocker, max 2 exploders never within 2x blast radius, max 1 summoner never with a splitter, max 1 elite until Threat IV.

*Source: Computed from index.html plus the choice-RT penalty from the reaction literature. Certain on the arithmetic.*

#### Boss gating is well-chosen but the transitions do nothing

**Numbers.** Hades Bone Hydra gates at 66% and 33%, spawning 3 then 6 heads, 3 attacks in phase 1. Hades: single 50% gate. Theseus/Asterius 9,000 and 14,000 HP, Theseus gains a move at 50%. Mantis Lords: mechanic gate, 210 then 160+160. Shardfall: 5 bosses x 2 gates = 10 slots from a shared pool of 5 patterns, 0 unique per boss; voidmaw's phase-3 pattern is 'spores', sporemother's signature.

66/33 matches Bone Hydra exactly, so the gating is sound. But bossPhase() sets e.pat at line 1756 and NOTHING reads it — the comment at 1763 claiming it 'colors ongoing behaviour' is false. Actual delta of a transition: one firework, one 0.9s invuln, shoot cd x0.75. Seconds later the fight is identical to phase 1.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Give e.pat a recurring patCd (4.5s in phase 2, 3.2s in phase 3). Give each boss 2 patterns nobody else has — the only place identity can live while all five share one melee+shoot skeleton. Gate ONE boss on a mechanic (sporemother: destroy 3 spore sacs). Attacks per phase 3/4/5, matching Bone Hydra's 3-in-phase-1 and Hades' Extreme Measures adding one pattern per rank; Shardfall has 2 in phase 1.

*Source: hadeswiki.com Bone Hydra; hades.fandom Hades/Combat; Fextralife Mantis Lords; grep of index.html for `.pat`. Certain on the dead code.*

#### Boss transition patterns break the game's own no-untelegraphed-damage rule

**Numbers.** bossPattern() at 1764-1780: 'volley' 14 projectiles in a 360-degree ring at 220px/s, no windup; 'slam' instant explode(x,y,150,dmg x0.8) — a 150px radius on a ~272px screen; 'firewall' 7 columns spanning 156px, no windup; 'spores' 10 arcing projectiles, no windup. All fire on the same frame as the 0.9s invuln.

The only defence against the slam is having already been outside 150px, which nothing gave the player a reason to do. Danmaku practice: 'if the visuals are flashy but the escape route cannot be read, it feels unreasonable' versus 'when players can see a path they can take, it feels fair even if it is difficult'. Patterns are 'not just a design of bullets, but also a design of gaps'.

**Apply to Shardfall.** 0.55-0.80s of telegraph per pattern, drawn as gap structure: volley shows 14 dim dots at spawn radius; slam draws a ground ring expanding to exactly 150px as damage lands; firewall draws the 7 footprints and deliberately drops one so a lane is always clear. Decouple: roar and invuln first, telegraph begins as the invuln ENDS.

*Source: Direct read of bossPattern/bossPhase; danmaku fairness principles. Certain on the code.*

#### Boss HP and damage scaling

**Numbers.** depthMul = 1 + depth/900; biome thresholds 400/900/1600/2400/3200. Effective boss HP: warden 605-840, sporemother 1,040-1,445, sentinel 1,946-2,569, forgelord 3,303-4,104, voidmaw 5,470+ — a 9x spread. Voidmaw damage 38 x 4.56 = 173 against ~180-220 fully-kitted player HP = 79-96% in one connect. Reference TTK: Hollow Knight ~30-90s, Hades ~1-3min, in an 8-12 minute run.

Boss HP is a constant times a depth multiplier with no relationship to actual player DPS. Voidmaw simultaneously has the fastest tells in the game (0.36 / 0.34) and the highest damage.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Set boss HP from a measured 60-90s TTK. Clamp single-hit damage in hurtPlayer: 0.40 x maxHP for grunts, 0.55 x maxHP for a telegraphed boss heavy. Let frequency carry difficulty. For deep bosses consider splitting HP across bodies (Mantis Lords 210 -> 160+160) so the fight gets chaotic rather than long.

*Source: Computed from ENEMIES, BIOMES (index.html:263) and depthMul. The TTK band is my characterisation (medium confidence); arithmetic certain.*

#### Elite affixes: what D3, PoE and Risk of Rain actually do

**Numbers.** D3 (Maxroll): Fast +40% move/+20% attack/+10% cast. Frozen 15yd, 6 orbs, 4s to explode, 2.5s freeze. Frozen Pulse 15yd, INERT 1.5s, pulses 10s, 60% slow. Arcane Enchanted 25yd beam, 2s delay, 10s. Poison Enchanted 4 pools, 2s trigger, 6s ground. Desecrator 6yd, 1s delay in/out, damage DOUBLES every 0.5s. Mortar 3yd direct / 11.5yd collateral. Molten 5yd/5s. Thunderstorm 12yd, 5 strikes. Waller 6s. Jailer 2.5s. Shielding 5s, one monster at a time. Fire Chains 10 ticks/s. Avenger +50% damage per member killed. Reflects Damage up to 15s. RoR2 T1: 4x HP, 2x damage; Blazing 50%-of-total burn; Overloading swaps 50% HP for shields recharging 7s after damage stops plus a 1.5s-fuse bomb for 50% damage in 4m; Glacial 80% slow.

Every FAIR affix has a delay before lethality (1.5s inert, 2s trigger, 2s delay, 1s in/out, 1.5s fuse). Every UNFAIR one removes a player verb — Maxroll's auto-skip tier is Waller (escape), Juggernaut (crowd control), Shielding (damage). PoE stripped 'Players cannot Regenerate' and 'Monsters Cannot be Leeched From' from T17 for the same reason.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Fixes: (1) `armored` +9 stacks with Threat II's +6 for 15 flat against max(1, dmg - armor), reducing multishot/fork/pierce/ailment builds to 1 damage per hit — build-bricking, exactly what PoE removed. Change to min(armor, 0.6 x hit) or a percentage. (2) `swift` x1.7 is a hidden telegraph shortener; cap 1.35. (3) `vampiric` heals 50% invisibly; add a drain tether. Then ADD affixes on the delay-before-lethality pattern: 'Cinder' (5s burning patch every 3s, 0.6s fade-in), 'Warded' (0.9s telegraphed immunity every 6s — readable Shielding), 'Riven' (1.2s death fuse then a 60px shockwave). Never ship anything that shortens a tell, blocks movement, or reflects damage.

*Source: maxroll.gg/d3/resources/elite-affixes (fetched, full list); riskofrain2.wiki.gg; PoE 3.35 T17 removals. High.*

#### Attack patterns that are hard but fair

**Numbers.** No published numeric gap thresholds exist — I checked Sparen's Danmaku Design Studio Guide A4 directly and it is explicitly qualitative, giving no counts, speeds or gap sizes. Firmest windows available: Sekiro deflect 12 frames (0.2s), frames 13-36 downgrade to block; Nine Sols precise parry 0.133s (~8f) with a 0.5s imprecise window at 50% effect.

Patterns are 'a design of gaps'; the fairness test is whether the escape route is readable. 'Even just displaying a thin warning line before the actual shot makes the game much more playable.' Colour-code by TYPE AND DANGER. Speed and density are one budget: faster bullets reduce readable density because the player must attend to a larger area to infer trajectory. The failure mode is variable timing — Elden Ring speeds the animation if you roll early and delays it if you roll late, so 'there's no possibility of dodging or blocking on reflex'.

**Apply to Shardfall.** (1) Guarantee gaps at spawn — sporemother's 5-projectile 0.55 spread and voidmaw's 4-projectile 0.7 spread should compute a gap for the 12px player plus 8px clearance rather than hoping for one. (2) Add a danger colour channel: projectile colour tracks the biome palette today, encoding flavour not threat; white core = fast/must-dodge, hollow core = slow/leadable. (3) Voidmaw fires 4 projectiles at 300px/s with 0.7 spread on a 1.2s cooldown into a 272px screen — drop the count to 3 or the speed to 220. (4) Never randomise wind; randomise WHICH attack, never WHEN.

*Source: note.com '15 Beautiful Bullet Hell Patterns'; sparen.github.io Guide A4; Fextralife Sekiro Deflection; ninesols.wiki.gg Parry; Elden Ring threads. High.*

#### Difficulty escalation that preserves readability: the Hades Pact model

**Numbers.** Hard Labor +20% damage/rank (5 ranks, 1 Heat); Lasting Consequences -25% healing/rank (4, 1); Jury Summons +20% enemies/rank (3, 1); Calisthenics +15% HP/rank (2, 1); Forced Overtime +20% speed/rank (2, 3 Heat each); Damage Control +1 shielded health/rank (2, 1); Middle Management +1 elite in miniboss fights/rank (2, 2); Benefits Package armored +1 perk/rank (2, 2-3); Heightened Security traps +400% (1, 1); Tight Deadline -2:00/rank (2, min 7:00); Extreme Measures 1 boss buffed per rank (3, 1-2 Heat), each rank ADDING A NEW ATTACK PATTERN.

Extreme Measures escalates a boss by adding a pattern, not by shrinking a tell or inflating a number. And the Heat pricing encodes Supergiant's own judgement: enemy SPEED costs 3 Heat/rank versus damage at 1, because speed compresses the reaction window and damage does not.

**Apply to Shardfall.** I Watched: keep, it is a pacing rule. II Armed: replace flat +6 armor with a small regenerating shield so it slows everyone equally instead of deleting multi-hit builds. III Swift: drop the windup term, speed to x1.15, add cooldown x0.75. IV Teeming: already the Jury Summons model and your best tier — gate it behind the on-screen cap and token pool. V Buried: replace stacked-everything with the Extreme Measures move — every boss gains one pattern, every biome gains one enemy from a deeper pool.

*Source: rpgsite.net Hades Pact of Punishment feature (full rank and Heat table). High.*

#### Per-enemy windup audit and a full retuned table

**Numbers.** Below 0.36 at Threat 0: stalker .22, voidling .24, ember .26, bat .28, rockling .30, sporeling .30, wraith .30, voidspawn .32. No margin: crawler .34, spitter .34, archer melee .36. Above: shieldman .44, brute .48, smith .58. Ranged: spitter .40, smith .50, archer .55. At Threat III, 11 of 14 fall below floor. Boss tells 0.34-0.50, all below archer's 0.55 despite 2-6x the damage.

Archer's 0.55s shoot windup is the best-tuned number in the table and should be the model. Active windows (0.12-0.24s) sit comfortably inside the 0.30s dodge i-frames; leave them alone.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Format wind / act / rec / lunge. crawler .42/.14/.35/150. bat .40/.12/.25/220. stalker .38/.10/.22/300 (must DISENGAGE 0.8s after striking — that is what makes it a role, not a fast rusher). voidling .38/.10/.22/240. brute .70/.18/.70/120. rockling .68/.18/.65/260 (retune to BRUISER; its 420 lunge is the worst reach lie in the game). shieldman .60/.18/.60/150. smith .78/.20/.85/130, shoot .60. archer .45/.14/.40/100, shoot .60. sporeling .42/.12/.30/170. ember .40/.12/.25/300 — both exploders need a 0.5s visible fuse ring, since burstOnDeath detonates instantly today. spitter .45/.14/.40/120, shoot .55. wraith .46/.14/.35/300. voidspawn .46/.14/.40/220. BOSSES warden .62/.22/.90, sporemother .58/.18/.85, sentinel .60/.20/.90, forgelord .68/.22/1.00, voidmaw .62/.18/.95; boss shoot .55-.70; phase patterns .55-.80. Hard floor: wind = max(0.36, base x threatMod).

*Source: Direct audit of index.html:468-530 against the reaction and Hornet findings. Values certain; the retuned numbers are my proposal.*

#### Role redundancy in the current roster

**Numbers.** 14 grunts collapse to 6 functional roles; 6 of 14 (43%) are the same role — crawler, bat, stalker, voidling, rockling and wraith all close and lunge, differing only in numbers (wraith at least adds terrain-phasing, which removes the put-a-wall-between-us verb). Then 2 heavies, 2 ranged, 1 blocker, 2 exploders, 1 splitter. BIOMES pools use only 1/3/4/3/3/3 types, and brute appears in exactly one biome despite being one of two heavies.

Skirmisher, area-denial and anti-air are entirely absent; population is boss-only. Archer and shieldman teach the sniper and blocker lessons once in ruins and are then abandoned.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Three new enemies, all behaviourally simple. SPORE VENT (fungal, area denial, cost 22): static, no movement AI, 0.7s cone telegraph then a 4s lingering cloud — teaches that the arena is a threat and is the first enemy the player can choose not to fight. FORGE DRONE (forge, anti-air, cost 24): hovers, ignores the player entirely while grounded, acquires and fires only while !P.onG — the counterweight your flight verb lacks, and the highest-value addition here. VOID HERALD (abyss, summoner/leader, cost 60): never attacks, +25% attack speed within 120px, summons 2 voidlings every 6s with a 0.8s tell, cap 1, never alongside voidspawn. Then retune rockling into the caves bruiser, retune stalker into a true skirmisher, and merge voidling into stalker as a reskin.

*Source: Direct classification of ENEMIES and BIOMES against the taxonomy sources. Certain.*

#### Proposed roster plan: enemies per biome and introduction order

**Numbers.** THE RIM (0-400m, ~45s): crawler. Cap 2, budget 24. CAVES (400-900m): crawler, bat, rockling-as-BRUISER; first elite ~600m, armored only. +bruiser, +flying rusher. Cap 3, budget 46-70. FUNGAL (900-1600m): sporeling, stalker-as-SKIRMISHER, spore vent[NEW], bat. +exploder, +skirmisher, +area denial. Cap 4, budget 74-112. RUINS (1600-2400m): archer, shieldman, brute, crawler. +sniper, +blocker. Cap 5, budget 112-156. FORGE (2400-3200m): ember, smith, forge drone[NEW], spitter. +anti-air, +heavy-ranged. Cap 5, budget 156-200. ABYSS (3200m+): wraith, voidspawn, void herald[NEW], stalker, plus two pulled from shallower pools. +summoner, +phaser. Cap 6, budget 200+. Elite order one per biome: armored -> volatile -> swift -> vampiric -> full pool. Totals: 16 grunt types (14 minus 1 merged plus 3 new), 5 bosses.

Each biome introduces exactly one new ROLE plus one recombination of taught roles, so the player never learns two things at once but always sees a new combination. Ruins gets two new roles, acceptable because sniper and blocker are the most legible and the player has ~4 minutes of practice by then.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Fixes concrete gaps in BIOMES (index.html:263-270): brute appears only in ruins despite being one of two heavies, and archer/shieldman teach once then vanish. Under this plan every role recurs at least twice at increasing budget, so the abyss feels like a graduation rather than a new game. Caves is where the punish loop is taught, so rockling's 0.68s tell and 0.65s recovery must land before anything faster does.

*Source: My proposal, structured on Halo/Dead Cells recombination logic and constrained by the reaction-time and token findings above.*

#### Readability wins inside the flat-rectangle constraint

**Numbers.** Choice-RT penalty currently being paid: 150-200ms. Cost of the fix: about five extra fillRect calls.

What matters is silhouette distinctiveness between idle and windup, not fidelity — a rectangle changing proportion is a bigger silhouette delta than most sprite animations. One shared swell+strobe is exactly why the group floor has to be 0.45s rather than 0.36s.

**Apply to Shardfall.** RUSHER compresses horizontally and leans into its facing. BRUISER stretches vertically 25% and drops a hard ground shadow whose length equals the reach line. SKIRMISHER shrinks and strobes faster. RANGED pulls a bright dot BACK along the firing line into the body, telegraphing timing and exact line together. EXPLODER fades in a hollow ring at blast radius. BLOCKER thickens its front edge. Plus the reach line in every case — together these give role identification from silhouette alone at 272px.

*Source: Tekken animation-distinctiveness discussion; danmaku colour-by-danger principle; index.html:3024-3030. High on mechanism, proposal on treatments.*

### Recommendations

- Set a hard windup floor of 0.36s single-target / 0.45s in groups of 3+, implemented as a clamp: wind = Math.max(0.36, base * threatMod). Eight of 14 grunts are below it today; 11 of 14 at Threat III.
- Delete `wind: 0.8` from THREATS[3] and THREATS[5] (index.html:833, 838); replace with attack cooldown x0.75 plus damage x1.20. Shortening a telegraph deletes the verb 'react' — the same error as D3's auto-skip affixes and PoE's removed T17 mods.
- Add a recovery state: a `rec` field (0.20 / 0.35 / 0.60-0.80 / 0.9-1.2s by weight class) during which the enemy is velocity-damped, direction-LOCKED and takes x1.30 damage in a distinct colour. Highest-impact change here — it is the missing third of tell-punish-reset.
- Fix the reach lie: declare `reach` = range + lunge x act (currently 1.9x-3.6x the stated range) and draw it as a 2px streak fading in over the first 35% of the windup.
- Implement an attack-token pool of 2 (3 in boss arenas, one reserved for the boss). Your own numbers demand it: the dodge answers 1.82 strikes/sec while six in-range enemies generate 3.43/sec.
- Replace the `EN.length > 120` cap with 24 total alive and 6 ON SCREEN (2/3/4/5/5/6 by biome). On-screen count is what governs readability at a 17-tile camera.
- Port an RoR2-style credit budget: costs 8 (crawler) to 70 (smith), B = 24 + depth_m x 0.055 x threat.dens, elite cost x2.5 with a second tier at x8, plus the anti-trickle rule so a deep budget buys wraiths and smiths rather than twenty crawlers.
- Add composition caps: max 1 ranged per 4 on-screen (hard max 2), max 1 blocker, max 2 exploders never within 2x blast radius, max 1 summoner never alongside a splitter, max 1 elite until Threat IV.
- Fix `e.pat` — written at index.html:1756, read nowhere — with a recurring patCd (4.5s in phase 2, 3.2s in phase 3). Today a transition is one firework, a 0.9s invuln and shoot cd x0.75, then the fight reverts to phase 1.
- Give each boss 2 patterns nobody else has (10 total instead of a shared pool of 5; voidmaw's phase-3 'spores' is sporemother's signature). It is the only place boss identity can live while all five share one melee+shoot skeleton.
- Telegraph the boss transition patterns — the 14-projectile ring, 150px instant explosion and 7-column firewall all fire with ZERO windup, violating the contract at index.html:466. Give each 0.55-0.80s drawn as gap structure, starting as the invuln ENDS.
- Raise boss tells above grunt tells: voidmaw has the fastest tells in the game (0.36 / 0.34) with the highest damage. Retune boss melee to 0.58-0.68 and boss shoot to 0.55-0.70.
- Clamp single-hit damage in hurtPlayer at 0.40 x maxHP for grunts and 0.55 x maxHP for a telegraphed boss heavy. Voidmaw currently hits for 79-96% of a fully-kitted player's HP.
- Change flat armor to min(armor, 0.6 x hit) or a percentage: `armored` (+9) plus Threat II (+6) gives 15 flat against max(1, dmg - armor), reducing multishot/fork/pierce/ailment builds to 1 damage per hit.
- Cap the `swift` elite at spd x1.35 and make speed and windup modifiers mutually exclusive. Approach time is a hidden telegraph; Supergiant prices enemy speed at 3 Heat/rank versus 1 for damage.
- Add SPORE VENT (fungal, static area-denial), FORGE DRONE (forge, anti-air, engages only while !P.onG — the counterweight your flight verb lacks) and VOID HERALD (abyss, summoner/leader). Retune rockling into the caves bruiser, retune stalker into a skirmisher that disengages, merge voidling into stalker as a reskin.
- Adopt the teaching order: Rim (rusher) -> Caves (+bruiser, +flying) -> Fungal (+exploder, +skirmisher, +area denial) -> Ruins (+sniper, +blocker) -> Forge (+anti-air, +heavy-ranged) -> Abyss (+summoner, +phaser plus recombination). One new role per biome; elites introduced armored -> volatile -> swift -> vampiric.
- Give windups role-specific silhouettes rather than the single shared swell+strobe at index.html:3028. Best readability-per-byte available in a flat-quad renderer.
- Add a danger colour channel on projectiles (white core = fast/must-dodge, hollow core = slow/leadable). Colour currently tracks the biome palette, encoding flavour instead of threat.
- Never randomise `wind`. The Elden Ring backlash is specifically about variable timing converting reaction into memorisation. Randomise WHICH attack, never WHEN.
- Instrument boss TTK before touching boss HP again — 605 to 5,470+ effective HP is a 9x spread with no relation to player DPS. Target 60-90s, and consider splitting deep-boss HP across bodies (Mantis Lords 210 -> 160+160).

### Caveats and unsourced claims

- Raising every windup by 0.06-0.20s will feel slower on first playtest and the instinct will be to revert. Do not — cut attack COOLDOWNS by the same absolute amount (crawler 1.5 -> 1.35, brute 2.1 -> 1.85) so threat-per-second stays flat. Measure hits-taken-per-minute, not perceived pace.
- Adding a recovery state makes every enemy easier to kill in a duel, so clear speed rises and the 8-12 minute run may stretch. Expect to re-tune HP down and density up; the budget system is the lever.
- The token pool makes a crowd of 6 read as less threatening even though damage-received is unchanged. Compensate with non-attack pressure from token-less enemies (crowding, blocking escape lanes, body-checking) or Threat IV/V density will feel hollow.
- Drawing the reach line removes surprise damage some players read as difficulty; deaths will drop and testers may call the game easier. Track deaths-per-run alongside a self-reported 'was that death my fault' — the second number is the one that matters.
- The three new enemies need bespoke AI branches, cutting against the single-file constraint. The forge drone must read P.onG and P.fuel, coupling enemy code to player state in a way nothing currently does.
- Changing flat armor rebalances every armor source at once (enemy arm, Threat II, the armored elite, the tree's +6, Bulwark's +10, plate's +22) and the player side uses the same max(1, dmg - armor) formula, so player survivability shifts too. Isolate it in its own playtest.
- Boss uniqueness (2 bespoke patterns each) is the largest content cost here and it is easy to under-deliver by shipping 10 variations of 'projectiles come out'. If budget forces a cut, cut the count not the distinctiveness.
- Several numbers are practitioner consensus rather than developer statements (Tekken's 20/23-frame thresholds, Sekiro's 12-frame deflect, Maxroll's D3 values). The firmest sources here are the GDKeys Hornet frame data, the RoR2 Director formulas (datamined), the Hades Pact rank table, the Griesemer Halo interview and the PoE 3.35 T17 removals — lean on those when defending a decision.
- The 0.36s floor is my synthesis (250ms RT + ~60ms identification + ~50ms input lead-in), not a figure any source states. It is more conservative than Hollow Knight's 0.50s minimum, but treat it as a hypothesis; Shardfall's camera and enemy size may push it to 0.42.
- Retuning boss HP against measured DPS will expose how wide the build power spread already is — a stacked more-multiplier build versus a fresh Vanguard may differ 5-10x, so no single HP number gives both a 60-90s fight. Expect to need a soft damage cap or a DPS-aware HP scalar.
- Nothing was written to the repo. All findings are against the working tree at /workspaces/Sweet-Spot/shardfall/index.html on a clean main branch; line numbers will drift as soon as anything is edited.


<a id="items"></a>
## Itemisation, loot and drop maths

### Summary

Shardfall's itemisation has one structural hole that dominates every other issue: **loot has no depth axis**. `mkItem(baseId, rarity)` takes no item level; affix ranges are constant (`AFFIXES` rolls uniform min..max forever); the base pool is unlock-gated but not depth-gated; rarity odds are fixed constants at the drop site. Only shard *count* scales with depth (`depthMul`). So a 3200-tile descent through 6 biomes and 5 threat tiers returns statistically identical items at tile 50 and tile 3000 — which contradicts the whole premise of descending. Both reference games solve this the same way: D2 gates affixes by alvl derived from ilvl and pushes rarity via `(ilvl - qlvl)`; PoE gates every affix tier by `required_level` (verified from datamined RePoE: IncreasedLife has 13 tiers gated at ilvl 1/5/11/18/24/30/36/44/54/64/73/81/86). Second finding: **armour is uncapped while every other defensive stat is capped** (`sres` capped 0.85, `crit` 0.95, but `applyArmor = max(1, raw - armor)` with no ceiling) — ~64 flat armour is reachable today via Plate + The Mountain + shield + arm affixes + ironskin, which reduces every grunt hit at depth 3200 to 1. Third: sockets are the dominant power lever and rarity 2+ grants +1 socket unconditionally, which is a bigger power jump than any affix. Measured current drop volume for an assumed 220-kill / 8.2-chest / 1.7-boss ten-minute run: ~16.9 gear items (0.72 unique, 3.8 rare, 7.6 magic, 4.8 normal) plus ~32 gems — roughly 3.3:1 noise-to-signal by item count, but with zero progression texture inside any of it. The plan below adds an ilvl axis, a 6-tier affix table with PoE-style spawn weights, depth-banded rarity and base pools, a pity system modelled on Slay the Spire's, and takes bases 12→20 and uniques 24→44 organised by depth tier rather than evenly per base.

### Findings (10)

#### D2 rarity determination — exact datamined formula and constants

**Numbers.** LoD v1.10+ non-uber row from ItemRatio.txt: Unique ratio 400 / divisor 1 / min 6400; Set 160 / 2 / 5600; Rare 100 / 2 / 3200; Magic 34 / 3 / 192; HiQuality (superior) 12 / 8; Normal 2 / 2. At ilvl == qlvl with 0 MF and no treasure-class bonus this yields: unique 128/(400*128) = 0.25%, set 0.625%, rare 1.0%, magic 2.94%, superior 8.33%, normal 50%. The minimums cap the ceiling: unique can never exceed 128/6400 = 2.0%, set 2.29%, rare 4.0%, magic 66.7% — i.e. infinite Magic Find buys at most 8x the base unique rate. Class-specific bases use a harsher row (Unique 240/3, Rare 80/3, Set 120/3, Magic 17/6). Confidence: high — this is the shipped 1.13 data file, not an estimate.

Diablo 2 rolls quality sequentially Unique → Set → Rare → Magic → Superior → Normal, each with a ratio/divisor/minimum from ItemRatio.txt. Formula (Phrozen Keep): chance = (Ratio - (ilvl - qlvl)/Divisor) * 128; chance = chance * 100/(100+EMF); if chance < Min then chance = Min; chance -= chance*tcbonus/1024; P = 128/chance. Note the direction: a HIGHER chance number means a RARER item, so subtracting (ilvl-qlvl)/Divisor is what makes deeper/higher-level areas drop better rarity. Magic Find has per-rarity diminishing returns: EMF = MF*250/(MF+250) for unique, MF*250/(MF+500) for set, MF*250/(MF+600) for rare, and no DR for magic.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Adopt the SHAPE, not the constants — D2's numbers are tuned for a 40-hour grind, Shardfall needs a 10-minute run. Take three things: (1) rarity is a single sequential roll per drop, cheap to implement; (2) area level pushes rarity, which Shardfall currently lacks entirely; (3) there is a hard ceiling on rarity so that a magic-find/greed build cannot break the economy — Shardfall's `greed` affix currently scales shard count only, so add a `rare` push with an explicit cap of +80% relative to base like D2's 8x-but-capped structure.

*Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/fabd/diablo2/master/code/d2_113_data/ItemRatio.txt (datamined 1.13 game data) and https://d2mods.info/forum/viewtopic.php?t=44413 (Phrozen Keep, formula)*

#### PoE affix tier structure — two distinct idioms, verified from datamined mod data

**Numbers.** IncreasedLife prefix (idiom A, all weights 1000): T13→T1 gated at ilvl 1/5/11/18/24/30/36/44/54/64/73/81/86 with ranges 3-9, 10-19, 20-29, 30-39, 40-49, 50-59, 60-69, 70-79, 80-89, 90-99, 100-109, 110-119, 120-129. At ilvl 86 all 13 tiers are live at weight 1000, so P(top tier | life prefix rolled) = 1/13 = 7.7%. LocalIncreasedPhysicalDamagePercent (idiom B): 8 tiers at ilvl 1/11/23/35/46/60/73/83 with ranges 40-49, 50-64, 65-84, 85-109, 110-134, 135-154, 155-169, 170-179 and spawn weights 1000/1000/1000/400/200/100/50/25 — at full unlock the weight sum is 2775 so P(Merciless, the top tier) = 25/2775 = 0.90%. FireResist suffix (idiom A + compression): 8 tiers at ilvl 1/12/24/36/48/60/72/84, ranges 6-11, 12-17, 18-23, 24-29, 30-35, 36-41, then compressed 42-45 and 46-48. LocalIncreasedAttackSpeed (compression): 5-7, 8-10, 11-13, 14-16, 17-19, 20-22, 23-25, 26-27 — the top tier adds only 1 point of range. Confidence: very high, these are exact values from the datamined mods table.

Every PoE affix family is a list of tiers, each with a `required_level` (ilvl gate), a value range, and a per-tag `spawn_weight`. Critically, PoE uses TWO different weighting idioms and they do different jobs. Idiom A (staple stats): all tiers have equal spawn weight, so the top tier's rarity comes purely from being unlocked last and from competing with more tiers as ilvl rises. Idiom B (chase stats): spawn weight decays sharply at high tiers, so the top tier is a genuine lottery even at max ilvl. Also note the top-tier value COMPRESSION on stats that would break the game if they scaled linearly.

**Apply to Shardfall.** This is the single most transferable finding. Shardfall's 12 affixes currently roll one uniform range forever. Give each affix 6 tiers gated on depth, use idiom B (decaying weights) for the four affixes that define builds (dmg, critMult, cdr, crit) and idiom A (uniform weights) for the eight staples, and compress the top two tiers of ms and leech exactly as PoE compresses attack speed and resists. Concrete table in the recommendations.

*Source: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/brather1ng/RePoE/master/RePoE/data/mods.min.json (RePoE, PoE game-data extraction; 30,909 mods, queried directly)*

#### Affix counts per rarity in D2 and PoE, and the prefix/suffix frame

**Numbers.** D2 magic affix split: 50% suffix only, 25% prefix only, 25% both. D2 rare affix count array in D2Game.dll is literally {3,4,4,5,5,6}, giving P(3)=16.7%, P(4)=33.3%, P(5)=33.3%, P(6)=16.7% (Nefarius, Phrozen Keep). Jewels are capped at 4 affixes / 2 per side. D2 crafted items scale count with ilvl: ilvl 1-30 → 4 affixes 20% / 3 20% / 2 20% / 1 40%; ilvl 31-50 → 4 20% / 3 20% / 2 60%; ilvl 51-70 → 4 20% / 3 80%; ilvl 71-99 → always 4. Worked frequency example: on a Colossus Sword at ilvl 30, prefix 'Cruel' has frequency 4 against a total legal-prefix frequency of 285, so P = 4/285 = 1.4%. Confidence: high on the magic split and the array (multiple independent sources agree); medium on the crafted table.

D2 magic items roll at most one prefix and one suffix, with a documented split. D2 rares roll a count drawn from a hardcoded array in D2Game.dll rather than a formula, and enforce max 3 prefixes / 3 suffixes plus a group-exclusion rule (never two affixes from the same group). Affixes are selected by summing the `frequency` field of every affix legal at that item's affix level and item type, then rolling proportionally — identical in spirit to PoE's spawn_weights. PoE: magic = up to 1 prefix + 1 suffix, rare = up to 3 prefixes + 3 suffixes on most bases.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall has no prefix/suffix concept and a fixed 1/3/1 affix count. Split the 12 affixes 6 prefixes (dmg, hp, arm, critMult, fuel, leech) / 6 suffixes (cdr, crit, ms, sres, greed, focus) and make the affix COUNT itself a depth-scaled reward — that is a second, free progression axis costing about 6 lines of code. Also adopt the frequency-sum selection so a Plate stops rolling '% attack speed' and a Sword stops rolling '+fuel', which is a large share of Shardfall's current noise.

*Source: https://planetdiablo.eu/diablo2/itemdb/affix_info_en.php and https://d2mods.info/forum/viewtopic.php?t=43793*

#### What makes a unique build-defining rather than incremental — the rule-break-plus-cost pattern

**Numbers.** PoE Kaom's Heart (PoE1): +500 to maximum Life and 'has no sockets' — breaks the socket rule, and pays for it by deleting your 6-link. PoE2's version: +1500 to maximum Life, (30-40)% increased Stun Threshold, 'You have no Spirit', 445 armour, req level 68 / 121 Str — same design, bigger numbers, new cost. PoE Shavronne's Wrappings: chaos damage does not bypass energy shield, which breaks the game's core chaos rule and single-handedly created the low-life/CI archetype. PoE Mjölner: triggers socketed lightning spells on hit, breaking the cast-vs-attack separation. D2 Enigma runeword (JahIthBer): +2 to all skills, +45% FRW, **+1 to Teleport regardless of class**, +750-775 defence, +0-74 Str based on clvl, +14 life after each kill, 15% damage taken goes to mana (+1 per clvl), +1-99% MF based on clvl — the Teleport oskill is the rule break and it rewrote every build in D2. D2 Stone of Jordan: +1 to all skills and 25% increased maximum mana — the first item to carry a mod no rare could ever roll, and it became the game's de-facto currency. Confidence: high on Enigma and Kaom's (verified this session); high on Shavronne's/Mjölner/SoJ from well-established documentation.

The consistent pattern across both games: a build-defining unique changes what is POSSIBLE, not how much. It grants an effect no affix can roll, and — in the good ones — carries an explicit cost that forces the rest of the build to reorganise around it. Uniques that are merely 'big numbers with no downside' are functionally just a higher rarity tier and generate no build diversity. D2's own escalation ladder is instructive: it has three unique tiers (normal / exceptional / elite) on the same silhouettes, which is the cheapest possible content multiplier — the same art and the same identity, re-found at higher level with better numbers.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Audit against the rule: **every unique must break exactly one rule and pay one cost.** Currently 5 of Shardfall's 24 fail this. 'Second Skin' (+2 sockets, no cost) is strictly better than any rare vest, so it is not a unique, it is rarity 4. Same for 'Scalemail of the Deep' (+35 HP, +8 armor, no cost) and 'Skyrigger' (huge fuel + fall immunity, no cost). By contrast 'Judgment' (more×2.4, cd×1.9, +5 pierce) and 'Hornet's Call' (+2 arrows, more×0.65) are exactly right. Fix: give Second Skin '+2 sockets, -6 armor and -15 max HP'; give Scalemail an offsetting -10% move speed; give Skyrigger '+160 fuel, fall immune, but -25% max HP'. Also add D2's three-tier ladder: the same unique name re-found deeper with better rolled values.

*Source: https://pathofexile2.wiki.fextralife.com/Kaom's+Heart+Conqueror+Plate ; https://diablo2.diablowiki.net/Enigma and corroborating runeword databases*

#### Loot pacing and pity systems — the measured cadences other games ship

**Numbers.** Slay the Spire card rewards: base roughly 60% common / 37% uncommon / 3% rare, with a hidden offset that starts at -5% on the rare chance, increments +1% per combat reward roll (+0.5% on Ascension 7+), resets to -5% whenever a rare is rolled, and caps at +40%. Realised split on the sequel's non-combat rewards measures ~63/34/3 (~63/35.5/1.5 at A7+). Dead Cells: legendary drops at 1% from common mobs and 6% from elites; legendary altars have a 2% base spawn chance per biome that increases by 15% for each biome where no altar spawned — an explicit escalating pity. Hades (from Herodata.lua): RareChance 0.10, EpicChance 0.05, LegendaryChance 0.12, so a standard boon is ~75-85% Common, with Heroic reachable only via an Epic exchange. Diablo 3 Torment legendary bonuses: T1 +15%, T2 +32%, T3 +52%, T4 +75%, T5 +101%, T6 +131%. Confidence: high on Dead Cells and STS (datamined and corroborated); medium on the Hades figures (single datamined source); medium on the D3 Torment table (community testing, Blizzard never published full figures).

Nothing published gives a validated 'wow moments per minute' figure, and the pop-psychology sources on variable-ratio reinforcement returned by search are SEO content, not research — I would not cite them. What IS solid is the shipped pity mathematics of games in Shardfall's exact bracket, which reveal a shared design position: keep the base rate genuinely low, then add an escalating offset so that a dry streak is bounded. Every roguelite in the reference set does this; PoE deliberately does not, and compensates with deterministic crafting instead.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall has no pity of any kind. Add two counters modelled directly on STS's offset: `rareBonus` starting at -0.5%, +0.35% per gear drop, reset on any rare-or-better, capped +8%; and `uniqueBonus` starting at 0, +0.25% per gear drop since the last unique, reset on unique, capped +5%. This costs four lines and converts the worst run in a hundred from 'zero rares' into 'a late rare', which is what actually protects a 10-minute session.

*Source: https://spire-codex.com/mechanics/card-rarity ; https://deadcells.wiki.gg/wiki/Gear ; datamined Herodata.lua via Hades community; https://www.purediablo.com/diablo-3-legendary-drop-rates-revealed-updated (403 to fetch, numbers via search snippet — lower confidence)*

#### Power budget per slot — the only published breakdown worth copying

**Numbers.** WoW armour slot multipliers: Chest / Legs / Head = 1.00 (16/16); Shoulders / Feet / Hands / Waist = 0.75 (12/16); Wrist / Back = 0.5625 (9/16). Weapons: 2H = 100%, 1H = 66%, offhand = 33%. Stat conversion modifiers: all combat ratings and primary stats StatMod 1, Stamina 2/3, Attack Power 1/2. Jewellery carries no primary stat but roughly 330% of armour's secondary-stat budget. Base armour formulas at uncommon quality: Cloth = iLevel*1.19 + 5.1; Leather = iLevel*2.22 + 10; Mail = iLevel*4.9 + 29; Plate = iLevel*9 + 23; Shield = iLevel*85/3 + 133. Worked base-vs-total example from the tuning methodology: at level 25, base 1.0 + skills 0.24 + items 0.27 + abilities 0.20 = total power 1.71, i.e. 71% above an unmodified baseline. Confidence: high on the 1.0/0.75/0.5625 ladder (multiple independent sources); medium on the weapon ratios and the armour formulas (the wiki page is flagged outdated).

WoW is the one major game whose slot power budget is fully reverse-engineered and stable across expansions, and it uses a clean geometric ladder: each slot tier gets exactly 75% of the previous tier's budget. Weapons are budgeted separately by hand count. Separately, the standard methodology for splitting power between items, skills and passives is to define base power (everything that grows without player choice) as 1.0 and express every magnifier as a percentage of it.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall has no common power scale across slots: Plate offers hp:80 / arm:12 and Greataxe offers dmg:30, and nothing asserts these are comparable. Define one `power(item)` function and target weapon 1.00 / armour 0.60 / offhand-shield 0.40 / second weapon 0.50. Then check base DPS spread: Sword 10/0.38 = 26.3, Axe 16/0.55 = 29.1, Greataxe 30/0.85 = 35.3, Crossbow 20/0.9 = 22.2, Bow 8/0.5 = 16.0, Wand 7/0.34 = 20.6 — a 2.2x spread that currently reads as random rather than as tiers, because gearPool() is unlock-gated only and a Greataxe can drop at depth 20.

*Source: https://warcraft.wiki.gg/wiki/Stat_budget ; https://wowutils.com/loot/slot-strength ; https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/the-craft-of-game-systems-tuning-rpg-content*

#### Currency sinks that stay meaningful — the repeatable-variance rule

**Numbers.** D2 gambling: rings cost a flat 50,000 gold and amulets a flat 65,000 regardless of character level (all other slots scale with clvl). Outcome odds are 1797/2000 magic (89.85%), 200/2000 rare (10%), 2/2000 set (0.1%), 1/2000 unique (0.05%). Gambled item level is clvl-5 to clvl+4, uniform 1/10 across that band. Upgrade-to-exceptional chance = (ilvl - qlvl)*0.9% + 1%; upgrade-to-elite = (ilvl - qlvl)*0.33% + 1%. D3 Kanai's Cube costs: Archive of Tal Rasha (extract a legendary power) = 1 each of the five bounty materials + 5 Death's Breath; Law of Kulle (reforge a legendary, the long-term sink) = 5 each of the five bounty materials + 50 Forgotten Souls; Hope of Cain (upgrade a rare to legendary) = 50 Reusable Parts + 50 Arcane Dust + 50 Veiled Crystal + 25 Death's Breath. PoE's headline sink is the vendor recipe loop: a full set of rare items sells for at least 1 Chaos Orb, or 2 if every piece is unidentified, and the currency produced is determined by the lowest-ilvl item in the set (below ilvl 60 the recipe degrades to an Orb of Chance). Confidence: high on the D2 gambling table and the Cube recipes; high on the chaos recipe.

The pattern shared by every currency that survives into the late game: the top sink is repeatable, uncapped, and returns VARIANCE rather than a fixed unlock. Once every sink is a one-time purchase, income outruns expenditure and the currency dies. D2's gambling and D3's Law of Kulle are the canonical examples — both are deliberately bad expected value, both are infinitely repeatable, and both are the only route to a specific targeted outcome.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall's shards currently buy meta-tree nodes (finite) and gem fusion (semi-infinite). That is one and a half sinks. The economy will stall the moment the tree is bought out. Add at least one uncapped variance sink — a Vault-item affix reroll priced as a multiple of a run's income — plus an escalating Vault-slot ladder. Calibration rule derived from D2: one gamble ≈ one deep run's gold income, which is the right anchor for 'the exciting purchase'.

*Source: https://diablo2.io/forums/gambling-guide-by-hastmannen-t6604.html ; https://maxroll.gg/d3/resources/kanais-cube ; PoE vendor recipe documentation*

#### Vendor trash: the accepted answer is not fewer drops, it is fewer drops SHOWN

**Numbers.** No published percentage exists for how much each filter strictness hides — I searched for it and the maintainers do not publish it, so any figure you have seen is community estimate. My own estimate for a T16 map: 150-250 items drop, semi-strict shows roughly 15-25% of them, uber-strict under 5%, and of what is shown perhaps 0-2 per map are actual equipment upgrades for the character running it. Mark that whole sentence as my estimate, not a source. Confidence: high that 7 strictness levels and the cheapest-layer rule exist; low on the percentages.

The genre converged on three mechanisms in a clear order of leverage: stop generating the trash (D3's Loot 2.0 explicitly moved to 'fewer item drops, but what does drop will be of greater value', and whites/blues stop appearing at high difficulty); hide the trash client-side (PoE's loot filter ecosystem); or auto-convert the trash into currency (D2 gold, PoE vendor recipes, Last Epoch/D4 auto-salvage). PoE's canonical filter ships in 7 strictness levels from soft to uber-plus-strict, is driven by economy data mining, and its stated rule is that 'the hidden items are always the cheapest layer' — expensive drops are never hidden at any strictness.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall's highest-leverage single change is not a drop-rate tweak — it is putting a computed delta on the ground label. `dpsOf(a)` already exists at line 1282 for the socket screen; reuse it to render '+14% DPS' or '-8% DPS' above the pickup before the player touches it. That converts every one of the ~17 gear drops from a tooltip-read into a glance, and it costs about 20 lines. Do that before touching any drop rate.

*Source: https://github.com/NeverSinkDev/NeverSink-Filter (README, strictness levels and design rule) ; https://www.diablowiki.net/Loot_2.0*

#### Shardfall's current drop mathematics, computed from index.html

**Numbers.** Assuming a 10-minute run of 220 kills across ~45 chunks (≈8.2 chests, ≈1.7 bosses, ≈20 elites): gear from grunts 220*0.047 = 10.3 (1.55 rare, 3.95 magic, 4.83 normal); from chests 8.2*0.6 = 4.92 (0.34 unique, 1.60 rare, 2.97 magic); from bosses 1.7 (0.37 unique, 0.66 rare, 0.66 magic). **Total ≈ 16.9 gear items per run: 0.72 unique, 3.81 rare, 7.58 magic, 4.83 normal.** Gems: 13.2 from grunts + 8.2 from chests + 3.4 from bosses + 7.0 from elites ≈ 32 per run. So the player picks up ~49 items in 10 minutes, ~4.9/min, of which about 4.5 are genuinely worth reading — a 3.3:1 noise-to-signal ratio on gear and roughly 10:1 on gems. Rare-item variety: with 12 affixes taken 3 at a time and no tiers and no weights, there are exactly C(12,3) = 220 distinct rare stat-lines in the entire game, and 4 of the 12 affixes (greed, fuel, focus, and sres for some builds) are dead on most builds, so the effective pool is closer to C(8,3) = 56. Chance of a specific unique = (1/12 bases) * (1/2 UNIQ2 coinflip) = 4.17% per unique drop, so 1/(0.72*0.0417) = 33 runs ≈ 5.5 hours to target a named unique. Confidence: high — computed directly from the source, subject only to the 220-kill/45-chunk traversal assumption which you should verify by instrumenting a real run.

Per grunt kill (lines 1670-1673): 6% gem, else 5% of the remaining 94% for gear = 4.7% gear. Gear rarity is `chance(0.15)?2 : chance(0.45)?1 : 0`, giving rare 15.0%, magic 38.25%, normal 46.75%, and unique 0% — grunts can never drop a unique except via the threat-tier upgrade at line 1118. Per chest (openChest): 8-14 shards, 1 guaranteed gem, 60% gear at `chance(.07)?3 : chance(.35)?2 : 1` = unique 7.0%, rare 32.55%, magic 60.45%, normal 0%. Per boss: 2 gems plus 1 gear at unique 22% / rare 39% / magic 39%. Per elite: 35% gem. Densities from the code's own note at line 101: 1 chest per 5.5 chunks, 1 boss per 26 chunks, CHUNK=48 tiles.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Two numbers stand out as wrong. 220 distinct rare stat-lines is far too few for a game asking for repeated 10-minute runs — that is the number the affix tier table fixes (it multiplies to ~47,500). And ~32 gems per run against 62 gems and typically 4-6 sockets means gems, not gear, are the real noise source; the gem drop rate should come down roughly 40% while gem TIER (the fuse system) becomes the drop-side reward.

*Source: /workspaces/Sweet-Spot/shardfall/index.html lines 101, 1110-1135, 1653-1673, 1691*

#### Armour is the only uncapped defensive stat and it already has an immortality breakpoint

**Numbers.** Enemy damage at maximum depth uses depthMul = 1 + depth/900, so at depth 3200 the multiplier is 4.56: crawler 8 → 36.5, stalker 13 → 59.3, brute 16 → 73.0, wraith 18 → 82.1, voidmaw boss 38 → 173.3. Reachable armour TODAY: Plate base 12 + The Mountain unique arm:22 = 34, plus Shield base 8, plus arm affix 2-9 on the shield's three rare affixes, plus arm affix on the unique's one affix, plus ironskin aura +4 ≈ 64. At 64 armour a max-depth crawler, stalker and archer all deal exactly 1 damage and a brute deals 9. Under the proposed 6-tier affix table with T1 arm at 19-24 this rises past 90 and brutes also floor to 1. Confidence: high — read directly from lines 241, 1181-1182, 1482.

`applyArmor(raw, armor) = Math.max(ARMOR_FLOOR, raw - armor)` with `ARMOR_FLOOR = 1` and `armorVal() = Math.max(0, inc('arm') + 4 if ironskin)` — no upper bound. Meanwhile `sresVal()` is capped at 0.85 and `critChance()` at 0.95. Flat subtraction with no cap means armour's value is not linear, it is a step function that goes to infinity at the point where armour exceeds incoming damage.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Change `applyArmor` to `Math.max(raw * 0.40, raw - armor)` — a 60% reduction cap, mirroring the 0.85 sres cap and D2's own 50% damage-reduction cap. Do this BEFORE shipping the affix tier table, because the tier table is what makes the breakpoint trivially reachable. This one line is more load-bearing than any drop-rate change in this report.

*Source: /workspaces/Sweet-Spot/shardfall/index.html lines 241, 1181-1182, 1482, 1698 and the ENEMIES/depthMul tables*

### Recommendations

- ADD AN ITEM LEVEL. `mkItem(baseId, rarity)` becomes `mkItem(baseId, rarity, ilvl)` where `ilvl = max(0, floor(y/TILE) - SURFACE)`, range 0-3140, and `band B = clamp(floor(ilvl/320), 0, 9)`. Threat tier adds a band bonus of [0,0,1,1,2,2][t]. Every table below keys off B. This is the structural change; everything else is a table.
