(5) HADES TIGHT DEADLINE - 9:00 per region at rank 1, minus 2:00 per rank to a 7:00 minimum, at 2 and 3 Heat.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall's Weight is the weakest version of all of these. WEIGHT_GRACE=90, WEIGHT_EVERY=18, WEIGHT_MAX=10 (index.html:246) means max penalty at 90 + 9x18 = 252s in a band and then it STOPS, so a durable build farms indefinitely at -30% damage and +30% enemy speed, and 6 bands x 252s is about 25 minutes of ceiling-capped free farming. And grace is flat while bands are not: Caves is 330 tiles and Forge is 800, so beating the timer needs 3.67 tiles/s in Caves and 8.89 in Forge, 2.4x harsher in the band that is also 22% less open. Take RoR2's uncapped run-time scaling plus Spelunky's terminal pursuer.

*Source: spelunky.fandom.com/wiki/Ghost_(Classic); riskofrain2.wiki.gg/wiki/Difficulty; deadcells.wiki.gg/wiki/Time,_killstreak_and_no-hit_doors and /Curse; rpgsite.net/feature/10287 - high except Hades (guide, corroborated)*

#### Hades Heat - difficulty as an itemised ladder

**Numbers.** Hard Labor +20% x5; Lasting Consequences -25% x4; Jury Summons +20% x3; Calisthenics +15% x2; Extreme Measures up to 10 Heat; Tight Deadline 9:00 / 7:00

15 conditions, each with per-rank numbers and a Heat price: Hard Labor +20% enemy damage per rank, max 5; Lasting Consequences -25% healing effectiveness per rank, max 4; Jury Summons +20% enemies in standard encounters per rank, max 3; Calisthenics +15% enemy health per rank, max 2; Convenience Fee +40% shop prices per rank, max 2; Middle Management adds an elite or distraction to mini-boss fights, max 2 at 2 Heat each; Extreme Measures arms one more boss per rank, up to 10 Heat; Tight Deadline 9:00 then 7:00 region clear time at 2 and 3 Heat.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall's threat tiers 0-5 are one ladder with bundled effects, so the player cannot express a preference or isolate what killed them. Split into named axes with per-rank numbers - Armored +3 armor x3, Swift +8% enemy speed x3, Teeming +20% density x3, Buried -15% light x2, Impatient -20s Weight grace x3 - and derive the shard and rarity multiplier from total rank rather than tier index. Note Supergiant caps enemy-HP scaling at +30% while Shardfall's depthMul already multiplies HP by 4.49x at the floor.

*Source: rpgsite.net/feature/10287; gameskinny.com hades-pact-of-punishment guide - medium-high, cross-corroborated*

#### Verticality - what changes, and Downwell's shipped lesson

**Numbers.** Downwell 4 areas x 3 levels = 12, about 10-15 min. Shardfall GRAV 1500, FALL_SAFE 520 gives 5.6 tiles; proposed 760 gives 12 tiles; camera bias 34% from top

Downwell is the only widely-studied pure-descent game: four themed areas of three levels each, so 12 levels, about 10-15 minutes per run. Fumoto's GDC 2016 talk records the key iteration - at first he tried to make each generated level as interesting as possible, but the game felt too enclosed and confined once the gunboots existed, so he changed things drastically to have a lot more space, way fewer platforms and way more floating enemies. A descent game needs EMPTIER levels than a horizontal one, and enemies that occupy air rather than ground, because gravity is the dominant axis and platforms are friction. Second, descent is one-way, so anything placed above the player after they pass is unreachable and no reward may be gated behind climbing back. Third, side-scroller camera practice is to bias the view in the direction of travel and lock the axis during long falls.

**Apply to Shardfall.** (1) Shardfall CLOSES UP with depth (0.564 to 0.348 air), the opposite of Downwell's lesson, in the bands where the player has flight and the harshest timer. (2) FALL_SAFE=520 px/s with GRAV=1500 gives a safe fall height of 520 squared over 3000 = 90px = 5.6 tiles, less than one viewport: in a game whose verb is falling the core action is taxed by default, and the only relief is the Featherfall aura, which charges an armour socket for the privilege of playing the premise. Raise FALL_SAFE to 760 (safe height 192px = 12 tiles), keep FALL_DMG 0.11 and the 55% cap, and add a dive - holding down above 700 px/s converts the landing into a 3-tile shockwave at weapon damage x1.4 and negates fall damage, turning the penalty into the verb as gunboots did. Move Featherfall to the meta tree. (3) Camera: player at 34% from the top while descending, lerping to 50% when rising, hard-locked horizontally during Shaft stanzas.

*Source: gdcvault.com/play/1023533; fandom.com/articles/gdc-five-things-learned-downwell; gamedeveloper.com Scroll Back (Itay Keren) - high on Downwell; the fall arithmetic is mine from index.html:234-235*

#### Noita - biome identity in a destructible vertical world

**Numbers.** 8 main-path biomes; 7 interior Holy Mountains

Closest structural relative: 8 main-path biomes descended in fixed order - Mines, Coal Pits, Snowy Depths, Hiisi Base, Underground Jungle, The Vault, Temple of the Art, The Laboratory - each separated by a HOLY MOUNTAIN, an enemy-free zone where the player edits wands, buys spells and picks a perk. Side biomes branch off and are optional, offering concentrated rewards. Two facts matter more than the list: the descent is interrupted at fixed intervals by a guaranteed safe room with a guaranteed permanent choice, and the biomes are not palette swaps - Hiisi Base is a built structure with mechanical enemies, Underground Jungle is organic and dense, The Vault is architectural, so the GENERATOR differs per biome. Per-biome depth coordinates are not published on either wiki mirror.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall has band boundaries but nothing happens at them - no safe room, no guaranteed choice, no punctuation, and the Weight just resets. The Holy Mountain is the missing punctuation mark and does four jobs at once: relief beat, guaranteed reward choice, checkpoint feel, and a home for the depth anchor. Put one at every band boundary - carved, enemy-free, 20x12, holding the band's guaranteed shrine, the anchor stone and a heal to 70% max HP - and make it the ONLY place the Weight refunds. That converts anchors from an out-of-run menu into an in-run destination.

*Source: noita.wiki.gg/wiki/Biomes - high on structure, depths not found*

#### Secrets - fairness, and Shardfall's secret walls are literally invisible

**Numbers.** secret wall #5a5f66 equals stone #5a5f66 (index.html:253, 260); buried-check success 39.6% over 48 gate fires; proposed 7 secrets per run = 1 per 90s

The published critique of Super Metroid: every block could hide something, and there was often no visual difference between fixed geometry and destructible blocks - consistent rules, but wall-bombing tedium. Modern practice and Hollow Knight use a consistent visual tell plus a proximity cue. The target is that the RULE is learnable and the INSTANCE discoverable without exhaustive search.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Shardfall fails this in the strictest way. index.html:260 defines the secret wall as tile 10 with colour #5a5f66, and index.html:253 defines stone as tile 2 with colour #5a5f66 - the same hex string, so in a flat-quad renderer the secret wall is pixel-identical to stone. The only ways to find one are the Prospector aura (an armour socket) or random digging. Second, the gate at index.html:990-993 requires a 7x6 region to be entirely solid before stamping, which I measured succeeding only 39.6% of the time and which biases secrets toward solid biomes, while the sealed vault eight lines above carves its room unconditionally - two POIs behaving inconsistently for no reason. Fix all three: give tile 10 the colour #5f6167 plus a deterministic 1px lighter diagonal hairline (one extra fillRect in the chunk bake, zero per-frame cost); emit one particle every 0.6s from secret tiles within 4 tiles of the player using the existing PART pool; carve the shell solid before stamping instead of testing. Keep Prospector as the from-anywhere minimap reveal. Density target: 1 guaranteed per band in the Seam stanza plus 1 floating at 40%, about 7 per run or one every 90 seconds.

*Source: thomaswell.medium.com Hollow Knight and Metroid essay for the critique; code facts measured directly - high confidence*

#### MEASURED: POI placement fails 75-80% of the time, silently

**Numbers.** chest 15.0% to 3.1%; shrine 17.8% to 4.4%; secret 15.0% to 5.9% (39.6% success); vault 7.8%; boss 2.5%. Corridor 320 chunks, mean air 0.422

Replicated genChunk over a 5-chunk-wide x 64-row corridor of 320 chunks. Gate fire versus actual placement: chest 15.0% to 3.1% (20.8% success); shrine 17.8% to 4.4% (24.6%); secret cache 15.0% to 5.9% (39.6%); sealed vault 7.8%, always places because it carves its own room; boss arena 2.5%, always places. The chest and shrine loops try 14 random positions for a strict tile signature - for chests, air with solid below AND air above - and just break with nothing on failure, and the failure probability is a function of local noise. Total chest-bearing POIs land at 0.031 + 0.078x1.5 + 0.059 = 0.207 per chunk = 1 per 4.8 chunks, which is why the docs say 1 chest per 5.5 chunks - but that number is an emergent accident of three systems, two of which fail most of the time.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Root cause of the structural problem, and invisible in code because failure is a silent break. Minimal fix: on failure carve a 3x3 pocket at the least-bad candidate instead of dropping the POI, which makes gate rate equal placement rate and the documented densities true. Correct fix: the stanza-schedule model. Either way add test/suite-9.js generating 500 chunks at a fixed seed and asserting placed-POI counts per band.

*Source: Measured from index.html:961-1007; script at /tmp/claude-1000/-workspaces-Sweet-Spot/00ff769b-1474-43f1-895c-6b76d1865b29/scratchpad/gen2.js - high confidence*

#### MEASURED: the world closes up with depth, opposite to the code comment

**Numbers.** th falls from 0.5164 at ty=96 to 0.42 at ty=3000. Air: caves 0.564 / fungal 0.510 / ruins 0.428 / forge 0.378 / abyss 0.348; row extremes 0.760 and 0.117

index.html:948 reads 'cave carve - density grows a touch with depth' and implements th = 0.52 - Math.min(0.10, ty/WORLD_H*0.12) with air where noise is below th. Lowering the threshold with depth means FEWER tiles fall below it, so air DECREASES. Measured per-band mean air along the corridor: caves 0.564, fungal 0.510, ruins 0.428, forge 0.378, abyss 0.348. At individual chunk rows the swing is worse - 0.760 at depth 384 down to 0.117 at depth 2976, an open void with almost no platforms versus near-solid rock only a digger passes.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Three compounding harms: it contradicts Downwell; it makes the Weight harsher exactly where terrain is slowest, since Forge needs 8.89 tiles/s through 0.378-air rock; and it silently ramps enemy density per unit playable space by +88% on top of depthMul 4.49x. Replace the depth ramp with a per-band air target as a sixth BIOMES field - caves 0.52, fungal 0.60, ruins 0.42 (deliberately architectural), forge 0.46, abyss 0.55 - plus a stanza modifier of +0.06 in Gallery, -0.10 in Seam, and a forced carve in Shaft. Controlled per-band texture instead of a monotone drift, and Ruins becomes tighter than Fungal as a design statement rather than an accident.

*Source: Measured from index.html:944-949 - high confidence*

#### MEASURED: nothing in a band is guaranteed, so runs lose their climaxes

**Numbers.** boss gate 0.042 per chunk; Caves about 30 corridor chunks giving lambda 1.26 and P(zero) 28%; band depths per column 6.9 / 10.4 / 14.6 / 16.7 / 15.4 rows

The boss arena gate is a hash below 0.045 per chunk, measured 0.042 on the corridor. The Caves band spans tiles 70-400 = 6.9 chunk rows, so a 5-wide corridor holds about 30 chunks and expects 1.26 arenas, making P(zero Warden in Caves) = e^-1.26 = 28%. My sampled corridor had 0 boss arenas in Caves and 0 shrines across the entire Ruins band of 70 chunks. Since threat tiers are gated on bosses killed, a run can simply fail to offer the progression content. Bands are also very uneven: per single chunk column they are 6.9 / 10.4 / 14.6 / 16.7 / 15.4 rows for caves, fungal, ruins, forge and abyss, so Forge is 2.4x the depth of Caves while sharing the same 90s grace.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Guarantee the climaxes and roll only the filler, as Slay the Spire guarantees floors 1, 9, 15, 16 and 17: exactly one boss arena per band at 78-88% band depth on the spine, one shrine per band immediately past the arena exit, one secret cache per band in the Seam stanza. Everything else stays probabilistic.

*Source: Measured from index.html:999-1006 and BIOMES at index.html:263-270 - high confidence*

#### BUG: boss arena side walls are never written

**Numbers.** 26x16 stamp; 28 perimeter tiles unwritten per arena; about 2.5% of chunks carry an arena

index.html:1002-1005 computes edge as (y===0 or y===rh-1 or x===0 or x===rw-1), then writes the ground tile only when edge AND (y===rh-1 or y===0), else writes air only when NOT edge. For a left or right edge column at a middle row (x===0 with y between 1 and rh-2), edge is true so the first branch's second condition fails, and NOT edge is false so the second branch is skipped - the tile keeps whatever the cave noise produced. On a 26x16 stamp that is 2 x 14 = 28 tiles of wall never built; in a 0.5-air chunk roughly half will be open.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Boss arenas leak: the boss walks or flies out, the player kites out and cheeses from outside, and in high-air chunks there is no lateral containment at all. Likely a live cause of boss fights feeling unstructured. Fix: write the ground tile for every edge tile and air otherwise. Add a suite-9 assertion that a generated arena perimeter is 100% solid.

*Source: index.html:1002-1005 - high confidence, read directly*

#### MEASURED: the Weight's flat grace is 2.4x harsher in Forge than in Caves

**Numbers.** grace 90s flat; band depths 330/500/700/800/740; required 3.67/5.56/7.78/8.89/8.22 tiles/s; cap at 252s per band; 6 x 252 = 1512s of capped farming available

WEIGHT_GRACE=90, WEIGHT_EVERY=18, WEIGHT_MAX=10 (index.html:246). Band depths are 330 / 500 / 700 / 800 / 740 tiles. Crossing a band inside the 90s grace requires 3.67 / 5.56 / 7.78 / 8.89 / 8.22 tiles per second, which is 58.7 / 88.9 / 124.4 / 142.2 / 131.6 px/s of net downward progress while fighting. Player horizontal speed is 170 px/s and free-fall safe terminal is 520 px/s, so meeting the Forge requirement means free-falling roughly 27% of the time with zero backtracking, through terrain that is 37.8% air. Meanwhile the 10-stack cap stops the penalty at -30% damage and +30% enemy speed, and 6 bands x 252s is about 25 minutes of ceiling-capped free farming per run.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Simultaneously too harsh at target pace in the deep bands and too weak against actual farming anywhere. Rework to run-time-based and uncapped with descent refunds. The refund model also taxes anchor-skipping for free: an Abyss-anchor start has skipped four band-descent refunds and has no bank, so the Weight lands far earlier than on a full-descent player. That closes the anchor degeneracy without a new system.

*Source: Derived from index.html:246, 263-270, 2009-2022 - high confidence*

#### PLAN: the descent spine and the beat grid

**Numbers.** spine step at most 1 chunk per row; POI radius 1 column; flood-fill 2304 cells per chunk; WORLD_W 1600 to 624; WORLD_H 3200 to 3168 (66 rows)

(1) SPINE: sx(cy) is a deterministic 1D random walk over chunk columns seeded from SEED, moving at most 1 chunk per row, clamped to the interior; one memoised function. Designed POIs stamp only within 1 column of the spine; off-spine chunks keep terrain, ore and ambient spawns but no chests, shrines, vaults, secrets or arenas. (2) CONNECTIVITY: after generating a spine chunk, flood-fill from the top opening and carve a 2-tile channel from any disconnected air region over about 12 tiles. (3) WORLD WIDTH: narrow WORLD_W from 1600 to 624 tiles (13 chunks) with bedrock edges - still 39 screens across, plenty of wander room for ore, but it stops 92% of designed output landing where nobody goes. (4) DEPTH: WORLD_H from 3200 to 3168 so it is exactly 66 chunk rows and band boundaries land on chunk boundaries. (5) BEAT GRID: every band is exactly five stanzas in fixed order, with contents randomised within type.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Additive - genChunk keeps its signature, the noise pass stays, and the stanza type just selects a different air target and POI stamp list. No new assets, no build step, and it does not violate the single-file rule.

*Source: My proposal, built on Spelunky's solution path and Dead Cells' per-biome graph*

#### PLAN: the five-stanza band and the combat/traversal/reward ratio

**Numbers.** stanza shares 15/25/20/25/15%; shaft at least 40 tiles with at most 3 tiles obstruction; 55% combat / 30% traversal / 15% reward and rest; 30 stanzas per run = 3 per minute versus Hades 1.1-1.8

Five stanzas in fixed order, sized as a share of band depth. S1 THRESHOLD (15%): one combat pocket of 3-4 grunts, the band's signature hazard appearing once in a survivable demonstration form, no reward. S2 GALLERY (25%): the band's most open terrain (air +0.06), two combat pockets, one guaranteed chest. S3 SEAM (20%): the most solid terrain (air -0.10), dig-and-route focus, the band's guaranteed secret cache, a sealed vault every other band, at most one grunt per pocket. S4 ARENA (25%): a walled 26x16 room holding the band boss (guaranteed, one per band) or an elite pocket, with the guaranteed shrine immediately past the exit. S5 SHAFT (15%): a guaranteed vertical shaft at least 40 tiles tall with at most 3 tiles of obstruction, zero enemy spawns, ending at the band boundary - the relief beat and the payoff for descending. Target wall-clock split in a 120s band: 15s / 30s / 25s / 35s / 15s, which is 55% combat, 30% traversal, 15% reward-and-rest. Thirty discrete beats over a 6-band run is 3 per minute at a 10-minute target; Hades runs 43-73 chambers over about 40 minutes for 1.1-1.8 per minute, but Shardfall's stanzas are smaller denser units so 3 per minute is the right analogue.

**Apply to Shardfall.** The stanza is also the natural unit for a forward-visibility readout, as in Slay the Spire's map and Hades' door icons: show the next stanza type and the one after, within the current band only. That converts the descent from a random walk into a route decision without exposing the map.

*Source: My proposal, benchmarked against the Hades chamber counts*

#### PLAN: band-by-band layout with times, depths and grace

**Numbers.** Rim 0-64 (20s) / Caves 64-448 (95s, 4.0 t/s) / Fungal 448-1024 (115s, 5.0) / Ruins 1024-1600 (115s, 5.0) / Forge 1600-2272 (120s, 5.6) / Abyss 2272-3040 (125s, 6.1) / Floor 3040-3168 (30s). 66 rows, 620s. Minimal alternative: per-band grace 90/110/140/140/115

Six bands plus a final arena, sized so descent ACCELERATES with depth (matching the player's growing movement kit) rather than bands getting longer while the timer stays flat. Rim/Surface 0-64 tiles, 1.3 chunk rows, 20s, safe, no Weight. Caves 64-448, 8 rows, 384 tiles, 95s, 4.0 tiles/s. Fungal Bloom 448-1024, 12 rows, 576 tiles, 115s, 5.0. Ruins 1024-1600, 12 rows, 576 tiles, 115s, 5.0. Forge 1600-2272, 14 rows, 672 tiles, 120s, 5.6. Abyss 2272-3040, 16 rows, 768 tiles, 125s, 6.1. The Floor 3040-3168, 2.7 rows, 30s, final arena. Total 66 chunk rows and 620s = 10.3 minutes, inside the 8-12 minute target with room for a slow player at about 14 and a rushed one at about 7. Each band boundary is a Noita-style chamber: carved, enemy-free, 20x12, holding the guaranteed shrine, the anchor stone and a heal to 70% max HP, and the only place the Weight refunds.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Close to existing boundaries of 400/900/1600/2400/3200 - Caves +48 tiles, Fungal +124, then redistribution, with every boundary on a chunk row. If you want the truly minimal patch instead, keep current boundaries and make WEIGHT_GRACE per-band at 90/110/140/140/115 instead of flat 90, derived from band tiles divided by target rate.

*Source: My proposal*

#### PLAN: biome mechanical identity and hazard tile shares

**Numbers.** Caves 3% shale, 0.4s collapse. Fungal 6% spore vent (5-tile puff per 3s, fuel drain 1.5x) plus 4% bounce cap (2x jump). Ruins 2% plate plus 5% hard brick (hardness 2), air 0.42. Forge 8% magma (1.0s grace, 6 to 14 hp/s over 3s) plus 3% steam vent (900 px/s, +25 fuel). Abyss 4% void seam (no collision, 2x gravity), air 0.55

CAVES, the dark and the ceiling: 3% unstable shale collapsing 0.4s after being stood under or dug adjacent to, light radius as the resource, self-illuminating ore veins. Teaches dodge and that terrain is not inert. FUNGAL BLOOM, the air is contested: 6% spore vents emitting a 5-tile gas puff every 3s that ticks poison and drains flight fuel at 1.5x, dispersed by fire or explosions, plus 4% bounce caps giving a 2x jump impulse; air target 0.60, the most open band, where vertical movement is free but breathing is not. Teaches area denial. RUINS, architecture rather than caves: air target 0.42 with room-stamp share raised so the band reads as corridors and chambers rather than noise, 2% pressure plates firing dart volleys or dropping portcullises, 5% hard brick at hardness 2 that only a digger weapon or Bore/Quake cracks, and falling straight down blocked so you must route. Teaches flanking, which archer and shieldman already want. FORGE, heat as a clock: 8% magma using the Asphodel model exactly, 1.0s contact grace then 6 hp/s ramping to 14 over 3s, plus 3% steam vents giving a 900 px/s upward impulse and +25 fuel; the floor is temporary and the updrafts are the compensation. Teaches burn management. ABYSS, no ground: air target 0.55, the second most open, 4% void seams with no collision, double gravity inside and light-blocking; wraiths already phase through terrain so rock is advisory; long shafts and freefall, and terminal-velocity landings matter. Teaches everything.

**Apply to Shardfall.** All five are tile types with one behaviour each, fitting the existing TILES table and CLAUDE.md's data-tables rule. Add fields for behaviour id and share (fraction of band solids) to tile entries plus a per-band hazard list in BIOMES. The only new system is a contact timer for shale and magma, which the existing status-array shape can carry on the player rather than on tiles.

*Source: My proposal; the magma grace is copied from Hades' Asphodel*

#### PLAN: POI density targets per full descent

**Numbers.** Per run: 25 combat pockets, about 100 grunts (1 kill per 6s), 17 chests (1 per 35s), 5 shrines plus 5 descent rolls, 2-3 vaults, 7 secrets (1 per 90s), 5 bosses, 10 elites, 5 shafts, about 77 beats over 620s = 1 per 8s. K_band 500/470/440/410/400 air tiles per grunt

Guaranteed per band: 1 boss arena in S4 at 78-88% band depth, 1 shrine in the boundary chamber past the arena, 1 chest in S2, 1 secret cache in S3, 1 shaft in S5. Rolled from the band budget: 1 extra chest at 50%, 1 sealed vault every other band, 1 extra secret at 40%, 2 elite pockets. Totals over 5 combat bands: 25 combat pockets at about 4 grunts each for roughly 100 grunts, one kill per 6s over 620s; 17 chests, one per 35s, against Hades' reward per chamber at one per 35-55s; 5 shrines plus up to 5 descent-bonus rolls; 2-3 vaults; 7 secrets at one per 90s; 5 boss arenas; 10 elite pockets; 5 shafts. Roughly 77 designed beats over 620s is one meaningful thing every 8 seconds. Enemy budget n = clamp(round(airTiles/K_band),0,6) with K_band 500/470/440/410/400, giving 2.00 to 2.50 enemies per 1000 air tiles - a deliberate +25% ramp against the current accidental +88% - with n=0 in Shaft stanzas and within 2 chunk rows of a shrine.

**Apply to Shardfall.** BOONS has only 12 entries and a run now offers up to 10 boon picks (5 shrines plus 5 descent bonuses) at 3 choices each, so draw without replacement per run or the last shrines offer nothing new; the same concern applies to 22 attunements against a 14-level curve. Weight each band's shrine toward its identity, Fungal toward ailments and Forge toward burn and armour, so the roll reinforces the biome instead of being uniform noise.

*Source: My proposal, calibrated against Dead Cells' 1-per-5 ratio and the Hades reward cadence*

#### PLAN: the Weight rework, with tuning arithmetic

**Numbers.** stacks = max(0, floor((weightT-90)/22)+1), uncapped; band refund 70s, boss refund 40s; +4% enemy HP per stack from stack 8; Hollow at stack 12, reached at 354s. 620s run: 530 accrued versus 550 refunded, 0 stacks; 800s run: 8 stacks. shardMul = depthMul x (0.55 + 0.09 x bandsThisRun), full descent x1.09 versus abyss start x0.73

Track RUN time, not band time, and remove the cap. weightT accumulates continuously, paused only at camp and in boundary chambers. stacks = max(0, floor((weightT - 90)/22) + 1), uncapped. Reaching a new band refunds 70s of weightT and killing a band boss refunds 40s. Per stack: -3% player damage and +3% enemy move speed as now, plus from stack 8 upward +4% enemy max HP per stack, uncapped, the RoR2 property. At stack 12 a HOLLOW spawns and pursues through terrain, unkillable and only outrun - the Spelunky ghost, the only thing that reliably ends a farm. Arithmetic: a 620s run accrues 530s and refunds 5x70 plus 5x40 = 550s, so a clean run ends at 0 stacks and never sees the mechanic. An 800s run accrues 710, refunds 550, and nets 160s for 8 stacks. A farmer sitting in one band accrues without refunding and reaches stack 12 at 90 + 12x22 = 354s, under six minutes.

**Apply to Shardfall.** The anchor interaction needs no new code: an Abyss-anchor start has only 1 band descent and 1 boss left to refund, 110s of bank instead of 550s, so the Weight lands within about three minutes and deep starts stop being free. Pair with a shard multiplier keyed to bands traversed this run rather than absolute depth - shardMul = depthMul(depth) x (0.55 + 0.09 x bandsThisRun) - so a full 6-band descent earns x1.09 while an Abyss start reaching the Floor earns x0.73, a 49% advantage to the full descent at equal depth. That is the concrete answer to the anti-degenerate check DESIGN-PLAN section 5 asks for.

*Source: My proposal; uncapped scaling from RoR2, terminal pursuer from Spelunky*

### Recommendations

- Fix the boss arena wall bug first - one condition, and it affects every boss fight. index.html:1002-1005 leaves 28 perimeter tiles per 26x16 arena as raw cave noise because the left and right edge columns at middle rows fall through both branches. Write the ground tile for every edge tile and air otherwise.
- Invert the depth/air relationship. index.html:948 claims caves get denser with depth and the code does the opposite (air 0.564 to 0.348). Replace the depth-ramped threshold with a per-band air target as a sixth BIOMES field - caves 0.52, fungal 0.60, ruins 0.42, forge 0.46, abyss 0.55 - plus a stanza modifier of +0.06 in Gallery, -0.10 in Seam and a forced carve in Shaft.
- Stop dropping POIs silently. Chest and shrine loops try 14 random positions for a strict tile signature and break with nothing, measured success 20.8% and 24.6%. Either carve a 3x3 pocket at the least-bad candidate on failure, which makes gate rate equal placement rate, or move to the stanza-schedule model. The secret cache is worse: it tests a 7x6 region for being fully solid (39.6% success) while the sealed vault eight lines above carves unconditionally - make them consistent.
- Add a descent spine: a deterministic 1D random walk over chunk columns moving at most 1 chunk per row, with designed POIs stamping only within 1 column of it. The world is 33.3 chunks wide and the player touches 2-3, so about 92% of designed content generates where nobody goes, which is also why per-band POI counts are Poisson-noisy. This is Spelunky's solution path rotated 90 degrees and costs one memoised function.
- Guarantee the climaxes and roll only the filler: exactly one boss arena per band at 78-88% band depth, one shrine per band in the boundary chamber, one secret per band in the Seam stanza. The boss gate is 4.2% per chunk over about 30 Caves corridor chunks, so 28% of runs contain no Warden while threat tiers are gated on boss kills. Slay the Spire guarantees floors 1, 9, 15, 16 and 17 for exactly this reason.
- Rework the Weight to run-time-based and uncapped with descent refunds: stacks = max(0, floor((weightT-90)/22)+1), band descent refunds 70s, boss kill refunds 40s, +4% enemy HP per stack from stack 8, unkillable Hollow pursuer at stack 12. A 620s target-pace run accrues 530s and refunds 550s so it never sees the mechanic, while a farmer hits the Hollow at 354s. One-line alternative: make WEIGHT_GRACE per-band at 90/110/140/140/115 instead of flat 90, since the flat grace demands 3.67 tiles/s in Caves and 8.89 in Forge.
- Make secret walls visible. index.html:260 gives tile 10 the colour #5a5f66, byte-identical to stone at index.html:253, so the secret wall is literally invisible and the only way to perceive one is spending an armour socket on Prospector. Give it #5f6167 plus a deterministic 1px lighter diagonal hairline - one extra fillRect in the chunk bake, zero per-frame cost - and one particle every 0.6s within 4 tiles. Keep Prospector as the from-anywhere minimap reveal.
- Raise FALL_SAFE from 520 to 760 px/s and add a dive attack. With GRAV=1500 the safe fall height is 90px = 5.6 tiles, less than a viewport, so in a descent game the core action is taxed by default and the only relief is an aura slot. At 760 it becomes 12 tiles. Then make holding down above 700 px/s convert the landing into a 3-tile shockwave at weapon damage x1.4 with no fall damage, and move Featherfall from an aura to a meta-tree node.
- Budget enemies against air volume, not chunk count. index.html:962 spawns a flat 1-3 per 48x48 chunk regardless of solidity, producing a measured +88% density ramp per unit playable space caves-to-abyss on top of depthMul 4.49x. Use n = clamp(round(airTiles/K_band),0,6) with K_band 500/470/440/410/400 for a deliberate +25% ramp, and n=0 in Shaft stanzas and near shrines. Dead Cells' shipped ratio is 1 monster per 5 combat-eligible tiles.
- Give the descent punctuation: a Noita-style boundary chamber at every band edge - carved, enemy-free, 20x12, holding the band's guaranteed shrine, the anchor stone and a heal to 70% - and make it the only place the Weight refunds. That is the relief beat, the reward choice, the checkpoint feel and the anchor placement from one room.
- Show the reward before the room. Hades puts the icon on the door and Slay the Spire shows the whole act, while Shardfall's minimap is fog-of-war so the player has no forward information at all. A readout naming the next one or two stanza types within the current band turns the descent from a random walk into a route decision, and costs nothing because the schedule is deterministic.
- Draw boons and attunements without replacement. With one guaranteed shrine per band plus the descent bonus, a run offers up to 10 boon picks against a 12-entry BOONS table at 3 choices each. Weight each band's shrine toward its identity so the roll reinforces the biome.
- Itemise the threat tiers the way Hades itemises Heat: replace six bundled tiers with named per-rank axes - Armored +3 armor x3, Swift +8% enemy speed x3, Teeming +20% density x3, Buried -15% light x2, Impatient -20s Weight grace x3 - and derive the shard and rarity multiplier from total rank. Supergiant caps enemy-HP scaling at +30% while Shardfall's depthMul already multiplies HP by 4.49x at the floor.
- Add test/suite-9.js for world structure: generate 500 chunks at a fixed seed and assert placed-POI counts per band, exactly one boss arena per band, every arena perimeter 100% solid, every spine chunk's air connected to the top opening, and per-band air fraction within 0.05 of target. The harness has caught a real bug every session and none of these would have survived it.
- Consider narrowing WORLD_W from 1600 to 624 tiles (13 chunks, still 39 screens across) and WORLD_H to 3168 so it is exactly 66 chunk rows. Band boundaries then land on chunk boundaries, the spine's wander becomes meaningful instead of lost in a 94-screen field, and the existing POI budget concentrates about 11x onto chunks the player visits.

### Caveats and unsourced claims

- The stanza schedule makes runs legible but also repeatable - after ten runs a player knows a Shaft precedes every band boundary and the shrine is past the arena. Randomise hard WITHIN type (arena shape, gallery topology, seam dig route) and occasionally swap two stanza positions, as Slay the Spire keeps 22% of nodes Unknown until entry.
- Guaranteeing one boss per band means five forced fights at a 10-minute target, one every two minutes. If boss TTK is high that alone blows the budget - voidmaw is 1200 base x 4.49 depthMul = 5387 HP at the floor. Check against realistic DPS, or make the band boss skippable-but-signposted so run length stays elastic.
- The Weight rework makes target-pace players never see the mechanic. That is arguably correct for a farm-killer, but it is invisible until it bites, and a player killed by a Hollow at minute nine has no model of why. Needs a visible bank indicator, or lower refunds so a normal run ends around 3-4 stacks.
- Narrowing WORLD_W is a save-compatibility hazard: anchors store depth not column so they should survive, but any stored x outside the new width needs clamping in migrate(). CLAUDE.md rule 2 forbids discarding old saves.
- Per-band air targets need the noise threshold solved per band - either a hardcoded lookup table, which drifts if the noise function changes, or a cheap sampling calibration at boot costing a few thousand noise calls. Measure on a phone first, per DESIGN-PLAN's own note about the chunk-canvas blit bottleneck.
- The flood-fill connectivity pass on spine chunks says nothing about the two flanking columns where POIs also stamp, so a chest can still generate in a sealed pocket. Either extend it to the flanking columns, tripling a trivial cost, or accept that flanking POIs may need a digger - arguably a feature, since the sealed-vault design already assumes one.
- Five hazard tile types is five new behaviours, and CLAUDE.md rule 12 is explicit that scope should not be added unrequested. If only one lands, make it Forge magma: most mechanically distinct, with a shipped reference to copy in Hades' 1.0s grace, and Forge has the worst timer-versus-terrain mismatch.
- Several doc numbers do not match the code: design/CURRENT-STATE.md and the header comment claim 1 shrine per 30 chunks and 1 boss per 26, while measured corridor rates are 1 per 23 and 1 per 40, and 1 chest per 5.5 chunks is only true as the emergent sum of three sources, two of which fail 75-80% of the time. Regenerate the audit after any change or the design tables keep encoding accidents as intent.


<a id="feel"></a>
## Game feel and UX polish

### Summary

Research pass on measurable game feel for SHARDFALL, built mostly from primary sources I mined directly rather than secondhand articles: Hades' shipped Lua scripts (cloned to /tmp/claude-1000/-workspaces-Sweet-Spot/00ff769b-1474-43f1-895c-6b76d1865b29/scratchpad/hades/Scripts), Celeste's released Player.cs and the Celeste Classic C# port (/tmp/claude-1000/-workspaces-Sweet-Spot/00ff769b-1474-43f1-895c-6b76d1865b29/scratchpad/celeste), SmashWiki's datamined hitlag formulas, Eiserloh's GDC 2016 camera-math slides, and the arXiv "impact feel" study. Headline verdicts on your eight numbers: coyote 0.1s is EXACTLY Celeste's JumpGraceTime — keep it. Camera lerp 5 is within 8% of Celeste's effective rate (4.605/s) — keep the constant, fix the integrator to be frame-rate independent. Jump buffer 0.12s is fine but is being under-used (buffer attack and dash too). Everything else needs work: your hitstop is a hard freeze when it should be a partial time-scale drop with a 1-frame pre-delay and asymmetric player-vs-enemy values; your screenshake peak amplitude (16px on a 272px-wide view = 5.9% of screen width) is roughly 4x Celeste Classic and 15x Hades and is decaying linearly rather than on a squared/cubed trauma curve; 17 tiles of camera width is very tight for a game with flight and ranged enemies; and a 6-voice audio budget will comb-filter on multi-hit frames. One structural finding dominates the whole report: in Hades, ordinary combat hits do NOT shake the camera at all — they shake the enemy sprite (Distance 6, Speed 550, Duration 0.15) and flash it red for 30ms. Of 207 enemy entries in EnemyData.lua, exactly 3 shake the screen on death, and all 3 are explosive units.

### Findings (21)

#### Hitstop — fighting game reference values (the upper bound)

**Numbers.** Street Fighter V: light 8f / medium 12f / heavy 15f (133/200/250ms at 60fps). Guilty Gear Xrd -SIGN-: light attacks freeze 7 frames (117ms), heavy ~10 frames (167ms). Smash Ultimate hitlag: floor((d*0.65+6) * h * e * s) frames, capped at 30f (500ms); electric hits x1.5; shielding x0.67. Melee: floor(d/3+3), cap 20f. Brawl/Smash4: floor(d*0.3846+5), cap 30f.

Fighting games are the ceiling for hitstop because they have low hit density (one exchange per ~0.5-1s). The Smash formula is the most useful artifact here: hitlag is a LINEAR FUNCTION OF DAMAGE with a floor (+6 frames = 100ms minimum) and a hard cap (30 frames). Note the per-hitbox multiplier `h`, which lets designers override the formula per move — Marth's tipper is 1.25x, his non-sweetspot 0.7x, Kazuya's attacks 0.2-0.6x. So even in a formula-driven game, the designer keeps a per-attack knob. The SFV 8/12/15 numbers are documented on block; I could not verify the on-hit values, so treat the on-hit/on-block split as unconfirmed.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Your three fixed values (0.05 / 0.08 / 0.18) ignore damage magnitude entirely — a 7-damage tickle and a 900-damage crit feel identical. Replace with a formula plus a per-source override, the Smash way. Proposal (mine): hitstopSec = clamp(0.020 + 0.14 * sqrt(dmg / targetMaxHP), 0.020, 0.16), then multiply by a per-gem/per-weapon `hitstopMul` defaulting to 1.0 with a documented range of 0.2 to 1.6. The sqrt matters: it makes the first 10% of a target's HP buy most of the feel, so chip damage still registers without letting a 4x crit produce a 600ms lockup. Cap at 0.16s for anything that is not a boss kill.

*Source: ssbwiki.com/Hitlag (datamined, high confidence); arXiv 2208.06155 'What Features Influence Impact Feel?' citing Guilty Gear Xrd -SIGN- 7f/10f (medium confidence, secondhand observation); shoryuken.com 2016-06-07 'Hitstop in Street Fighter V' via search snippet (medium confidence)*

#### Hitstop — Hades does NOT freeze; it drops sim time-scale, after a delay, asymmetrically

**Numbers.** 242 HitSimSlowParameters blocks in WeaponData.lua. Player weapons on enemies: ScreenPreWait 0.01-0.06s at full speed, THEN Fraction 0.05-0.30 (usually 0.10), held 0.01-0.07s, then LerpTime 0.02-0.10s back to 1.0. Enemy weapons hitting the PLAYER: ScreenPreWait 0.02, Fraction 0.01 (99% stopped), held 0.08-0.16s (commonly 0.14), then instant return. Examples — SwordWeapon: {pre 0.04, f 0.1}, {pre 0.01, f 1.0, lerp 0.03}. SpearWeapon: {pre 0.02, f 0.1}, {pre 0.02, f 1.0, lerp 0.02}. FistWeapon: {pre 0.02, f 0.01}, {pre 0.02, f 1.0, lerp 0}. BloodlessMelee (enemy hitting you): {pre 0.02, f 0.01}, {pre 0.14, f 1.0, lerp 0}. Fraction distribution across all 242 blocks: 156 uses of 0.01, 53 of 0.25, 33 of 0.10, 11 of 0.02. Critical/important hits add criticalSlowHoldTime = 0.04s. Weapons carry a HitSimSlowCooldown, and AddSimSpeedChange early-outs if any sim-speed change is already active.

Four techniques here, all cheap to implement and all measurable. (1) PRE-DELAY: the impact frame plays at full speed for 10-60ms BEFORE the slow starts. This is why Hades reads as 'the hit landed then time bent' rather than 'the game stuttered'. (2) PARTIAL not FULL: player-dealt hits go to 10% speed, not 0%. Particles, VFX and the swing keep crawling forward, which reads as weight; a hard freeze reads as a dropped frame. (3) ASYMMETRY: taking damage gets a near-total freeze (1% speed) held ~140ms — roughly 4-14x longer than dealing damage. Getting hit is supposed to be jarring. (4) MUTUAL EXCLUSION: only one sim-speed change at a time, plus a per-weapon cooldown, so a 5-projectile volley cannot stack into a slideshow. Also note LastKill: AddSimSpeedChange('LastKill', {Fraction=0.35, LerpTime=0.08}) held ~0.5s — the room-clear is a slow-mo TAIL, not a longer freeze.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Rewrite hitstop as a global `timeScale` on the sim, not a hard pause, with four fields per event: preDelay, fraction, hold, lerpBack. Proposed table — light hit {0.016, 0.25, 0.020, 0.030}; medium {0.016, 0.15, 0.040, 0.050}; heavy or crit {0.024, 0.08, 0.070, 0.070}; grunt kill {0.016, 0.10, 0.060, 0.060}; miniboss kill {0.024, 0.02, 0.130, 0.120}; boss kill {0.030, 0.005, 0.220, 0.250} followed by a 0.35x tail for 0.5s; PLAYER TAKES DAMAGE {0.020, 0.02, 0.120, 0.060}. Your current 0.05/0.08/0.18 map roughly onto medium/heavy/miniboss, so the magnitudes are not crazy — the missing pieces are the pre-delay, the partial fraction, and the fact that you appear to have no distinct 'player got hit' hitstop, which is the single biggest miss. Enforce max() not sum(), and a 0.08s per-source cooldown. Critically: hitstop must scale the SIM only. Damage numbers, screen flashes and audio must keep running at real time — Hades spawns damage text on an object literally named 'BlankObstacleNoTimeModifier' and drifts it with TimeModifierFraction = 1.0 precisely so the numbers do not freeze with the world.

*Source: Hades shipped Lua, WeaponData.lua and CombatPresentation.lua (function DoWeaponHitSlow / AddSimSpeedChange), via github.com/xuqifzz/hades-mod-tutorial. High confidence — this is the game's own data.*

#### Hitstop — Celeste's freeze values (a low-hit-density platformer, the lower bound)

**Numbers.** Celeste (Player.cs, released source): Celeste.Freeze(0.05f) on DashBegin (guarded by `if (Engine.TimeRate > 0.25f)`), Celeste.Freeze(0.05f) on RedDashBegin, Celeste.Freeze(0.1f) on ExplodeLaunch, Celeste.Freeze(0.05f) on dream-block exit, Celeste.Freeze(0.01f) when smashing a crystal spinner. That is the complete set — five call sites. Celeste Classic (PICO-8, 30fps): freeze=2 frames (67ms) on dash, freeze=10 frames (333ms) when collecting the double-jump crystal.

Celeste freezes on the PLAYER'S OWN empowered action (dash), not on damage — there is no combat. The 0.01s crystal-smash freeze is interesting: 10ms is barely a frame, and it is there purely to punctuate a destruction event without interrupting momentum. Note the guard on the dash freeze: it is skipped when the game is already in slow motion, i.e. the same mutual-exclusion rule Hades uses. Also note Celeste never freezes for longer than 100ms in normal play; the 333ms freeze in Classic is a one-time collectible.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Your 0.18s boss-kill freeze is longer than anything Celeste does in normal play and longer than Hades' near-freeze on player damage. That is defensible for a boss kill, but it should not be a flat freeze — split it into 0.22s at 0.5% speed then a 0.35x slow-mo tail for 0.5s, which is what Hades does for its LastKill. Also add a 0.01s freeze on terrain destruction events (Noita-style digging) — it is nearly free and it is the exact trick Celeste uses for the crystal spinner.

*Source: github.com/NoelFB/Celeste Source/Player/Player.cs lines 3449, 3839, 3985, 4239, 5000; Source/PICO-8/Classic.cs lines 356, 1124. High confidence — official released source.*

#### Screenshake — the Vlambeer talk is qualitative, and that matters for how you cite it

**Numbers.** The talk is a 32-step ordered progression with timestamps and no published numeric values: 7:24 baseline, 8:28 animations+sound, 9:03 lower enemy HP, 9:28 higher rate of fire, 9:45 more enemies, 10:13 bigger bullets, 10:39 muzzle flash, 10:58 faster bullets, 11:19 less accuracy, 11:46 impact effects, 12:10 hit animation, 12:42 enemy knockback, 13:18 permanence, 14:49 camera lerp, 15:27 camera position offset, 16:36 screen shake, 17:15 player knockback, 18:06 sleep (hitstop), 19:52 gun delay, 20:01 gun kickback, 20:23 strafing, 20:36 shell casings, 21:43 more bass, 23:16 machine-gun mode, 23:54 random explosions, 23:59 faster enemies, 24:40 more enemies, 25:09 higher RoF + camera kick, 26:47 bigger explosions, 27:54 increased permanence/smoke, 29:04 meaning/stakes, 31:00 balancing.

I checked the talk itself, two community recreations (colinbellino/screenshake, dkliao's itch recreation with an effects breakdown), and transcript-annotation pages. None publish amplitude, duration or frame values — the recreations are all on/off GIF comparisons. So anyone quoting 'Vlambeer says shake N pixels' is inventing it. What the talk DOES give you, and what is genuinely valuable, is the ORDER OF OPERATIONS: screen shake arrives at step 16 of 32, AFTER impact effects, hit animation, knockback, permanence and camera lerp are already in. Shake is a late garnish on a stack that already reads without it. Note also step 15 (camera position offset, i.e. framing the camera ahead of the player) is listed as a separate, earlier win than shake.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Do not cite Vlambeer for numbers. Do cite the ordering as an audit: before you tune shake amplitude at all, confirm you have impact sprites at the hit point, a hit animation/flash on the enemy, knockback, and permanence (corpses, scorch marks, embedded projectiles, blood on your destructible tiles). Your world is destructible — permanence is close to free and it is step 13, three steps before shake. In a game rendering flat fillRect quads, the single highest-leverage item on this list is step 11 (hit animation) and step 13 (permanence), not shake.

*Source: youtube.com/watch?v=AJdEqssNZ-U (INDIGO Classes 2013); timestamped step list from theengineeringofconsciousexperience.com; effects breakdown at dkliao.itch.io. High confidence on the ordering, high confidence that no numbers were published.*

#### Screenshake — the trauma model with real decay/exponent numbers

**Numbers.** Eiserloh, GDC 2016: maintain trauma in [0,1]; events add trauma (examples given: += 0.2, += 0.5); trauma decreases LINEARLY over time; final shake magnitude = trauma^2 or trauma^3. His worked examples are cubic: trauma 0.30 -> 3% shake, 0.60 -> 22%, 0.90 -> 73% (0.3^3=0.027, 0.6^3=0.216, 0.9^3=0.729). Recommends BOTH translational and rotational shake in 2D. Recommends Perlin noise over per-frame random. Camera smoothing weight guide at 60fps: 0.01 = slow, 0.1 = reasonably fast, 0.5 = incredibly fast.

The exponent is the whole trick. With a linear amplitude ramp (what you have), a shake spends most of its life in the mushy mid-range where it is visible but meaningless. With trauma^2 or ^3, small events are effectively invisible and large events dominate, and the DECAY reads as a snap rather than a fade. The rotational component is what separates 'the camera is jittering' from 'the world got hit' — a 2D translational-only shake reads as a rendering glitch. Perlin/smooth noise matters for a second reason he does not mention: per-frame random at 60fps is 30Hz white noise, which is squarely in the photosensitivity danger band.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Replace 'amplitude 2-16 decaying at 30/s' with a trauma model. Proposed constants (mine): maxShakePx = 8 world px, maxShakeDeg = 1.0, amplitude = maxShakePx * trauma^2, angle = maxShakeDeg * trauma^2, trauma -= 1.5 * dt. Trauma bumps: player takes damage 0.30; miniboss kill 0.45; boss kill 0.85; quake 0.55; explosion 0.35 * 1/(1 + (dist/(viewW*0.75))^2); GRUNT KILL 0.0. That yields boss kill = 5.8px (2.1% of your 272px view, matching Celeste Classic's 1.6%) decaying to zero in 0.57s, and player-hit = 0.72px decaying in 0.20s. Use quadratic rather than Eiserloh's cubic because your view is small and cubic would make mid-tier events invisible. Sample the noise at ~18-25Hz (three summed sines at 14/23/37 Hz is enough and is 6 lines of code) rather than Math.random() per frame. Round the final offset to whole device pixels so your 16px tiles do not shimmer.

*Source: mathforgameprogrammers.com/gdc2016/GDC2016_Eiserloh_Squirrel_JuicingYourCameras.pdf and the archive.org transcript of the talk. High confidence.*

#### Screenshake — measured amplitudes in shipped games, as a fraction of screen width

**Numbers.** Hades (1920x1080 native): ordinary combat = NO camera shake. ShakeScreen Distance values across all shipped scripts: 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 15, 30. The common combat/room values are Distance 4-6, Speed 500-600, FalloffSpeed 1000-2000, Duration 0.3-1.5. Biggest routine one is Distance 15 / Speed 900 / Duration 4 / Falloff 1400 (Cerberus assist). Distance 8 / 1920 = 0.42% of screen width; Distance 15 = 0.78%. Celeste Classic (128x128): `E.camera(-2 + E.rnd(5), -2 + E.rnd(5))` — a uniform integer offset in roughly [-2, +2] px, i.e. ±1.6% of screen width, held for a fixed frame count with NO falloff (shake=6 on dash, shake=5 on spring/bounce, shake=10 on death). Shake is gated by `!Settings.Instance.DisableScreenShake`. Hades enemy death shake: only 3 of 207 EnemyData entries define OnDeathShakeScreenSpeed, all explosive (BlastCubeFused 350/8/0.65/1500, GunBombUnit 150/4/0.25/1500, BlastCubeFusedRegenerating 350/8/0.65/1500). Player death: OnDeathShakeScreenSpeed 250, Distance 5, Duration 0.25, Angle 90.

Two independent shipped games, twelve years apart, one pixel-art and one 1080p, converge on 0.4-2% of screen width for the biggest routine shake. Hades' player-death shake specifies Angle = 90, i.e. purely vertical and directional, not random — a semantically meaningful axis. The rarity number is the striking one: 3 out of 207 enemies. What Hades does instead on every hit is DoUnitShake — `Shake({Id = victim, Distance = 6, Speed = 550, Duration = 0.15})` — it shakes the ENEMY SPRITE 6px for 150ms — plus DoUnitHitFlash — `Flash({Speed = 0.85, MinFraction = 1.0, MaxFraction = 0.0, Color = Color.Red, Duration = 0.03, ExpireAfterCycle = true})` — a 30ms red flash on the victim.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Your peak amplitude of 16 on a 272px-wide view is 5.9% of screen width: ~4x Celeste Classic's peak and ~14x Hades' typical combat value. At 16px tiles that is a full tile of camera displacement, which on a destructible-terrain game will make it genuinely hard to read where the ground is. Cap at 8 world px (2.9%) for the single biggest event in the game and 6px (2.2%) for everything else. Then — the bigger change — stop shaking the camera on ordinary hits at all. Port Hades' two-part local feedback instead: offset the enemy's own fillRect by up to 2-3px for 0.12-0.15s, and flash it (draw the quad in a bright tint) for 30ms. Both are trivially cheap on flat quads and both are strictly better feedback than camera motion because they tell you WHICH enemy got hit. With 19 enemy types and swarms, camera shake cannot disambiguate; a local flash can. Also adopt Hades' directional angle: give shake a semantic axis (vertical for quakes/slams, along the damage vector for hits) rather than isotropic random.

*Source: Hades WeaponData.lua / EnemyData.lua / HeroData.lua / CombatPresentation.lua (grep for ShakeScreen, DoUnitShake, DoUnitHitFlash, OnDeathShakeScreen); Celeste Classic.cs line 1498-1504. High confidence — both are shipped game data.*

#### Screenshake — when NOT to shake (rules, with thresholds)

**Numbers.** Distance falloff (my proposal): trauma *= 1 / (1 + (dist / (viewWidth * 0.75))^2), so an event one screen-width away contributes ~26% and two screen-widths ~13%. Repeat-suppression window: 150ms, using max() not sum(). Continuous sources: cap at 0.6px constant amplitude, no trauma accumulation.

The cases where shake actively hurts, in rough order of severity for a game like yours: (1) during precision platforming or flight through tight terrain — Celeste shakes on dash but the dash also freezes for 2 frames first, so the shake lands in a window where the player has no meaningful control; (2) repeated shakes from the same source (a multi-projectile volley, a DoT tick, an aura pulse) — you have 62 gems including auras and supports, so multi-hit is your default case, not an edge case; (3) off-screen events — a quake or a kill outside the view shaking the camera reads as a bug; (4) while any menu/inventory overlay is open; (5) player death — Celeste Classic shakes 10 frames on death but the player has no control then; if your death is a 'scene change' Dead Cells-style, freeze the camera instead so the death frame is readable; (6) when the OS reduced-motion preference is set.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Add a `requestShake(trauma, sourcePos, axis)` funnel that applies distance falloff, the 150ms max()-window, a reduced-motion check and a menu check before touching trauma. This is one function and it eliminates the entire class of 'why is the screen vibrating constantly' bugs that auras and DoTs will otherwise produce. Specifically: your aura gems and any 'increased' damage-over-time must go through a path that CANNOT add trauma.

*Source: Synthesis. The distance-falloff formula, the 150ms window and the 0.6px continuous cap are my proposals, not sourced. The 'shake only when the player has no control' observation is inferred from Celeste's dash (Freeze 0.05s then shake) and from Hades gating shake to deaths and room events.*

#### Input feel — Celeste's exact constants (your coyote value is already correct)

**Numbers.** Celeste Player.cs: JumpGraceTime = 0.1f (coyote time), VarJumpTime = 0.2f (variable jump-height hold window), CeilingVarJumpGrace = 0.05f, JumpSpeed = -105f, JumpHBoost = 40f, MaxRun = 90f, RunAccel = 1000f, RunReduce = 400f, AirMult = 0.65f, Gravity = 900f, HalfGravThreshold = 40f, MaxFall = 160f, FastMaxFall = 240f, FastMaxAccel = 300f, DashSpeed = 240f, EndDashSpeed = 160f, DashTime = 0.15f, DashCooldown = 0.2f, DashRefillCooldown = 0.1f, WallSpeedRetentionTime = 0.06f, WallJumpForceTime = 0.16f, WallSlideTime = 1.2f, WallSlideStartMax = 20f, DuckFriction = 500f, UpwardCornerCorrection = 4. Celeste Classic (30fps): grace = 6 frames = 200ms, jbuffer = 4 frames = 133ms.

Celeste ships coyote time at exactly 0.1s — identical to yours. Note that the full Celeste HALVED the Classic value (200ms -> 100ms): the tighter, more precise game uses less forgiveness. Two constants nobody talks about but which do enormous work: CeilingVarJumpGrace = 0.05f (if you bonk your head within 50ms of jumping, you keep variable jump height rather than getting a truncated hop), and UpwardCornerCorrection = 4 (a 4px horizontal nudge to slip past a ceiling corner — on Celeste's 8px-wide player hitbox that is 50% of the player's width). HalfGravThreshold = 40 is the classic 'float at the apex' trick: gravity is halved when |vy| < 40, which is what makes the peak of the jump feel controllable. AirMult = 0.65 means air control is 65% of ground control, not 100% and not 0%.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Keep coyote at 0.1s — you match Celeste exactly and there is no reason to move. But you are missing the constants that do the invisible work: (1) add corner correction at 5px, which on your 12px-wide player is ~42% of width, the Celeste ratio; (2) add a ceiling variable-jump grace of 0.05s; (3) add an apex half-gravity threshold; (4) since you have FLIGHT (Noita influence), add a 0.06-0.10s grace after flight fuel runs out before gravity resumes at full — the flight equivalent of coyote time, and the single most likely source of 'the game cut my flight off' complaints. Your player is 12x20 on 16px tiles, so the hitbox is 0.75 x 1.25 tiles — that 0.75 width means corner correction is essential or players will clip on tile edges constantly in destructible terrain.

*Source: github.com/NoelFB/Celeste Source/Player/Player.cs (official release); Source/PICO-8/Classic.cs lines 226, 235 and lemon32767/ccleste celeste.c lines 717, 726. High confidence.*

#### Input feel — buffering windows and what breaks

**Numbers.** Celeste Classic jbuffer = 4 frames @30fps = 133ms. Super Smash Bros. Ultimate: 9-frame hit-buffer window (150ms), reduced from Smash 4's 10 frames, PLUS hold-buffering (hold an input and it fires the moment it becomes legal, unbounded). Street Fighter 6: general move buffer 4 frames early (a 5-frame window, ~83ms); quarter-circle motion buffer 11 frames; half-circle 12 frames (up from 8 in earlier titles); 360/full-circle 32 frames (up from 25).

Two distinct mechanisms get conflated. PRESS-buffering (remember a press for N ms and fire it when legal) is the 100-150ms band. HOLD-buffering (Smash Ultimate) has no window at all — you hold the button and it fires at the first legal frame — and is strictly more forgiving but removes the ability to deliberately not-act. The failure modes are asymmetric: too SHORT and the game feels like it drops inputs (players describe this as lag even when latency is fine); too LONG and you get phantom actions — you tapped jump while falling, changed your mind, landed, and the character jumps anyway. From the values above the practical band is 80-160ms, with 130-150ms as the centre for action games and ~85ms for competitive precision. Above ~200ms the phantom-action problem becomes reportable. SF6's escalating buffers by input complexity is the useful generalisation: the more complex or higher-commitment the action, the longer the buffer it deserves.

**Apply to Shardfall.** 0.12s (120ms) is squarely in the good band — keep it. The problem is scope: you list it as 'jump buffer'. In a roguelite with 62 gems, buffering must cover attack, dash, gem-cast and interact, not just jump. Proposed per-action windows (mine, following SF6's escalate-by-commitment logic): jump 0.12s, dash 0.12s, basic attack 0.10s, skill-gem cast 0.16s, ability-gem/ultimate 0.20s, interact/pickup 0.25s. Additionally, buffer must survive hitstop — if a 0.12s buffer is consumed by ticking down during a 0.16s heavy-hit hitstop, every input during a big hit gets eaten. Freeze buffer timers while timeScale < 1, or measure them in unscaled time. This is a real bug you will have right now if hitstop pauses your whole update loop.

*Source: ssbwiki.com/Buffer; eventhubs / SuperCombo wiki for SF6 buffer frames (medium confidence — community-datamined); Celeste Classic source (high confidence).*

#### Damage numbers — Hades' complete shipped spec

**Numbers.** Normal hit: font RandomInt(30, 35) px on a 1920x1080 screen (2.8-3.2% of screen height), multiplied by a magnitude scale from GameData.DamageNumberTextScales = {0-19: 0.8}, {20-49: 0.9}, {50-99: 1.0}, {100-399: 1.2}, {400-999: 1.4}, {1000-99999: 1.6}. Crit: font RandomInt(56, 62) — 1.75-1.9x the base — with the raw text suffixed '!', ShadowBlur 2, shadow offset {2,2}, OutlineThickness 1 black, colour white. Spawn jitter: OffsetX RandomInt(-10,10), OffsetY RandomInt(-3,3), anchored at victim.HealthBarOffsetY or -180. Layout: numValuesPerRow = 3, spacerY = 40px between rows, 65px between columns, alternating left/right by index parity. Lifetime: hold CombatUI.DamageTextHoldTime = 0.25s attached to the victim, then unattach and drift OffsetX RandomInt(-120,120) / OffsetY -50 over 1.0s (crit: -100 over 2.0s) with EaseIn 0.99 EaseOut 0.1; at +0.1s tween ScaleTarget 0.75 over 0.4s and ColorTarget to black (crit: ScaleTarget 0.5 over 0.15s, ColorTarget yellow over 0.5s); then wait 0.2s (crit 0.45s); then FadeTarget 0 over 0.25s; destroy. Total lifetime ~1.55s normal, ~2.05s crit. COALESCING: numbers are keyed by (victimId, damageSourceName) where damageSourceName is the weapon genus or effect name; while a key's state == 'Hold', new damage ADDS to TargetNumber, ModifyTextBox rewrites the existing number, PulseCombatText fires, older duplicate ids are destroyed, and the thread waits 0.5s. Crits bypass coalescing (damageProjectileSourceName is set to nil). Gated behind ConfigOptionCache.ShowDamageNumbers. CombatUI.DamageTweenSpeed = 0.5.

Every readability decision here is a measurable one. The magnitude scale is a 2x span (0.8 to 1.6) across five orders of magnitude of damage — deliberately compressive, so a 5000 hit is only twice the size of a 10 hit rather than 500x. Crit is signalled THREE ways simultaneously and none of them is hue-only: 1.78x size, an appended '!' glyph, and a dedicated /SFX/CriticalHit sound. Colour arrives late (it tweens to yellow at +0.1s) — colour is the confirmation, not the signal. The single best readability trick is that numbers SHRINK to 0.75x and darken toward black 100ms after spawning: recent numbers are big and bright, older ones visually recede, so a screen with 15 numbers still parses. And coalescing per damage-SOURCE rather than per-hit is what makes a DoT/aura build legible at all.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Your view is 272 world px wide by ~153 tall. Hades' base font is 2.8-3.2% of screen height; applied naively that gives you a 4.5px font, which is unreadable. So damage numbers must be drawn in SCREEN space at a fixed CSS pixel size, not scaled with the world. Proposed spec (Hades' ratios, re-based): base 13 CSS px, crit 22 CSS px (1.69x), magnitude bands rescaled to your damage range (base weapon 7-30, depthMul up to 1+3200/900 = 4.6x, plus increased/more pools): {<25: 0.85}, {25-74: 1.0}, {75-199: 1.15}, {200-599: 1.3}, {600-1999: 1.45}, {2000+: 1.6}. Row spacing 11 CSS px, column 18, max 3 per row, spawn jitter ±6 X / ±2 Y. Lifetime 0.25s hold + 1.0s drift + 0.2s + 0.25s fade. Shrink to 0.75x and darken at +0.1s — this is the highest-value single line of code in the whole feature. COALESCE per (victim, damageSource) with a 0.25s hold — mandatory for you, because auras and DoTs will otherwise emit a number per tick per enemy. Crit = '!' suffix + 1.69x size + dedicated sound; let colour tween in afterwards as confirmation. Add a global cap of 24 live numbers, dropping oldest non-crit first. And ship a Show Damage Numbers toggle — Hades does.

*Source: Hades CombatPresentation.lua function DisplayDamageText (lines 1126-1350), HeroData.lua GameData.DamageNumberTextScales (line 8926), UIData.lua CombatUI (line 2491). High confidence — shipped game data.*

#### Damage numbers — when to hide them entirely

**Numbers.** Hades' guards: `if not ConfigOptionCache.ShowDamageNumbers then return`; `if victim.SkipDamageText then return`; `if victim.BlockSelfDamageNumbers and sourceId == victim.ObjectId then return`. Also environmental damage (wall slams) gets its own colour path and its own animation (CriticalHitWallSlam) rather than being folded into normal numbers.

Hades explicitly suppresses (a) numbers per player config, (b) numbers on specific victim types, (c) self-damage numbers. The player character in Hades gets no floating damage number when hit — damage to the player is communicated by a different presentation entirely. That is the important pattern: numbers are an ANALYTICAL channel for output you are optimising, and a bad channel for input you need to react to. When the player takes a hit they need to know 'I got hit, from that direction, badly' in under 100ms, and a three-digit number is a slow read.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Never show a floating number for damage TAKEN. Use a directional screen-edge vignette (a red gradient on the side the hit came from, alpha proportional to dmg/maxHP, 0.35s decay), an HP bar flash, and the long asymmetric hitstop. Suppress numbers for: DoT/burn/aura ticks (coalesce to one number per source per 0.25s, which the Hades rule already gives you), victims off-screen or beyond ~1.2 screen widths, and damage below 1% of the target's max HP. Given you have 12 affixes, 12 shrine boons and 22 attunement picks that all plausibly add damage sources, coalescing by source key is what stops the endgame from becoming an unreadable number blizzard.

*Source: Hades CombatPresentation.lua DisplayDamageText guards. High confidence.*

#### Camera — Celeste's smoothing constant validates your lerp factor 5

**Numbers.** Celeste's camera update (decompiled 1.4.0.0, widely reproduced): `Camera.Position = from + (target - from) * (1f - Math.Pow(0.01f / multiplier, Engine.DeltaTime))`, multiplier normally 1. Solving: 1 - 0.01^dt is exactly 1 - e^(-4.605*dt), so the continuous rate constant is ln(100) = 4.605 per second; time constant tau = 217ms; 99% of the distance is closed in 1.0s. At 60fps that is 7.4% of remaining distance per frame. Celeste's game resolution is 320x180 (Celeste.GameWidth / GameHeight). Camera target = player position minus half the screen, plus a per-room designer-authored level.CameraOffset — there is NO velocity lookahead in normal play. Lookahead exists only in special states: StStarFly adds 0.2 * Speed on both axes; StRedDash adds 48 * sign(Speed) on both axes (48/320 = 15% of screen width); StSummitLaunch pulls up 64px; StReflectionFall pushes down 32px. Target is then clamped to level bounds on both axes.

Your lerp factor of 5 is within 8% of Celeste's 4.605 — that is not a coincidence worth changing. The important thing is HOW it is applied. Celeste's form, `1 - base^dt`, is exactly frame-rate independent: at any dt it converges identically. The naive form `cam += (target-cam) * k * dt` is not — and it becomes unstable when k*dt approaches 1. With k=5 that is dt=0.2s. On mobile, a GC pause or a tab-switch producing a 250ms frame gives k*dt = 1.25, which overshoots past the target and can oscillate. Also note Celeste's clamp-to-bounds happens on the TARGET before smoothing, not on the smoothed position — so the camera eases into the level edge rather than sticking.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Keep k=5 but change the integrator to `cam += (target - cam) * (1 - Math.exp(-5 * dt))` and clamp dt to 1/30 before using it. Then three structural fixes from Celeste: (1) clamp the TARGET to world bounds before smoothing, not the result; (2) do not advance the camera at all while timeScale < 1 (hitstop should freeze the camera, otherwise the camera drifts during a freeze and the freeze reads as slow-mo); (3) apply shake AFTER the bounds clamp so shake is not eaten at level edges. Consider a per-axis split: k=5 horizontally, k=12 vertically when grounded plus a hard snap on landing (Itay Keren's 'platform snapping'), because a slow vertical camera during rapid platforming is the classic seasickness source.

*Source: github.com/NoelFB/Celeste Source/Player/Player.cs CameraTarget (lines 1353-1398, official). The Level.cs smoothing line is from the decompiled 1.4.0.0 build (TheCyndaquilDecompilers/Celeste_Decompiled) and community reproductions — medium-high confidence.*

#### Camera — framing, lookahead and how much you should see ahead

**Numbers.** Itay Keren, 'Scroll Back' (Gamasutra 2015, from his GDC talk): Super Mario Bros. places its camera acceleration zone 'around 25% off-center'; Defender (1981) uses a focus point 'around 25% screen-width forward' for bidirectional aiming; Bonanza Bros. switches the camera at 'double the player's' speed; Sonic uses a narrow horizontal window with an increased vertical window to suppress jump-induced camera motion. Celeste's largest lookahead anywhere is 48px on a 320px-wide screen = 15%. Position-smoothing guidance from camera literature: 0.2-0.4s settle time reads as responsive, 0.4-0.6s reads as cinematic (Celeste's tau is 217ms — the responsive end).

The 25% figure recurs across three unrelated games separated by decades and is the most reliable number in this area: a camera focus offset of about a quarter of the screen width in the direction of travel. Keren's article is explicit that hard numeric standards for camera windows do not exist in the literature ('a subject with more than 30 years of history in games, was hardly discussed'), so treat 25% as a ceiling and 15% (Celeste) as a conservative floor. The second, less-discussed point: vertical and horizontal want different treatment. Sonic widens the vertical window specifically so jumping does not move the camera — the camera should ignore ballistic vertical motion and respond to intentional vertical motion.

**Apply to Shardfall.** 17 tiles = 272 world px wide, roughly 153 tall at 16:9, giving ~9.5 tiles of height. Your player is 12x20, so 4.4% of view width and 13% of view height. Compare Celeste: an 8x11 player on 320x180 is 2.5% x 6%. You are roughly twice as zoomed in as Celeste while having flight, ranged enemies, and destructible terrain — three things that all demand more forward visibility. Reaction-time arithmetic: at 250ms human reaction plus ~100ms to act, a projectile at 200px/s needs ~70px of visible warning; a centred camera gives you 136px ahead, which is 0.68s of warning against that projectile and less once you are moving toward it. Two proposals: (1) widen the base view to 22-24 tiles (352-384px), or add a dynamic pull-out from 17 to 22 tiles driven by max(|vx|,|vy|)/vmax with a 0.6s lerp, which keeps the tight framing for melee and opens up for flight; (2) add velocity lookahead: targetX += clamp(vx * 0.28, -56, +56) which is ±20% of a 272px view (between Celeste's 15% and the classic 25%), and targetY += clamp(vy * 0.16, -32, +56), asymmetric so you see further DOWN when falling into unexplored depth — appropriate for a game with 3200 tiles of vertical descent. Suppress vertical lookahead entirely while ballistic and grounded-recently, Sonic-style. Add a small deadzone (24x20 px) so knockback and recoil jitter do not drag the camera.

*Source: gamedeveloper.com 'Scroll Back: The Theory and Practice of Cameras in Side-Scrollers', Itay Keren, 2015 — high confidence on the named-game figures, and the article itself flags the absence of general standards.*

#### Audio — how many distinct cues a game of this size needs

**Numbers.** Hades ships 501 distinct '/SFX/...' cue names referenced across its Lua scripts (438 distinct sound paths total across all prefixes). Hades has ~207 EnemyData entries, 6 weapons with ~4 aspects each, and hundreds of boons. That is roughly 2.4 SFX cues per enemy entry.

501 is a AAA-indie sound budget with a dedicated audio director and a full FMOD project. It is the wrong target for a single-HTML-file synthesized game, but it gives you the ratio. Applying ~2.4 cues per content entity to your content (19 enemies, 12 gear bases, 62 gems) would be ~230 cues, which is also wrong for procedural synthesis. The right model for synthesized audio is not 'cues' but PARAMETERISED PRIMITIVES: a small number of synthesis recipes with continuous parameters, where an enemy type selects a parameter set rather than a sample. The audit question is not 'how many sounds' but 'how many perceptually distinct EVENT CLASSES does the player need to distinguish without looking'.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Proposed target (mine): 8 synthesis primitives x parameterisation = ~34 perceptually distinct event classes. The 8 primitives: (1) transient click, (2) pitched body with pitch envelope, (3) filtered noise sweep, (4) resonant ring, (5) sub thump, (6) granular/crackle, (7) tonal chime, (8) sweep/whoosh. The ~34 classes you actually need: 4 material-class hit sounds (flesh/chitin/stone/metal) x {hit, death} = 8; 6 weapon swing/fire types; player {jump, land, dash, flight-loop, hurt, death, heal, level-up} = 8; 6 gem-school cast sounds (fire/cold/lightning/physical/chaos/support-proc); world {tile-break, tile-collapse, shrine, chest, portal, pickup} = 6. That is 34. Anything beyond that should be a PARAMETER (pitch, filter cutoff, decay) on an existing class, not a new class — which is also what keeps the file size at zero for audio.

*Source: Direct count from Hades Scripts/*.lua. High confidence on the count; the per-entity ratio and the primitives model are my analysis.*

#### Audio — pitch variation, retrigger and the specific comb-filter failure

**Numbers.** Standard practice: randomise pitch within 0-2 semitones (0-200 cents) depending on content; footstep practice cited as ±10% pitch and ±3dB gain across 5-10 variants. Two identical signals summed with a 0-15ms offset produce comb filtering with notches at f = 1/(2*delay); at 60fps a 16.7ms frame is exactly in this band. Two identical simultaneous signals sum to +6dB.

The specific failure you will hit with a synthesized 6-voice budget: when 4 projectiles from one gem hit in the same frame, you either play 4 identical hit sounds (+12dB and a metallic comb-filtered ring, which is the 'machine gun' ugliness) or you drop 3 of them and the multi-hit sounds weaker than a single hit — both wrong. The fix is a per-cue-class retrigger lockout plus gain compensation: if a cue class fired within the lockout window, suppress the new voice but boost the currently-playing one. Pitch randomisation alone does not fix it — two hits 3ms apart still comb-filter even at different pitches.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Raise the voice budget from 6 to 16 with PER-CLASS caps rather than a single global pool: hits 4, projectiles 3, player 2, world 3, UI 2, ambient 2. Add a 35ms per-class retrigger lockout; when a voice is suppressed, add +1.5dB (multiply gain by ~1.19) to the currently-playing instance of that class, capped at +4dB. Pitch variation: multiply all oscillator frequencies by 2^(r/12) with r uniform in [-1.5, +1.5] semitones for grunt hits, [-0.8, +0.8] for player actions (player sounds should be more consistent — they are the ones you learn), and [-2.5, +2.5] for ambient/world. For combo hits use a LADDER instead of random: r = min(combo, 8) * 0.4 semitones, so successive hits in a chain rise up to +3.2 semitones and then reset — this gives you free combo feedback with zero UI. Put a DynamicsCompressorNode on the SFX bus (threshold -10dB, knee 6, ratio 12, attack 0.003, release 0.10) so a swarm cannot clip.

*Source: Pitch-variation ranges from game audio practice literature (asoundeffect.com, gamedeveloper.com 'The Power of Pitch Shifting') — medium confidence, these are craft conventions not measured values. The comb-filter and +6dB summation numbers are acoustics, high confidence.*

#### Audio — the specific recipe for a punchy synthesized hit in WebAudio

**Numbers.** Proposed 3-layer recipe (mine, ~150ms total): LAYER 1 transient — 12ms of white noise from a 1-shot AudioBuffer, through a BiquadFilter bandpass at 3500Hz Q=1.2, gain 0 -> 1.0 over 0.5ms linear, then exponentialRamp to 0.001 over 12ms. LAYER 2 body — OscillatorNode (sine for flesh, triangle for chitin, square for metal), frequency.setValueAtTime(420) then exponentialRampToValueAtTime(90) over 45ms; gain 0 -> 0.8 over 2ms, exponentialRamp to 0.001 over 90ms. LAYER 3 tail — white noise through a lowpass sweeping 6000Hz -> 700Hz over 120ms, gain 0.25 decaying over 140ms. Metal adds two detuned bandpass-resonant sines at 1900Hz and 2850Hz, Q=18, decay 250ms. Crit variant: transient gain x1.4 (+3dB), body start frequency 620Hz instead of 420Hz, add a 3rd-harmonic sine at 3x the body frequency at 0.25 gain, and a 40ms feedback delay tail at 0.2 gain.

The punch comes almost entirely from the LAYER 2 PITCH ENVELOPE — a fast downward frequency sweep is the defining characteristic of a percussive impact, which is why the same technique builds kick drums. The transient (layer 1) is what makes the sound audible at low volume and on phone speakers, which have almost no output below ~300Hz; without it, your hit sound disappears on mobile. The tail carries the material identity. Attack times matter: gain must reach peak in under ~3ms or the sound reads as soft. Use exponentialRampToValueAtTime, never linearRamp, for decays — linear decays sound synthetic. WebAudio note: exponentialRampToValueAtTime cannot target 0, hence 0.001.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Ship this as a single `hit(materialIndex, weight, isCrit, pan)` function where weight in [0,1] maps to: body start frequency lerp(300, 620), body decay lerp(60ms, 140ms), transient bandpass lerp(4200, 2600)Hz. That gives you a continuous light-to-heavy hit spectrum from one recipe instead of discrete cues. Pan by (worldX - camX) / (viewWidth/2) clamped to ±0.8 using a StereoPannerNode — spatialised hits are the cheapest possible way to disambiguate a swarm, and directly addresses the same problem as the enemy-local flash. Crucially: schedule everything against audioCtx.currentTime, never setTimeout, and fire the sound on the SAME frame as the visual — see the sound-coherence finding.

*Source: Synthesis technique is standard percussion synthesis (pitch envelope + noise transient + amplitude envelope; see ModeAudio / Signal Flux percussion-synthesis references). The specific frequency, time and gain values are MY PROPOSAL and should be tuned by ear — they are a starting point, not measured from any game.*

#### Audio/visual coherence — the third of the three make-or-break features

**Numbers.** Study of 44 Steam action games ranked by NLP sentiment on ~5000 annotated impact-related comments; top 8 vs bottom 8 compared across a 19-feature juicy-design framework. Result: the three features whose absence reliably destroys 'impact feel' are HIT STOP, SOUND COHERENCE, and CAMERA CONTROL. Games lacking hit stop (Sacred Citadel, Draw Slasher, ranked 39th and 40th) scored under 70% positive impact-feel attitude. Audio/video sync detectability thresholds (ITU-R BT.1359-1): audio leading video becomes detectable around +45ms, audio lagging becomes detectable around -125ms.

The paper's method is coarse (Chinese-language Steam comments, binary feature presence/absence, 16 games in the comparison) but the finding is directionally strong and matches the Hades data: hitstop is not one juice feature among many, it is load-bearing. 'Sound coherence' in their framework means both timbral appropriateness AND temporal alignment — a delay between the visual impact and the audio 'ruins the user experience'. For a browser game this is a real engineering risk, not a theoretical one: audio scheduled via setTimeout or via a separate rAF tick can drift 16-50ms from the visual frame.

**Apply to Shardfall.** Three checks. (1) Fire the hit sound in the same function call as the hit resolution, scheduled at audioCtx.currentTime, and never behind a setTimeout or a queue that drains next frame. (2) If you buffer hit events to batch them, the buffer must be sub-frame. (3) The ~45ms lead threshold means you have almost no slack on the early side — audio must never precede the visual, so if anything, schedule at currentTime + 0.005. Also note the paper's finding gives you your priority order for polish work: hitstop first, audio coherence second, camera third, and everything else (particles, permanence, damage numbers) after.

*Source: arXiv:2208.06155v3, Lin, Duan, Wen, Cai, 'What Features Influence Impact Feel? A Study of Impact Feedback in Action Games'. Medium-high confidence on the ranking result, and the paper is explicit about its sample-size limitations. ITU-R BT.1359-1 thresholds: medium confidence, quoted from memory of the standard.*

#### Feedback layering — the canonical stack and a concrete channel budget

**Numbers.** Hades' DamagePresentation for one ordinary hit fires exactly 7 channels: (1) DoUnitShake — victim sprite offset 6px, Speed 550, Duration 0.15s; (2) DoUnitHitFlash — red Flash, Duration 0.03s, one cycle; (3) DisplayDamageText; (4) on-hit VFX; (5) weapon hit sound; (6) DoWeaponHitSlow — sim to ~10% for ~30ms after a 20-60ms pre-delay; (7) knockback. Exactly ONE of those seven (the sim slow) is global; six are local to the victim or the hit point. Camera shake is not among them. Vlambeer's ordering puts impact effect (step 10), hit animation (11), knockback (12) and permanence (13) all BEFORE screenshake (16) and sleep/hitstop (18).
